  - **Tests:** the rollback-only `purpose-of-use-vocabulary.integration.sql` inserts no code; as `hid_identity_api_runtime` it shows a direct-care grant honoured and a direct-care access request accepted, and both refused once the code is deactivated in the transaction. `schema.integration.sql` and `emergency.integration.sql` now insert their codes with `ON CONFLICT (code) DO NOTHING`, as every other suite and verifier already did. The rehearsal's 0028 upgrade check expects exactly the seeded row.
- **Document upload completion bound to the uploader** (D-Eminence/hid-system#59; EHR only, no migration or grant change; `documents/document.service.ts`). `POST /ehr/documents/:id/complete` checked the facility, `ehr.document.write`, a `write_records` grant and the stored object's size, SHA-256 and media type, but never compared the caller with the intent's `created_by`; neither do the `documents_update` policy (0005) nor `ehr.capture_record_version` on an update (0004). Any member of the facility with that permission and grant could complete a colleague's upload, and after the uploader had completed, a colleague's call got the idempotent `200` until the scan was clean or rejected. The content could not change (the object must match the uploader's declaration, and only the uploader gets the signed PUT); only the account recorded as completing the upload, in the record version and the audit event, could. Health-id completes only from the uploading session and expects the server to refuse anyone else (Health-id `docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`, "Staff session end").
  - Completion now reads `created_by` and refuses any other account that passes the route checks (active at the `X-Facility-ID` facility, with `ehr.document.write`) with `404 NOT_FOUND` ("Document not found", the answer for an unknown document), before the Identity check and the storage read, writing nothing. The completing `UPDATE` also requires `created_by = $6`, the caller (defence in depth; `created_by` is immutable), and the idempotent replay branch checks the creator it reads before answering `200`. The membership is not compared: an account with two memberships at one facility may be bound to either. A caller that fails the route checks still gets `400 FACILITY_REQUIRED`, `403 FACILITY_ACCESS_DENIED` or `403 PERMISSION_DENIED` first.
  - Who may complete an upload is not documented as a product rule; uploader only is the fail-closed current default (`INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md` §16, "Document upload completion"). There is no delegated completion; the remedy is a new upload.
  - Unchanged: the uploader's answers (`200` with the view, and for a repeat until the scan is clean or rejected; `403` without a grant; `404` at another facility; `409` on a mismatch or a state that cannot be completed, including a repeat once the scan is clean or rejected), the intent, list, download and OCR source routes, row-level security and grants. A refusal is recorded only by the request audit (`api.ehr.document.upload-complete.request`, outcome `failure`), which carries no document id.
- **Purpose of use and OCR workload refusals** (D-Eminence/hid-system#60; no migration or grant change; `common/request-context.ts` in `services/identity-api`, `services/ehr-api`, `services/ocr-api`, `services/lab-api` and `services/pharmacy-api`, and `services/ocr-api/src/integrations`).
  - **Purpose of use:** `requireRequestContext` threw a plain `Error` when the purpose it read from `X-Purpose-Of-Use` was missing or not exactly `direct-care`, `emergency` or `healthcare-operations`; no guard checks that header. The problem filter answered `500 INTERNAL_ERROR` and logged a server failure. This reached OCR's five reads (a job, find by document, extractions, validations, publications); every Lab and Pharmacy route that takes its purpose from the header (all but Lab's external import, whose body purpose is validated first); EHR's four document routes (upload intent, list, complete, download) and its two internal OCR routes (source read, clinical-note import); and Identity's registration-case read. Identity's consent and audit routes and EHR's clinical, consent and audit routes check the header against their own list first and already answered `400`. Reproduced on `main` by the new unit specs: no header and `treatment` each gave the plain `Error`, which each service's problem filter renders as `500`. They now answer `400 PURPOSE_OF_USE_REQUIRED` ("X-Purpose-Of-Use must be direct-care, emergency or healthcare-operations") before any read. The allow-list is unchanged. A request the guard did not establish stays an internal error (`500`), and so, in Lab and Pharmacy, does one with neither the bearer token nor the cookie their guards require; before, those two checks shared one error with the purpose.
  - **OCR workload refusals:** EHR, Lab and Pharmacy refuse OCR's own workload identity with `401 INTERNAL_SERVICE_AUTH_REQUIRED` (no or wrong internal-caller header or local secret, no workload bearer token), `401 INVALID_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` (any JWT verification failure, a failure to fetch the JWKS included) or `403 WORKLOAD_CALLER_DENIED` (another subject). OCR's integrations passed the status and code through. The browser got a `401`, for which Health-id refreshes the staff session and resends (Health-id `packages/http/src/client.ts`), for a fault between services; and a publication stored the refusal as terminal, so every later request for it answered `409 OCR_PUBLICATION_TERMINAL` and the reviewed validation could never be published (one publication per validation, 0016; a validated job cannot be validated again). Reproduced on `main` by the new unit specs: a Lab `401 INVALID_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` was stored as `INVALID_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY` with `retryable: false`. OCR's EHR, Lab and Pharmacy clients (`owning-service-auth.ts`) now answer a `401` or `403` with one of those codes, or with `WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_UNAVAILABLE` (which those services send as `503` today), as `503 EHR_SERVICE_AUTH_UNAVAILABLE`, `LAB_SERVICE_AUTH_UNAVAILABLE` or `PHARMACY_SERVICE_AUTH_UNAVAILABLE`, the codes they already used when no user credential could be delegated. A publication stores it as `OWNING_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` (retryable) through the existing path, with the `<SERVICE>_SERVICE_AUTH_UNAVAILABLE` code in `failure_summary` and in the audit event's `ownerFailureCode`. The mapping applies to every OCR call to those services, including job creation and every read of a job's source document through EHR.
  - **Unchanged:** any other `401` or `403` from those services, that is a refusal of the user's credential, facility or permission (`AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED`, `FACILITY_ACCESS_DENIED`, `PERMISSION_DENIED`, `ACCESS_DENIED`), keeps its status and code and stays terminal in a publication. So does an Identity refusal during the attempt (an authorization that lapsed since the request, `404 OCR_JOB_NOT_FOUND`): whether a lapsed grant or a refused permission should end a publication or leave it retryable is open decision D16, and the default is today's terminal behaviour. The claim, the lock order (the job's key share before the publication row), the attempt budget (`max_attempts`, 3), the 15 s backoff, the replay by requester and key, and the failure codes are unchanged. No check is relaxed: a retry passes `createPublication`'s authorization with the then-current grant, and the owning service's own checks.
  - **Identity (follow-up):** Identity renders its workload refusals (`workload-auth.service.ts`: `UnauthorizedException`, `ForbiddenException`) as `401 AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` and `403 ACCESS_DENIED` (`common/problem.ts`), the codes of a user refusal, so OCR cannot tell them apart and passes them through. A JWKS outage at Identity therefore still ends a publication as terminal and still reaches the browser as a `401`. Fixing it needs distinct Identity workload codes with unchanged statuses, a check of every Identity client (EHR, Lab, Pharmacy, OCR, Outreach), and then the same mapping in OCR's Identity client (`503 IDENTITY_SERVICE_AUTH_UNAVAILABLE`).
  - **Health-id cannot retry a publication (frontend dependency):** the provider portal sends a new `Idempotency-Key` with every publish (`publishOcrValidation` calls `keyHeaders()` without a key, `apps/patient-web/src/staff/api.ts`); a new key for a validation that already has a publication answers `409 OCR_ALREADY_PUBLISHED`; the Publish button renders only while the validation has no publication (`routes/Operations.tsx`); and its HTTP client never retries a `POST` (`packages/http/src/retry.ts`). So no retryable publication failure, from the OCR publication retry fix (§8) or from this change, can be retried from Health-id. OCR finds an earlier publication by facility, requesting account and key, so a retry must come from the same account in the same facility with the same key and an identical body (another body answers `409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`); the same key from another account, another member of the same facility included, answers `409 OCR_ALREADY_PUBLISHED`. Health-id needs to keep the key per validation, account and facility, resend the identical body, and offer a retry only when it holds a key for the signed-in account in the active facility (the publication projection does not name the requester), for a `failed` publication whose `failureCode` is retryable and whose `attemptCount` is below `maxAttempts`. The in-repo EHR review workspace retries with the original key while it stays open (§9).
- **EHR clinical safety and launch module set (D-Eminence/hid-system#64, migration 0084; EHR and Identity APIs; no grant change)**. Register items MOD-1 (the permission part), CLIN-EIE, PREC-NOTES, P10 and D8 (`DATABASE.md`, "EHR clinical safety and launch module set (0084)"; `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md` §11, "Launch module set", "Encounter status and its records" and "Record summaries").
  - **Launch module set (decision D17):** Lab, Pharmacy, OCR and Outreach are off at launch, and EHR prescriptions and lab requests are records only. Creating an active lab request committed it and then called Lab; with Lab off it answered `502` for an order no lab received. 0084 deletes `ehr.lab-request.write` (doctor, clinician, lab), `lab.work-item.accept` (doctor, clinician, nurse, lab), `lab.accession.create` (lab), `pharmacy.work-item.accept` and `pharmacy.dispensing.create` (pharmacist) from every role. Lab-request create and update, the EHR's `lab-work-item` and `pharmacy-work-item` commands, and Lab's and Pharmacy's acceptance, accession and dispensing answer `403 PERMISSION_DENIED` before any write or call to another service; Health-id hides those actions without the permissions (§3). Reads stay. An existing lab request then changes status only through its encounter's cancellation or retraction cascade, so the open lab requests of encounters already cancelled or entered in error are retracted before 0084 (P12). The role assertions fail if any role holds one of the five. The Lab, Pharmacy, OCR and EHR-to-Lab runtime verifiers give their actors these permissions through their fake Identity and never relied on the seeded roles, so they still exercise the module logic. The platform controls `outreach_portal_enabled`, `uploads_enabled` and `break_glass_enabled` (seeded on by 0039) are not changed by the migration: a change through the audited structure writes `platform.control_events`, whose acting account is required, and a migration has none. An operator sets all three false with the audited `POST /admin/controls` before ingress (P11). DesiredCounts and edge routes for the disabled services are separate operator and infrastructure work (since D-Eminence/hid-system#66 the regional stack pins those counts at 0, ADR-053).
  - **Records follow their encounter (CLIN-EIE):** every child create checked only that the encounter existed. Now a note (OCR imports included), vital, diagnosis, encounter-linked document, prescription or lab request on a cancelled or entered-in-error encounter, a new order or the activation of a draft order on a completed one, and resuming an order on a cancelled or entered-in-error one answer `409 ENCOUNTER_NOT_OPEN` with nothing written and nothing sent to Lab. Late notes, vitals, diagnoses and documents on a completed encounter, and resuming a held order there, stay allowed (current default; refusing late documentation would lose clinical information). The services check under a share lock on the encounter, and 0084's `SECURITY INVOKER` trigger refuses the same writes in the database.
  - **Retraction:** a completed encounter had no transition, so a wrong-patient encounter that was completed could never be retracted; and marking an encounter entered in error changed the encounter only. Now `completed` may move to `entered_in_error`, and marking an encounter entered in error marks, in the same transaction and under the encounter's row lock, its draft, signed and amended notes, its diagnoses' verification status, and its draft, active and on-hold prescriptions and lab requests `entered_in_error`; cancelling an encounter cancels its open orders. Each record gets a version with the request's change reason, and the encounter's audit event records the changed ids (`cascade`). Completed orders and vitals keep their state. A diagnosis entered in error answers any change `409 DIAGNOSIS_NOT_AMENDABLE` (before, it could be set back to confirmed). These semantics are the owner defaults; the clinical owner should confirm them.
  - **Lock order:** a child create, and an order activation, take the encounter's share lock before any lock on a record of it, the order in which the retraction locks; the trigger's own share lock is then already held. A path that locked the order first could deadlock with a retraction: with a temporary service mutant that locked the order row first, the lifecycle verifier's race produced `40P01` (§8); the committed verifier checks that the services' order produces none.
  - **Note release (PREC-NOTES, D7):** the patient summary and the 0030 patient-self policies released a signed note, with its content, whatever its encounter's status, so a signed note of a wrong-patient encounter stayed visible to that patient after the encounter was cancelled or retracted. The summary and both policies (recreated by 0084) now require the note's encounter to be completed. The emergency summary leaves out notes of encounters entered in error; it keeps notes of open encounters, which matter in an emergency, and of cancelled ones (D9).
  - **Break-glass (P10):** `identity.activate_break_glass` refuses with `22023` when no active `emergency` purpose of use exists, and Identity answered that `400 INVALID_CONSENT_COMMAND`. It now answers `423 BREAK_GLASS_DISABLED` ("Emergency access is not enabled on this platform"), as for the disabled control; other `22023` refusals stay `400`. Break-glass is off at launch: `break_glass_enabled` false and no `emergency` code (P10, P11).
  - **Recorded vitals (D8):** no API route changes a vital's recorded values, but the EHR runtime could. 0084 refuses any update of a vital except of its current correction number. The emergency summary still returns vitals as first recorded.
  - **Clinical note creates:** `POST .../clinical-notes` failed for every note on `main` with `500` (`42P08`, "inconsistent types deduced for parameter $4": the creator was also the signer inside a `CASE`), so no clinician could write a note, Health-id's draft-only note form included. Found by the new lifecycle verifier; the parameter is now cast.

- **EHR retraction integrity (D-Eminence/hid-system#74, migration 0090; EHR API; one runtime grant)**. Register items ENC-RETRACT-1, ENC-RETRACT-2, RX-FINAL, ORDER-CLOSED-EDIT, A9-EHR, A17-EHR-AUDIT and ALLERGY-1; ADR-056 (`DATABASE.md`, "EHR retraction integrity (0090)" and §7.1; `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md` §11, "Encounter status and its records", and §7).
  - **Retraction needs the records' write permissions (ENC-RETRACT-1, launch blocker):** cancelling an encounter or marking it entered in error changed its notes, diagnoses and orders for anyone holding `ehr.encounter.write`, because row-level security checks the patient's consent, not the role. A nurse could retract a doctor's confirmed diagnosis and active prescription, and a receptionist notes they cannot read; nothing restores them. Now the EHR refuses, before writing anything, a close that changes a kind of record the actor may not write on its own route (`403 ENCOUNTER_RETRACTION_NOT_PERMITTED` with a fixed detail; the missing permission codes go to the audit row only, because naming them would tell the caller which kinds of record the encounter holds), and `ehr.require_retraction_permission()` refuses it in the database too (`42501`). Only records the close would change count; records already entered in error and cancelled orders block nothing. Seeded roles, marking entered in error: doctors and clinicians unless the encounter holds an open or completed lab request, nurses unless it holds a diagnosis or an open or completed prescription or lab request, receptionists only when it holds none of the records a retraction changes. Cancelling changes only open orders: anyone holding `ehr.encounter.write` may cancel whatever notes, vitals, diagnoses and documents the encounter holds, unless it holds an open prescription (doctors and clinicians only) or an open lab request. Holding, resuming and completing need no record permission. No role holds `ehr.lab-request.write` since 0084, so an encounter that still holds an open (or, to retract, completed) lab request cannot be cancelled or retracted until Lab ordering is enabled again (none can be created at launch). The `PATCH` response gains the additive `cascade` field: the new status and the ids changed per category.
  - **Wrong-patient vitals, completed orders and documents (ENC-RETRACT-2):** a vital had no status, and a completed order no retraction, so a wrong-patient encounter left them as valid chart data. Vitals gain `status` (`final` or `entered_in_error`); `POST .../vitals/:vitalId/entered-in-error` (`ehr.vital.write`, `expectedRowVersion` and `changeReason`, versioned and audited as `ehr.vital.entered-in-error`) retracts one, which then takes no correction (`409 VITAL_NOT_AMENDABLE`); the emergency summary leaves retracted vitals out. A completed prescription or lab request may move to `entered_in_error`, and the encounter's retraction now also covers its final vitals, completed orders and linked documents. Nothing is deleted. The database keeps both retractions final: `ehr.require_final_vital()` refuses a correction row of a retracted vital (reading the vital `FOR SHARE`, so a correction cannot commit beside a retraction it did not see), and `ehr.keep_retracted_document()` refuses any update of a retracted document, which would otherwise be served as available again once set back to `uploaded`.
  - **Closed orders and encounters are final (RX-FINAL, ORDER-CLOSED-EDIT):** a completed, cancelled or entered-in-error prescription accepted a new frequency, instructions or end date, and the database let a cancelled one become active again; a cancelled or entered-in-error encounter accepted header edits. Now `PATCH` answers `409 PRESCRIPTION_NOT_AMENDABLE` (`LAB_REQUEST_NOT_AMENDABLE`) and `409 ENCOUNTER_NOT_OPEN`, and 0090's triggers refuse the same writes and any status change outside the order transitions. Status transitions refused before keep their `INVALID_*_TRANSITION` codes.
  - **Staff session on the patient's records (A9-EHR):** `GET /ehr/me/records` answered a staff session `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` and audited nothing: the handler's `403` became a facility-less staff `http-request` row, which the audit layer refuses. The route is now `@PatientOnly()`: the guard refuses any non-patient session with `403 PATIENT_SESSION_REQUIRED` before the handler and records `auth.authorization.denied`. A staff actor's failure on any facility-optional route is now also recorded as an `auth.*` event instead of failing with `503`.
  - **Refusal audit (A17-EHR-AUDIT):** a clinical command refused inside its transaction lost its semantic audit row with the rollback (consent lapsed under row-level security, `409 ENCOUNTER_NOT_OPEN`, `412 VERSION_CONFLICT`, the new refusals). `ClinicalRepository` now records it after the rollback as the command's action with outcome `denied` (401, 403, 409, 412, 423, 429) or `failure` (400, 404) and details `{status, code}` plus a refusal's own codes (for a retraction, `missingPermissions`), never clinical content. The guard's refusal rows and the interceptor's failure rows gain `status` and `code`, and the interceptor records a refusal as `denied` instead of `failure`. An audit write that fails still answers `503`.
  - **Allergies (ALLERGY-1, decision):** the EHR records no allergies, while `DATABASE.md` §7 described an `allergy_intolerance` table as current. §7 now lists the implemented EHR tables and marks the target model as not implemented; `scripts/tests/database-doc-relations.test.mjs` keeps it so. Allergies are not recorded at launch (`PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md`, "Allergies at launch").

- **Non-superuser database chain for RDS (D-Eminence/hid-system#63, migration 0083; the role bootstrap, `db:verify-logins` and every service that connects to the database)**. On Amazon RDS no role is a PostgreSQL superuser. As the migration administrator there, a `NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS CREATEROLE` LOGIN that owns the schema, four things failed (each reproduced locally on PostgreSQL 16.15):
  - **0015 (I3):** a fresh chain stopped at 0015, which gives `ocr.claim_worker_job` the clause `SET plpgsql.variable_conflict = 'use_column'`. The parameter has superuser context, so a non-superuser is refused (`permission denied to set parameter`) unless it holds `SET ON PARAMETER plpgsql.variable_conflict`, and once granted every OCR claim kept needing it, because the definer applies the setting as its owner on each call. 0083 replaces the function (`CREATE OR REPLACE`; same signature, result, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path` and body) with `#variable_conflict use_column` as the first line of its body. Its stored setting is gone, so the privilege is needed only while 0015 applies: the RDS master grants it for the migration run and revokes it after 0083 (`MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6.1). No other function sets a parameter that is not user-settable. 0015 is unchanged.
  - **The role bootstrap (P8):** `runtime-grants.sql` stopped at its first `ALTER ROLE … NOSUPERUSER … NOBYPASSRLS`, which PostgreSQL 16 allows only a superuser (and, for `BYPASSRLS`, a role that holds it) even when nothing changes, and rolled back, so no runtime role received a grant. It now checks the 25 group roles instead: a role with `SUPERUSER`, `BYPASSRLS` or `REPLICATION` is refused, as is a runner that is neither a superuser nor holds `ADMIN OPTION` on every role, and `LOGIN`, `CREATEDB`, `CREATEROLE` and `INHERIT` are corrected only when they differ. The three transfers to technical owners need `SET ROLE` on the owner and `CREATE` on its schema, and the later grants and revokes on those functions need the owner's privileges, so the runner holds `INHERIT` and `SET` membership in `hid_event_delivery_commands`, `hid_ocr_queue_metrics` and `hid_platform_control_reader`, and each holds `CREATE` on `integration`, `ocr` or `platform` around its transfer, only inside the bootstrap's transaction; the file revokes both and refuses the run if either is left. A run that transfers a function leaves its technical owner an `EXECUTE` entry on it, which the next run removes; after that a run changes nothing. PostgreSQL 16 records an `ADMIN OPTION`-only membership of a non-superuser creator in each role it creates, granted by the bootstrap superuser, which the creator cannot revoke; `runtime-roles.integration.sql` allows that row on the technical owners only when its member is the administrator running the check, and refuses any other membership in them, an `ADMIN OPTION`-only grant to a runtime role included (with it, that role's login could grant itself the owner and `SET ROLE` to it). A superuser bootstrap grants exactly what it did before, plus the new role. The platform control block comes before every other table lock of the file and recreates its policy only when it is missing or differs: the run that creates it locks `platform.control_settings` (`ACCESS EXCLUSIVE`) before any outbox table, in the same order as a break-glass activation (control read, then `identity.outbox_events`), so the two cannot deadlock, and a later run takes no lock on that table (§5 step 31).
  - **Platform controls (P8):** `platform.control_enabled(text)`, which the Identity, EHR and Outreach guards call on every request that is not a platform administrator's, read `platform.control_settings` (`FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`; its only read policy needs `platform.control.read`) as its owner. Under an owner without `BYPASSRLS` it saw no row and raised `PLATFORM_CONTROL_UNAVAILABLE`, which the guards answer with `503`, for every patient and provider request. The bootstrap now gives it a technical owner, the `NOLOGIN NOINHERIT NOBYPASSRLS` role `hid_platform_control_reader`, with `SELECT (control_key, enabled)` on the table and the policy `platform_controls_definer_read` (`FOR SELECT TO hid_platform_control_reader USING (true)`; the six flags are platform-wide and hold no PHI). It also has `USAGE` on `auth`, and nothing in it: PostgreSQL inlines the table's PUBLIC policy's SQL function `platform.current_account_id()` while planning, as the reading role, and that body names `auth`; the `true` policy then decides the row and the PUBLIC expression never runs. `EXECUTE` stays with PUBLIC (0040); `platform.require_control_enabled` keeps its owner.
  - **Runtime logins:** `db:verify-roles` checks only the NOLOGIN group roles, so a secret holding the migration URL passed S2. `npm run db:verify-logins` connects with each of the ten runtime credentials' own URL and refuses a login that is the RDS master or the migration administrator; has `SUPERUSER`, `BYPASSRLS`, `CREATEROLE`, `CREATEDB` or `REPLICATION`; is a direct member of anything but its aggregate (inherited, without `ADMIN OPTION`) or reaches a role its aggregate does not; has the privileges of the owner of the database or of any application schema, relation or function; is named by a direct grant; or may create objects. Two credentials may not share a login. In production each of the six APIs and three workers refuses to start (`Refusing to start: the database role …`) when its connection, or the EHR scanner pool's, is a superuser, bypasses row-level security or has an application owner's privileges. Nest stops before listening and the workers before their first claim. A production service whose database is unreachable at start-up now exits instead of starting unready; ECS restarts it.
  - **Tests:** `function-parameter-settings.integration.sql` and `platform-control-owner.integration.sql` (new), `runtime-roles.integration.sql`, and the start-up check spec in each of the nine services. `ocr-worker-lease.integration.sql` now expects, under a non-bypass owner, the claim of a provider whose only job that owner cannot see to return no row and change nothing; until 0083 the parameter privilege refused it. The rehearsal runs `scripts/verify-non-superuser-database.mjs` (`checks.non_superuser_database`; `TUF-STAGING-MIGRATION.md`).
  - **Still open:** the OCR, document-scanner and Outreach definer paths of P8 still need an owner that bypasses row-level security, or the technical owners of ADR-041; launch decision I3 keeps them out of the release unless they pass under a non-bypass owner. Whether the RDS master may grant `SET ON PARAMETER` is unverified (§10).

- **Production workload identity (D-Eminence/hid-system#54; ADR-047 accepted 2026-10-13 with D1-D3; not deployed; no migration or grant change)**. Production and development token-bearing tasks (Identity, EHR, Lab, Pharmacy, OCR API, Outreach) mount a task-local token volume read-only that nothing writes: only staging has the `workload-token-agent` writer, and a task-local volume cannot be written from outside the task. So in production every service-to-service call fails closed (`503 OTP_DELIVERY_UNAVAILABLE`, `503 SERVICE_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_UNAVAILABLE`). The issuer Lambda and the agent also accepted only `staging`.
  - **Infrastructure:** `staging-workload-identity.ts` becomes `workload-identity.ts`, a `WorkloadIdentity` bound to `staging` or `production`. Production gets its own retained P-256 key, issuer function, REST API with stage `production`, log group (365 days) and `hid:production:<service>` subjects, the six token agents, and receivers wired to them; it loses the eight external `WorkloadIssuerUrl`, `WorkloadJwksUrl` and `*WorkloadSubject` parameters (62 to 54) and gains a fourth KMS key (cost inventory). Synthesis fails for staging or production if a token task lacks exactly one rw `workload-token-agent` mount. Development is unchanged.
  - **Issuer and agent:** both accept only `staging` and `production` and bind that one value to the issuer path, every subject prefix, the API Gateway request stage (issuer) and the signed `/<stage>/token` path (agent). Error texts no longer name staging.
  - **Key custody (D1, DEP-4-KEY):** the signing key of staging and production keeps the account root statement (no lockout) and adds three Deny statements for `kms:Sign`: any principal whose `aws:PrincipalArn` is not the issuer Lambda role, any `kms:SigningAlgorithm` other than `ECDSA_SHA_256`, any `kms:MessageType` other than `RAW`. A warm issuer re-reads the public key at least every five minutes, so a disabled key stops being published and stops minting. Account administrators can still change the key policy or the function; ADR-047 accepts that risk.
  - **Receivers (DEP-4-KEY):** Identity (service and document-scanner paths), Notification, Lab, Pharmacy and the EHR's OCR caller guard accept only ES256, require `exp` and `iat`, refuse a token older than 300 s or issued in the future (5 s clock tolerance) and refuse a `token_use` other than `workload` (`401`, the existing codes). Before, they accepted RS256, a token without `exp`, and any `iat`.
  - **Edge (D3, DEP-4-DOS):** `POST /token` and the JWKS `GET` each have their own method throttle (30 rps, burst 60) and the stage has no `/*/*` setting. Where NAT gateways exist (staging economy and fidelity, production) a REGIONAL web ACL on the issuer stage blocks by default and allows only the VPC's NAT gateway Elastic IPs; sleep has none. The agent keeps the keys that verified its tokens by `kid` and fetches the JWKS only at start, for an unknown `kid` and after a cached key stops verifying, at most every 30 s once a token has verified, so a JWKS outage or flood does not stop renewal. Until its first token verifies it may refetch on each 10 s renewal retry (never faster), which leaves about six attempts before its health check (start period 30 s, interval 10 s, three retries) turns it `UNHEALTHY` at about 60 s.
  - **Issuer outage (D2, DEP-4-RES):** the agent is `essential: false` with an ECS container restart policy (60 s); the application keeps its `HEALTHY` start dependency. During an issuer outage a task keeps running and only its service-to-service calls fail closed (`503`); Identity password sign-in keeps working. ECS restarts the agent only if it exits after running at least 60 s. One that exits sooner is not restarted: its task runs on without a token writer until it is replaced, and the token files the agent last wrote stay after they expire, so callers other than Identity's Notification client send an expired token and are refused `401` (`AWS_DEPLOYMENT_RUNBOOK.md` §7, "Agent stopped").
  - **Identity's Notification client:** in JWT mode a missing, empty or malformed token file no longer sends the request without a credential, and a token file whose `exp` has passed (left by an agent that was not restarted) is no longer sent. It sends nothing, logs `notification_workload_token_unavailable` (reason `missing`, `invalid` or `expired` and a running count, no recipient, code or token) and counts it. An OTP answers as before (`definitive_failure`, so enrollment answers `503 OTP_DELIVERY_UNAVAILABLE`); an emergency-contact alert is `unknown` with `safeCode` `workload_token_unavailable` and is retried.
  - **Staging:** the sleep, economy and fidelity templates change in the issuer Lambda asset, the key policy, the method settings and the agent's `Essential`/`RestartPolicy`; economy and fidelity gain the issuer IP set, web ACL and association. The retained key keeps logical ID `StagingWorkloadIdentitySigningKey53DFD52F`.
  - **Not included:** alarms with routing (D4), a document-scanner token (D5, DEP-4-SCAN, DOC-3: the in-stack issuer mints only the six service subjects, so the malware-scan callback cannot authenticate), key custody beyond the key policy, and the public gateway's internal routes.

- **Client address, upstream resolution and route closure at the edge (D-Eminence/hid-system#65; Cloudflare Worker, gateway, `infra/aws` and Identity; no migration or grant change)**. Register IP-1, GW-DNS, the internal-route part of DEP-4-KEY and the gateway part of MOD-1.
  - **Client address (IP-1):** the Worker deleted every client-address header and added none, and the gateway set `X-Forwarded-For` to `$remote_addr`, the public ALB node. Identity trusts the VPC, so `request.ip` was an ALB node's address for every client: recovery start, enrollment start and contact, provider enrollment and organization intake each had about five starts per 15 minutes per ALB node for the whole platform, and audit source addresses and Turnstile `remoteip` named the load balancer. The Worker now deletes any caller-supplied `X-Real-IP` and sets it from `CF-Connecting-IP` only when that is one valid IPv4 or IPv6 address. The gateway's realip module takes the client address from that header only on connections from `VPC_CIDR` and sends it as `X-Forwarded-For`; it drops the raw header. With `HID_REQUIRE_CLIENT_IP` on (CDK: staging and production; image default off) a proxied API request without a usable client address answers `403 CLIENT_ADDRESS_REQUIRED`. The header is `X-Real-IP` because the Worker before this change deletes it: behind a Worker not yet redeployed, or rolled back, the gateway sees no address and refuses, where a new header (the first draft used `X-HID-Client-IP`) would pass through that Worker with a value the caller chose, letting the caller pick its own per-network bucket and audit address.
  - **Identity:** one helper (`common/client-network.ts`) gives the public IP-keyed limits their key: an IPv4 address, or the /64 of an IPv6 address, so rotating addresses inside one allocation shares one bucket. In production an address inside `TRUST_PROXY_CIDRS` (one of the platform's own proxies) is refused like a missing one. Recovery start answers the new `503 OTP_RATE_LIMIT_UNAVAILABLE` instead of charging the shared `unavailable` bucket; enrollment answers its existing `503 ENROLLMENT_RATE_LIMIT_UNAVAILABLE`, now before any database work in the contact step too; provider enrollment and the organization flows answer their existing `503`s. Enrollment start has its own limit, `PATIENT_ENROLLMENT_START_RATE_MAX_REQUESTS` (default 5, as before). Sign-in lockout is keyed per identifier, not per IP, and is unchanged; the platform MFA `mfa_failure_ip` bucket is unchanged and now keys on the real client through the gateway.
  - **Upstream resolution (GW-DNS):** every location used a literal `proxy_pass` to a Cloud Map name, which nginx resolves once at start-up: after an Identity or EHR redeploy the gateway kept the old task addresses, and with any routed module at zero tasks a new gateway task could not start. Each location now sets `$hid_upstream` and proxies to it, with `resolver ${GATEWAY_DNS_RESOLVER} valid=10s ipv6=off` (CDK: the VPC CIDR base plus two). The upstream values carry no URI part, so nginx sends the client's unparsed request URI, as the literal form did (`ngx_http_proxy_create_request`). With a variable `proxy_pass` nginx applies no default `proxy_redirect`; no backend sends a `Location` header.
  - **Route switches (MOD-1):** `HID_ENABLED_API_ROUTES` names the proxied modules; any other module's prefixes answer `404 API_ROUTE_NOT_FOUND` before proxying, and a misspelt name stays closed. CDK passes `identity,ehr`; a staging synth may opt modules in with the synth input `HID_ENABLED_API_ROUTES`; a production synth refuses any module beyond the launch set. Identity's caller subjects and EHR's Lab and Pharmacy token files stay: production configuration requires them (register MOD-2 verdict).
  - **Internal routes (DEP-4-KEY d):** `/api/v1/auth/service-session`, `/api/v1/identity/service/*`, `/api/v1/identity/outreach/*`, `/api/v1/ehr/internal/*`, `/api/v1/lab/work-items/accept-ehr-order`, `/api/v1/lab/imports/from-ocr`, `/api/v1/pharmacy/work-items/accept-ehr-prescription` and `/api/v1/pharmacy/imports/from-ocr` were proxied from the public edge. Each prefix location now holds a nested case-insensitive regular expression that answers `404 API_ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`, because Express routes without regard to case or a trailing slash; nginx matches its decoded, normalized URI, so dot segments, repeated slashes and percent-encodings are denied too. Every proxied location also refuses a request target with a backslash before the query or a `#` anywhere with `400 INVALID_REQUEST_TARGET`: Express's `parseurl` passes a target holding `#` to `url.parse`, which turns those backslashes into slashes, so `/api/v1/identity/service\authorization/check#x` missed the deny expression and reached the internal handler (found in review, reproduced with nginx 1.29.8 and Express 5, §8). A browser cannot send either character through the Worker; a backslash in the query is still proxied. Every caller uses the private service names; no Health-id or hid-system browser code calls these routes (Health-id lists them as non-frontend endpoints). `/api/v1/ehr/documents/scan-events` stays reachable until D5 is decided.
  - **Checks:** `scripts/verify-container-platform.mjs` now checks the gateway through `scripts/lib/gateway-contract.mjs`, which routes requests through a model of nginx (`scripts/lib/nginx-config-model.mjs`): ownership, the client URI passed unchanged, the client address, the refusal without it (including an `X-HID-Client-IP` a pre-change Worker would forward), the request-target refusal, the route switches and the internal-route denial. `infra/aws/scripts/verify-synth.mjs` checks the gateway environment of each synthesized profile. The rendered configuration was also run locally by an nginx 1.29.8 binary in front of an Express 5 backend (§8); the gateway image has not run.
  - **Deployment order matters (§6):** every Worker that proxies `/api/v1` (the web, EHR, Lab, Pharmacy, OCR, Outreach and admin hosts), then the gateway with the regional stack, then Identity. Behind any Worker that does not yet send `X-Real-IP` (not redeployed, or rolled back) a gateway that requires it refuses every API request from that host with `403`; it does not take a caller's address. An Identity build with this change behind a gateway without it answers `503` to every public IP-keyed request in production.
  - **Not in this change:** the accountless QoreID cost ceiling for public enrollment (register IP-1 part 4, a migration planned with the enrollment changes), keying the WAF `ApiOriginRateLimit` rule on the client address (it still sees Cloudflare egress addresses), and the EHR, permission and control parts of MOD-1. The gateway still turns a backend `502`, `503` or `504` into `503 UPSTREAM_UNAVAILABLE` (its `error_page` with `proxy_intercept_errors`), so a browser sees that code rather than the Identity `503` code. (Later: D-Eminence/hid-system#72 keys the WAF rule on the client network the Worker sends, `X-HID-Client-Network`, and passes backend answers through, ADR-055.)

- **Launch frontend hosting (D-Eminence/hid-system#67; ADR-052; Cloudflare configuration and offline tooling only; no migration, no Worker code change)**. Register FE-HOST, including MOD-EDGE.
  - **Before:** `hid-web` served hid-system's legacy `apps/web` on www and staging, and `hid-admin` the legacy `apps/admin` on admin.*. `apps/web` still calls about 30 retired `/api/v1/functions/*` routes, which the gateway answers with `404`, and `apps/admin`'s staff sign-in is refused by the platform-scoped admin endpoints. Health-id, the only frontend with the governed access loop and the platform console, had no deployment definition, and `verify-config.mjs` required a `manifest.webmanifest` and `service-worker.js` that Health-id deliberately lacks. The dry-run and the staging readiness plan covered all seven application Workers.
  - **Hosts:** `hid-web` and `hid-admin` have no default asset directory. Each named environment serves `staged/<environment>/site`, the Health-id `apps/patient-web` build staged for that host, with `provenance.json` beside it (not served). Staged artefacts are git-ignored.
  - **Staging step:** `infra/cloudflare/scripts/stage-health-id-build.mjs --worker <hid-web|hid-admin> --environment <staging|production> --build <dir> --source-commit <40-hex>` stages a build only if its inlined `VITE_HID_ENVIRONMENT` is the environment and `VITE_HID_API_BASE_URL` and `VITE_HID_APP_ORIGIN` are each exactly `https://<host>` (same-origin cookie sessions); it has a deployable Turnstile site key (`0x…`) and no error-reporting DSN; it has no Google sign-in (no `accounts.google.com`, `/gsi/client` or `google.accounts.id` and no Google OAuth client id: Health-id inlines `VITE_HID_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` as a bare value where it is used, so these markers are the check); it holds only `index.html` and content-hashed files under `assets/`, with no source map or `sourceMappingURL`, service worker, web app manifest, symbolic link or credential-shaped value; and its `index.html` is exactly the shell Vite writes, checked against an allow-list of elements and attributes (one module entry script, and scripts and links only to `.js` and `.css` files of its own `/assets/`; `base`, `style`, inline handlers, `meta http-equiv`, any other element or repeated attribute, and markup it cannot parse exactly are refused). It records the Health-id commit, every file's SHA-256 and size, a tree hash and the inlined public environment record (the five keys of Health-id's `src/publicEnvironment.ts`). On a refusal it writes nothing. A restage replaces only an earlier artefact of the same Worker and environment (exactly `site/` and a `provenance.json` of this schema); it stages into a missing or empty directory and refuses any other destination, such as a mistyped `--staged-directory`, leaving it unchanged.
  - **Launch subset:** `infra/cloudflare/launch-deployment.json` deploys `hid-web` and `hid-admin` in staging and production, plus the production apex redirect; `hid-ehr` (legacy `apps/ehr`; clinicians use `/provider/clinical` on www or staging), `hid-lab`, `hid-pharmacy`, `hid-ocr`, `hid-ocr-redirect` and `hid-outreach` are not deployed; the TUF repository Workers stay with their protected publication. `verify-config.mjs` checks the manifest against the decided subset and that every configured Worker is classified once, keeps every earlier check of the legacy Workers, and drops the manifest and service-worker requirement only for the Health-id artefact. `npm --prefix infra/cloudflare run verify:launch:<environment>` also requires a staged artefact for each launch Health-id Worker of that environment and re-verifies it against its provenance and the staging rules; it keeps every Worker configuration check but needs no legacy `apps/<app>/dist` build of a Worker the launch does not deploy there. `dry-run:<environment>` runs that check, then Wrangler for the launch Workers only. The staging readiness plan binds the two launch frontends, expects no Custom Domain binding on the six hosts that are not deployed, proposes a Turnstile widget for the launch hosts only and names `VITE_HID_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY`.
  - **Unchanged:** `frontend-worker.mjs` (host and environment binding, `X-Real-IP`, security headers, `/api/v1` proxying); the legacy Workers' configurations and their checks; the gateway route switch, which keeps the APIs of the deferred modules closed (`HID_ENABLED_API_ROUTES`, D-Eminence/hid-system#65); Identity's Turnstile allowlist. The Worker sets no Content Security Policy for any frontend; Health-id loads only its own assets and the Turnstile script from `challenges.cloudflare.com` and needs none to work. (Later: D-Eminence/hid-system#72 adds HSTS and the portal policy, ADR-055.)
  - **Not in this change:** the signed release bundle's frontend contract (`release/config/components.json` `frontends` and `frontend_archive.required_files`), which still describes the seven legacy builds with a manifest and a service worker, so until it is extended the staged artefact's provenance is the frontend release record; narrowing the regional stack's `browserSubdomains` (Identity `CORS_ORIGINS`) and Identity's Turnstile allowlist to the launch hosts; an edge CSP (later: D-Eminence/hid-system#72, ADR-055); the legacy `GET /migrate*` and `/ocr*` redirects on www, which point at the Migrate host that is not deployed.
  - **Health-id team:** the four builds of P14 from the release's Health-id commit; no Health-id code change.

- **Production launch configuration (D-Eminence/hid-system#66; ADR-053; `infra/aws`, notification-api, EHR, the OCR worker, the two external preflights; no migration or grant change)**. Register INFRA-COUNT, D4, the code part of NOTIF-PROD, CFG-PROD, and the preflight parts of I1 and REC-GOOGLE. Not deployed.
  - **Capacity (INFRA-COUNT):** every production `*DesiredCount` defaulted to 2 and the autoscaling minimum equalled it, contradicting ADR-032 and the I6 rollout. Each now defaults to 0, keeps its live minimum and recommended count in its description, and is raised only through the reviewed I6 parameter file (P20). A module outside the enabled set is pinned at 0 tasks in every environment (desired count and scaling ceiling 0..0, no scaling policy, no alarm): Lab, Pharmacy, OCR API and worker and Outreach unless `HID_ENABLED_API_ROUTES` names them, and the Notification Worker unless the new synth input `HID_NOTIFICATION_WORKER_ENABLED` (default `true` outside production; production refuses `true` until a Novu account and region are approved). In staging and development the modules that are off now default to 0 too (economy and development Lab, Pharmacy, OCR API and Outreach from 1, fidelity from 2 and the fidelity OCR worker from 1); an acceptance run opts them in as before (§6 step 11).
  - **Alarms (D4):** none of the 47 regional alarms had an action. The synth input `HID_OPERATIONAL_ALARM_TOPIC_ARN` names the operator-created topic `hid-<env>-operational-alerts` by exact ARN (production refuses to synthesize without it; a stack bound to an account and region refuses a topic elsewhere), and every alarm, the issuer's included, notifies it on ALARM and on OK. New: issuer Lambda `Errors` and `Throttles`, issuer stage `5XXError` and `4XXError` (429s count there), `TokenRenewalUnavailable` from a literal-text metric filter for the agent's `Workload token renewal unavailable` line on each enabled token task, and `OtpDefinitiveFailures`. Production has 31 alarms and 16 metric filters. The topic, its customer-managed key and its subscriptions are operator work (P16).
  - **OTP delivery (NOTIF-PROD):** production notification-api required SES plus Termii, Meta and Brevo (`full`) and received eleven provider fields. The new `otp-email-sms` profile requires `SES_FROM_ADDRESS` and the three Termii fields, refuses WhatsApp configuration and sends (`400`), and treats Brevo as optional; production refuses every other profile and a local service token. The CDK injects only `sesFromAddress`, `termiiBaseUrl`, `termiiApiKey` and `termiiSenderId`. Every OTP send writes one PHI-free `notification.otp.outcome` line (channel, purpose, outcome, provider, fallback used, the response `requestId` as correlation id, an allowlisted safe code; never recipient, code or text), and a refused send is a `definitive_failure`.
  - **Configuration refusals (CFG-PROD):** EHR refuses `S3_ACCESS_KEY_ID`/`S3_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` and the OCR worker `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`/`AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` in production (Notification and the event dispatcher already did). Table-driven production refusal specs cover Identity (Turnstile disabled, insecure cookies, wildcard CORS, local-secret modes, local tokens and the rest it already refuses; no Identity code change), EHR, the OCR worker and Notification. The synth input `HID_QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED` (default `false`, refused unless `HID_QOREID_ENABLED=true`) becomes Identity's `QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED`; Identity's own production refusal of the flag is in the enrollment change.
  - **Preflights (I1, REC-GOOGLE):** `scripts/production-external-preflight.py` checks the approved production account and region (inputs; the repository fixes none; the staging account is refused), read-only and value-free: the auth, Identity sensitive and notification provider fields, the five Identity keys in canonical 32-byte base64, exactly the four `otp-email-sms` fields, `googleOidcClientIds` present and empty (reports `google_sign_in_enabled`, fails the launch profile otherwise), SES production access, sending and DKIM, and the topic's customer-managed key and confirmed subscriptions (P15). The staging preflight accepts an empty `googleOidcClientIds`, reports `google_sign_in_enabled`, and with `--qoreid` checks both NIN keys and the QoreID credential field names.
  - **Review follow-up:** a production synth refuses `HID_QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED=true` even with QoreID on, and the regional stack refuses a production configuration that enables it (before, two synth inputs injected `true` into identity-api, and Identity on `main` accepts it in production; the N1 change lifts both refusals with Identity's, so the merge order cannot enable it); `verify:synth` requires the production identity-api flag to be `false`. The production preflight resolves the topic's key with `kms describe-key` (customer managed, enabled, in the topic's account and region; every spelling of the AWS-managed `alias/aws/sns` key fails, where only the literal alias did) and reads its default key policy for a `cloudwatch.amazonaws.com` grant of `kms:Decrypt` and `kms:GenerateDataKey`, reporting booleans only. `notification.otp.outcome` adds `primaryProvider` and `primarySafeCode` when the fallback ran: 0047 seeds Brevo as an enabled fallback, production gives it no key, and its `not_configured` hid the SES or Termii code; an operator pauses Brevo before ingress (P21). EHR also refuses `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` and `AWS_SESSION_TOKEN` in production (its S3 client's default chain reads them before the task role). Documented: the five enabled running-task alarms are in ALARM, and notify, while production runs zero tasks (P20); the staging ECS check of an empty `googleOidcClientIds` (S4m (6), P19); `AWS_COST_MODEL.md` default runtimes; the runbook §12 synth command with the topic ARN.
  - **Not in this change:** an alarm for a stopped token agent or for Identity's `notification_workload_token_unavailable` line, scoping `ses:SendEmail` to the verified identity, a Brevo or Novu production decision, and anything in AWS (topic, key, secrets, SES, Termii).
- **Enrollment gate, re-display and re-verify windows, QoreID lookup quota (D-Eminence/hid-system#68, migration 0085; Identity and database)**. Launch decisions N1 and N2 (`PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md`), blockers ENR-4 step 1, ENR-3 Part B, ENR-3b, IP-1 (QoreID cost) and CFG-PROD (NIN-only flag); `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, `QOREID_VERIFICATION_CONTRACT.md`, `DATABASE.md` ("Public enrollment windows and NIN lookup quota (0085)"), ADR-048.
  - **Gate first (ENR-4 step 1):** `POST /identity/patient-enrollments` read whether the NIN was bound or pending, and answered `409 NIN_ALREADY_REGISTERED`, before the QoreID flags and the runtime pause were checked inside the provider, so a bound or pending NIN was told apart from any other whatever the gate's state; a unit test pinned that order. Start now asks the gate (`QOREID_ENABLED`, `QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED`, then `qoreid`/`patient_nin` not paused) right after the Origin, Turnstile, signed-in-session and input checks, before any rate bucket or registration read: while it is closed every NIN, and a same-key resume, gets the same `503`, and nothing is charged. With the gate open a bound or pending NIN is still `409` before QoreID is asked; only the N1 holder step can close that.
  - **HID re-display (N2(b), 15 minutes):** `GET /current` and an activation replay showed the HID until the cookie expired, 24 hours after start. Every successful activation response now expires `hid_enrollment`; an enrollment activated more than 15 minutes ago (or with no activation time) is `404 ENROLLMENT_NOT_FOUND`, without the HID, on every enrollment route, and the activation command refuses such a replay (`P0002 PATIENT_ENROLLMENT_REPLAY_EXPIRED`, answered `404`).
  - **Contact proof (N2(c), 30 minutes):** nothing checked `contact_verified_at`. Activation with a proof older than 30 minutes now clears the proof and the contact by the database clock, returns the enrollment to `verify_contact` and answers `409 ENROLLMENT_CONTACT_REVERIFY_REQUIRED`, before the activation budget, argon2id or the command; the command refuses it too (`23514 PATIENT_CONTACT_REVERIFY_REQUIRED`), and Identity then resets and answers the same.
  - **QoreID lookup quota (IP-1):** nothing bounded the paid NIN lookups of public enrollment; the 0049 `patient_nin` quota needs an account. `platform.consume_public_patient_nin_quota` (0085, modelled on 0063) charges each lookup, after the gate and immediately before QoreID is called, to a keyed digest of the client network (IPv4 address or IPv6 /64) per hour and per day and to a platform-wide daily cap: `PATIENT_ENROLLMENT_QOREID_NETWORK_HOURLY_MAX` 5, `PATIENT_ENROLLMENT_QOREID_NETWORK_DAILY_MAX` 15 and `PATIENT_ENROLLMENT_QOREID_DAILY_CAP` 200 by default, bounded by the command. A refusal is audited (`identity.public-patient-enrollment.nin-lookup-quota`, `denied`, the exhausted limit) and answered `429 VERIFICATION_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` without a lookup; any quota fault is `503 VERIFICATION_QUOTA_UNAVAILABLE`. A resume, a duplicate and every earlier refusal are not charged.
  - **NIN-only flag (CFG-PROD):** production accepted `QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED` whenever QoreID was on. Identity now refuses to start with it under `HID_DEPLOYMENT_ENV=production`, or `NODE_ENV=production` outside the staging profile, until the N1 holder possession step ships; a table-driven spec covers it and the other Identity production refusals.
  - **Pending decisions and dependencies:** the owner sets `PATIENT_ENROLLMENT_QOREID_DAILY_CAP` from the QoreID contract (200 is a conservative launch default, not a contract figure). The cap bounds cost, not availability: every lookup counts toward it whatever QoreID answers, so with the defaults 14 networks passing Turnstile can spend the day's cap with unregistered NINs (40 within one calendar hour), and every enrollment start that needs a lookup then answers `429 VERIFICATION_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` until the next day bucket; resumes and the later steps are unaffected. Size the cap with that in mind and alert on `platform_day` denials (§10). Until the edge delivers the real client address (IP-1, the edge and gateway change), every client behind one load balancer node shares one network budget, so the per-network limits can deny real enrollments; a carrier-grade NAT address is one network too. HID delivery and the HID reminder (N2(a), ENR-2), and the holder step and contact-step non-disclosure (N1, ENR-4 step 2), are later changes.
  - **Unchanged:** request and success bodies, the Origin, Turnstile, idempotency and signed-in-session checks and their order, the start, contact and activation rate limits, the contact-code budget and the provider self-enrollment quota.
  - **Registration not open, and an availability read (FE-4; Identity only, no migration or grant; ADR-057):** in production every start answers `503 QOREID_NIN_ONLY_DISABLED` or `503 QOREID_DISABLED` (N1 keeps NIN-only enrollment off; infrastructure leaves QoreID off by default; from 0093 `503 PATIENT_REGISTRATION_NOT_OPEN`, with the same detail, before these, ADR-062), and with facility-assisted registration then out of scope (D17; N2(d) revised that, ADR-063) no new patient could get a Health ID at launch. Health-id 82b474e links to `/enroll` and learns this only after a NIN and a spent Turnstile token. Identity's problem filter replaced every 5xx detail with "The service could not complete the request." (what a client calling Identity directly got), and the gateway then replaced every upstream 502/503/504 with `503 UPSTREAM_UNAVAILABLE` "A required platform service is temporarily unavailable." and dropped the `QOREID_*` code, so that is what the patient saw until ADR-055 (D-Eminence/hid-system#72, merged) made the gateway pass upstream errors through. Start now answers those two codes, with status and codes unchanged, with "New Health ID registration is not open yet. If you already have a Health ID, sign in." (a `PublicDetailProblem`; every other 5xx detail is still replaced), and `GET /api/v1/identity/patient-enrollments/availability` answers `200 { "open": boolean }` from the same checks (enrollment keys, both flags, the runtime pause): public, no input, no audit row, no cookie, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=30`; a runtime-state fault is `503`. Identity's CORS middleware still runs first, as on every route: no `Origin` or one in `CORS_ORIGINS` gets the answer, a foreign `Origin` gets `500` with no CORS permission. The gateway serves it under the identity prefix only. Open for the owner: confirm launching with registration closed, or prioritise ENR-4 step 2 (`PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md`, N1).

- **Account recovery and patient search hardening (D-Eminence/hid-system#69, migration 0086; Identity and database)**. Launch blockers REC-AUDIENCE, REC-UNVERIFIED-CONTACT, REC-GOOGLE, SIGNIN-LOCKOUT, PSEARCH-1 (with ENUM-1 and DB-RLS-PATIENTS) and the recent-sign-in and hold parts of REC-NOTIFY (`PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md`, "Account recovery and patient search"; `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`; `DATABASE.md`, "Account recovery and patient search hardening (0086)"):
  - **Recovery reaches only its own account kind (REC-AUDIENCE):** the patient, staff and administrator reset pages and legacy activation all call `POST /auth/otp/start`, which looked the identifier up among every account. A staff or administrator reset page could therefore send a patient's code, and the patient page an administrator's. Each page now names its audience by its `turnstileAction` (`patient-reset-start` and `legacy-recovery`: an active patient record; `staff-reset`: an active staff record; `admin-reset`: a platform administrator, `auth.recovery_account_audience`), and an account of another kind is answered and counted as an unknown identifier: `202`, a `challengeId`, no code. A completed recovery already revoked every session of the account, staff and platform ones included; the verifier now shows it for a staff reset. The resend cooldown is kept per purpose, page and identifier, so a start on another page never holds back the account's own page.
  - **Codes only to verified contacts (REC-UNVERIFIED-CONTACT):** a patient's code went to the account email whether or not it was ever verified, so whoever entered an address at enrollment could take the account over. It now goes to the email only once `email_verified_at` is set, else to the verified phone of the account's active public enrollment, else nowhere, with the same `202`. Staff and administrator accounts carry no `email_verified_at` (their work email is provisioned) and keep receiving at it. Imported legacy patients whose source never confirmed the email and who have no verified enrollment phone can no longer activate by code (§10). The same holds for every account that facility-assisted (governed) enrollment creates (`POST /identity/registration-cases/:caseId/enroll`, `identity.enroll_registered_patient`, 0032): a `pending_reset` account with an unverified email and no enrollment phone, which can never receive an activation or recovery code. Facility-assisted registration is off at launch (D17); enabling it needs an email verification step before activation (`PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md`, risk (b)).
  - **Recovery ends Google sign-in (REC-GOOGLE):** a Google link survived the owner's password reset, and Google sign-in issues a session at the current token version, so a link made by whoever once held the password kept working. `auth.complete_recovery_otp` now revokes every external sign-in identity of the account (today Google links) not already revoked, in the same transaction, and its audit event counts them. The legacy importer's provenance record (issuer `legacy-identity`) is not a sign-in: it is kept and not counted (the first version of this change revoked it too, so reconciliation would have flagged every recovered legacy account). The revoked row stays and the Google link commands (0059) refuse any subject ever linked, so after a recovery the owner can link only a different Google account; a later change must allow re-linking the owner's own subject before Google sign-in is enabled (off at launch, D17).
  - **One failure budget per account (SIGNIN-LOCKOUT):** sign-in failures were counted per identifier as typed, so the email and the HID of one account had two budgets of five per 15 minutes. Each failure is now also counted against the resolved account (`auth.login_attempts`, its own HMAC label), and while that budget is locked even the correct password is refused by either identifier, with the same `401`. No schema change.
  - **Patient search does not enumerate (PSEARCH-1, ENUM-1, DB-RLS-PATIENTS):** `POST /identity/patient-lookup` answered `404` for an unknown HID and `403` for a patient without a grant, and `POST /identity/access-requests` answered `404` for an unknown HID and returned the `patientId` of a known one, so a clinician could test which HIDs exist and learn patient ids. The clinician's own list, `GET /identity/access-requests`, also named the patient of every request, pending ones included. Every refused lookup is now the same `404 PATIENT_NOT_AVAILABLE` (the audit keeps `not_found` or `not_authorized`); a lookup that succeeds answers `200` (it was `201`). An access request for an unknown HID answers like a recorded one (`201`, a new `accessRequestId`, `pending`) without persisting anything and is audited as `identity.access-request.refused`; no create answer carries `patientId`, and the clinician's list names the patient only while a grant made from the request is active (otherwise the database returns `null` and Identity answers the string `withheld-` plus the last four characters of the request id, with `patientIdWithheld: true`, so Health-id 82b474e keeps rendering the list, FE-1; `identity.list_my_staff_access_requests` is replaced by 0086 and Identity's projection applies the same rule). The first version of this change left the list as it was, so one request for a guessed HID and one list call still gave the patient id. Access requests and refused lookups are counted per actor and per facility (`429 PATIENT_LOOKUP_RATE_LIMITED`, `429 ACCESS_REQUEST_RATE_LIMITED`; defaults in `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`); a lookup that returns a granted patient is not (FE-10, below). In the database, the policy `patients_registration_read` let any member with a registration permission read every patient row of every facility; 0086 narrows it to patients linked to a registration case or candidate at the member's facility (dropping and creating the policy again in one transaction: the first version used `ALTER POLICY`, which the static privilege replay `verify-runtime-locking-privileges.mjs` refuses, so `npm run verify` and the rehearsal failed), and registration's duplicate checks use two narrow functions. A lookup still never grants access.
  - **Recent sign-in and recovery hold (REC-NOTIFY, part):** a patient's approval of an access request needed only a session. It now needs a session family first issued within 10 minutes (`403 PATIENT_RECENT_AUTH_REQUIRED`, detail "For your security, sign out, sign in again, and approve within 10 minutes."; FE-8 below), as the access PIN already did. For 24 hours after an OTP recovery of the account, an approval, an access-PIN set and an emergency-contact add or update answer `403 PATIENT_RECENT_RECOVERY_HOLD`; denials, revocations, a PIN removal, contact deactivation and contact verification stay available, so the owner can still shut access.
  - **Health-id compatibility (FE-10, FE-8; Identity only, no migration or grant change):** the first version counted every `POST /identity/patient-lookup` against the budgets of 60 per actor and 600 per facility in 5 minutes, successful ones included. Health-id's clinical page looks the patient up before it loads encounters and before every clinical write (about ten lookups a consultation), so a busy clinician or facility would have had documentation saves refused `429 PATIENT_LOOKUP_RATE_LIMITED` in normal use (FE-10). A lookup now reads the actor's and the facility's budgets first and answers `429` before reading anything only while one is blocked; it counts only a refusal (unknown HID, no grant, grant at another facility or purpose, alike), after its audit event and before the `404`, and the refusal that crosses a maximum answers `429` for an unknown and an unauthorized HID alike. A lookup that returns a granted patient and a dependency failure (`5xx`) are not counted; access-request budgets are unchanged. Every refusal is still counted the same way, so the budget still bounds HID guessing at 60 refusals per actor per window, and once it is blocked a clinician's lookups of granted patients are refused too until the window ends. The approval's `403 PATIENT_RECENT_AUTH_REQUIRED` detail was "Sign in again before approving access to your records", which Health-id shows as it is on Approve with no way forward for a patient who is signed in; it is now "For your security, sign out, sign in again, and approve within 10 minutes." (FE-8). Same status and code.
  - **Pending (REC-NOTIFY, owner notice):** the owner is not yet told that their password was changed. Identity offers every completed recovery, after commit, to an optional `ACCOUNT_RECOVERY_NOTICE_SINK` (account id, purpose and correlation id); none is bound in this change, so nothing is sent. The notification route change binds it and must send to every verified contact and the inbox.
  - **Residual:** a clinician can still tell whether an HID exists from their own records of it: their access-request list shows only recorded requests (without the patient id until a grant is active), and repeating a request for a known patient answers the pending request again (same `accessRequestId`, `existingRequest: true`) where an unknown HID gets a new id each time. The actor and facility budgets (20 and 200 requests per 15 minutes by default) bound how fast, and every request is audited. Hiding these would need decoy requests in the clinician's list. A refused lookup for a known HID also runs one more read (the audit subject) than for an unknown one, which response times can show.
- **Governed clinician provisioning and one membership per facility** (launch decisions D14, workforce part, and N5; ADR-049; D-Eminence/hid-system#70, migration 0087; Identity `admin/workforce-*`, `auth/current-staff-context.service.ts`; `services/ehr-api/scripts/promote-legacy-identity.mjs`).
  - **Before:** onboarding created one `org_admin` per facility and nothing else could create a clinician except the legacy promotion or SQL (launch register WF-1). One account could hold two active memberships at one facility, and which one a request bound, with its permissions and consent grants, depended on row order (N5).
  - **Provisioning:** platform administrators holding the new `platform.workforce.manage` (`platform_super_admin` only) attach a staff identity to a verified facility (`POST /admin/workforce/memberships`; a new account is `pending_reset` with no password, the membership `pending`), record a professional-credential attestation (`POST /admin/workforce/accounts/:accountId/attestations`; documented manual review, evidence reference, reviewer, lapse at most three years ahead), grant or revoke one clinical role on one membership (`POST /admin/workforce/memberships/:membershipId/roles`) and end a membership (`POST .../end`). Every command needs an MFA platform session, a fresh step-up, a reason and an `Idempotency-Key`, and `If-Match` where it changes a staff record or membership (`INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md` §19). A grant needs a current attestation for the role by a reviewer who is neither the target, nor working at the facility (so not its `org_admin`), nor the granter: every clinical role takes two platform administrators. Grants are limited to `doctor`, `clinician` and `nurse` (D17 keeps `lab` and `pharmacist` off; both can be revoked). A membership holding `org_admin` or `admin` cannot also hold a clinical role. Ending a membership revokes its roles, expires its pending access requests, revokes its consent grants (an approval that commits during the end included) and, if it was active, revokes all the account's staff sessions; ended is terminal. A role stops giving permissions when its attestation lapses; a new attestation and a second administrator's grant renew it (`renewed: true`), before or after the lapse.
  - **Review fixes:** the commands lock the account rows FOR UPDATE and then the membership or staff row FOR NO KEY UPDATE, so an approval or access request that references the membership no longer deadlocks with an end or a grant; the end expires requests before it revokes grants; a grant renews a role resting on a lapsed or shorter attestation instead of refusing it; the staff actor's `roles` (and the token's `roles` claim and Health-id's staff badges) are the membership's effective role assignments, not its stored labels; the platform admin verifier runs on either side of 0086; the legacy promotion's duplicate check is tested against the real schema (`verify-membership-duplicates-runtime.mjs`, a new rehearsal step).
  - **N5:** a unique partial index allows one active, unheld membership per account and facility; 0087 stops and changes nothing if duplicates exist (I4 first). Identity's staff context answers `409 FACILITY_MEMBERSHIP_AMBIGUOUS` instead of binding by row order if two are ever read, and orders by membership id last. The legacy promotion refuses duplicates before it writes.
  - **Unchanged:** onboarding still creates only an `org_admin`, which still has no clinical permission; patient consent is still needed for each patient. Staff sign-in is MFA-verified once staff MFA (0088, D-Eminence/hid-system#71) ships; a clinician provisioned here enrols an authenticator at the first sign-in.
  - **External dependencies:** product and legal owners decide which credential source counts (regulator registry or documented manual review) and who may review; a registry method needs a later migration. Launch needs at least two MFA-enrolled Super Admins. If launch relies on migrated hid-1.0 clinicians, the legacy cutover still needs its operator authorization, and I4 must run first.
  - **Frontend dependency:** Health-id has no workforce page yet; until it has one, the routes are reachable only through the API with a platform session (health_id_impact in the change's report).

- **Staff MFA (D-Eminence/hid-system#71, migration 0088; Identity and database)**. Launch blocker MFA-1, decision D14 (MFA part: decided yes, under "verified identity for sensitive operations"; ADR-054; `PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md`, "Staff MFA"; `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, "Staff sign-in with an authenticator"; `PHASE_4_STAGE_2A_BACKEND_SECURITY.md` §10; `DATABASE.md`, "Staff MFA sessions (0088)"):
  - **Defect:** a staff session, which reaches patient data through Identity, EHR, Lab, Pharmacy, OCR and Outreach, was issued on the password alone: by `POST /auth/login`, by provider enrollment activation and by a staff Google exchange. Only platform administration required an authenticator.
  - **Two-step staff sign-in:** `POST /auth/login` now answers only `{ status: 'mfa_required' | 'mfa_enrollment_required', expiresAt }` with an httpOnly `hid_access_mfa` challenge cookie (path `/api/v1/auth/mfa`) and no session cookie; an account without an eligible staff membership gets the wrong-password `401`. The session and its `auth.session_assurance` row are issued at `POST /auth/mfa/verify` (TOTP or recovery code) or, on a first sign-in, `POST /auth/mfa/enroll/activate`. No permission allowlist: every staff sign-in needs it.
  - **First enrolment needs a fresh proof:** `POST /auth/mfa/enroll/email-code` sends a six-digit code to the account email (only a keyed digest bound to the challenge is stored; five sends per 15 minutes), and `POST /auth/mfa/enroll/start` refuses without it (`403 MFA_ENROLLMENT_PROOF_REQUIRED`, no factor created) and counts a wrong one. The code goes to `auth.accounts.email` without requiring `email_verified_at`, which no staff flow sets; the code proves the address. Provider enrollment activation is its own proof: it now answers a staff enrolment challenge instead of a session. The database refuses to consume a staff enrolment challenge without a verified proof (0088).
  - **The proof belongs to the factor (review fix):** the first version let a factor enrolled at platform sign-in, which is trust on first use, verify the staff sign-in of an account that is both a platform administrator and a staff member, so its password alone became a staff session with facility access. 0088 now records the proof on the factor (`auth.mfa_factors.enrollment_proof`, `enrollment_challenge_id`; a trigger accepts them only from a live, verified staff enrolment challenge of the same account and never lets them change), and `auth.guard_session_assurance_change` refuses staff assurance on a factor without one (`STAFF_MFA_FACTOR_UNPROVEN`). A platform first enrolment of an account with an eligible staff membership answers `409 MFA_ENROLLMENT_STAFF_SIGN_IN_REQUIRED` (enrol at staff sign-in, then use that factor for both); a staff sign-in on a factor without a proof (every factor enrolled before 0088, or before the account held a staff membership) answers `403 STAFF_MFA_FACTOR_UNPROVEN` at the password step, with no challenge, until a two-person reset (P22).
  - **Staff per-IP failure budget (review fix):** staff second-factor failures count per source address in a bucket of their own that blocks at the 100th failure in 15 minutes; platform sign-in keeps its bucket of 20. The shared budget of 20 let a few accounts' failures refuse every correct code from a facility whose staff share one address. The per-account budget (the fifth failure) is unchanged.
  - **Unassured staff sessions refused:** `SecurityGuard` refuses a staff session whose family has no live assurance with `403 STAFF_MFA_REQUIRED` (audited), so the delegated checks of EHR, Lab, Pharmacy, OCR and Outreach refuse it too and relay the code; `POST /auth/refresh` refuses it with `403 STAFF_MFA_REQUIRED`, clears the cookies and ends the sign-in (`staff_mfa_required`). **Every staff session issued before the deploy has no assurance and is invalidated at deploy**; every staff member signs in again and enrols. Sign-out keeps working.
  - **Google exchange is for patients only:** `POST /auth/oidc/exchange` answers any staff action `403 GOOGLE_STAFF_SIGN_IN_DISABLED` before Turnstile or the token exchange.
  - **Recovery and reset:** OTP password recovery keeps the factor (`auth.complete_recovery_otp` unchanged, now pinned by `staff-mfa.integration.sql`); a lost staff authenticator is reset by the existing two-person MFA reset (0070 checks no account kind), after which the next sign-in enrols again on a fresh emailed code.
  - **Audit and notices:** `auth.staff.mfa.enrolled`, `auth.staff.mfa.failed`, `auth.staff.mfa.recovery-code.used` (and, new, `auth.admin.mfa.recovery-code.used`), `auth.login` with the MFA method. Enrolment, recovery-code use and an MFA reset are offered after commit to an optional `MFA_NOTICE_SINK`; none is bound, so nothing is sent until the notification route change binds it.
  - **Residual:** an account without an authenticator is as strong as its mailbox until it enrols, and the mailbox alone is enough: staff password recovery and the enrolment code use the same address, and accounts provisioned without a password (0046, 0087) set their first one by that recovery. Once an account has a factor, a mailbox alone never yields a staff session. P22 makes enrolment before clinical roles, and a review of enrolments that followed a recovery, operator gates; a second enrolment channel is an open decision (D14). `MFA_SECRET_KEY_B64` (P3) is now needed for staff sign-in too (`503 MFA_UNAVAILABLE` without it). With `AUTH_MODE=oidc` staff tokens carry no assurance and are refused. The in-repo `apps/ehr`, `apps/admin` and `apps/web` still expect a session from `/auth/login`; they are not launch frontends.

- **Edge and gateway hardening for launch (D-Eminence/hid-system#72; ADR-055; Cloudflare frontend Worker, gateway and `infra/aws`; no migration, grant or service change)**. Register WAF-CRS-BODY, the non-Identity part of A7, A8, D6, D7 and SCAN-EDGE/EDGE-DOCS; IP-THRESHOLDS decided (`PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md` §2). Not deployed.
  - **Long clinical notes (WAF-CRS-BODY):** the public API web ACL ran the AWS Core Rule Set with its defaults, whose `SizeRestrictions_BODY` blocks every body over 8 KB, while ehr-api accepts note content up to 100,000 characters (512 KiB JSON) and Health-id's editor allows 100,000: a long note got a WAF `403` with a non-JSON body and was not saved. The rule now only counts and labels; `AwsCommonRulesBodySize` (priority 11) blocks a labelled request unless it is a `POST` or `PATCH` whose raw path (no transformation, lowercase UUIDs) is `/api/v1/ehr/patients/<uuid>/encounters`, `/<uuid>`, or its `clinical-notes` (with `/<uuid>/revisions`), `diagnoses`, `prescriptions` or `lab-requests` (with `/<uuid>`). Vitals, every other route, and encoded, dot-segment or upper-case spellings keep the limit; every other CRS rule keeps blocking (on an ALB they inspect a body's first 8 KB). The gateway states the bound: `client_max_body_size 512k` with a Problem Details `413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` (the services' own code).
  - **WAF rate limit per client (A7, WAF part):** `ApiOriginRateLimit` aggregated by the connecting address, which is always a Cloudflare egress address, so one client could exhaust 2,000 requests per 5 minutes for everyone behind it. It now aggregates on `X-HID-Client-Network` (`FORWARDED_IP`, fallback `MATCH`), which the Worker derives from `CF-Connecting-IP` and always sets, replacing a caller's value: the IPv4 address, or the canonical network address of the IPv6 /64 (IPv4-mapped IPv6 counts as IPv4, as in Identity's `quotaNetwork`), so a client cannot escape the limit by rotating addresses inside its /64 (review follow-up; keyed on the full IPv6 address in `X-Real-IP` it could).
