Rule 0 has already refused anything without the Worker's origin secret. A request without the header is not counted there (the Worker sends it whenever it sends `X-Real-IP`, without which the gateway refuses the request); `ApiOriginEdgeRateLimit` (priority 31) blocks a connecting address over 50,000 per 5 minutes, the backstop for a leaked secret. That backstop is shared: traffic spread over many client networks that passes it blocks every user behind that Cloudflare egress address for the rest of the window. Both limits are code constants (`API_ORIGIN_CLIENT_RATE_LIMIT`, `API_ORIGIN_EDGE_RATE_LIMIT`), changed only by a reviewed CDK change. The gateway also stops forwarding `X-HID-Origin-Authorization` and `X-HID-Client-Network` to the services. The MFA failure bucket (the Identity part of A7) is not in this change.
  - **Backend answers pass through (A8):** with `proxy_intercept_errors on`, the gateway's `error_page 502 503 504` replaced every backend 502/503/504 by `503 UPSTREAM_UNAVAILABLE`, dropping the code, `Set-Cookie`, `Retry-After` and `X-Correlation-ID` (for example `PLATFORM_MAINTENANCE`, `MFA_UNAVAILABLE`, `TURNSTILE_UNAVAILABLE`, `OTP_DELIVERY_UNAVAILABLE`, `QOREID_DISABLED`, `EMERGENCY_CONTACT_DELIVERY_UNAVAILABLE`). The directive is removed; `error_page` still answers `503 UPSTREAM_UNAVAILABLE` when nginx gets no answer (refused, reset, timed out, unresolved, closed without a response). Refresh: the only refresh 5xx the interception swallowed was the `503` of a failed post-commit audit, after rotation; its cookie clearing now reaches the browser instead of leaving it a spent refresh token whose next use was treated as reuse. A refresh `500` (a database error before rotation) already passed through and already cleared the cookies. A6 (keep cookies on a 5xx; Identity) stays open and this change adds no new sign-out.
  - **Portal CSP and HSTS (D6):** every frontend Worker response now carries `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000` (no `includeSubDomains`). `hid-web` and `hid-admin` send `default-src 'none'; script-src 'self' https://challenges.cloudflare.com; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; font-src 'self'; connect-src 'self' https://challenges.cloudflare.com; frame-src https://challenges.cloudflare.com; worker-src 'none'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; upgrade-insecure-requests`, derived from the Health-id 82b474e build output (its own `/assets/` only, no inline script, no `eval`, no external fonts) and Turnstile's `api.js` (redirect and self-replacement within `challenges.cloudflare.com`, the widget frame, `sendBeacon`/`fetch` failure feedback there). API and Worker-written answers send `default-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'`. A policy from the origin or the asset binding is replaced. The undeployed legacy product hosts get no page policy. `hid-web` grants no camera or microphone.
  - **Access log (D7):** the gateway inherited the image's `main` format, which logs the whole request line, so administrator searches (`GET /api/v1/admin/principals?query=…`) and audit filters were kept in CloudWatch for a year. It now logs its own JSON format `hid_api` (`escape=json`) to the image's access-log path (stdout): time, client address, method, path (`$uri`, no query), status, bytes, request and upstream time, upstream status, `request_id` and `X-Correlation-ID`. Health checks stay unlogged. The error log (review follow-up): nginx appends the request line and the upstream URL, query included, to every error or warning about a request (a refused, reset, timed-out or unresolved upstream, a body over 512 KiB, a body buffered to a temporary file, which every clinical write over 16 KiB is), and the image's notice-level error log is the container's stderr, in the same log group; the server now logs errors at `crit` only. A critical message about a request (nginx cannot write a temporary file, or cannot open an upstream connection for a reason other than refusal or an unreachable network) still carries the request line.
  - **Scanner callback (SCAN-EDGE/EDGE-DOCS):** `POST /api/v1/ehr/documents/scan-events` was public through the gateway with no legitimate caller (no scanner subject exists, D5); every anonymous POST with an `Authorization` header cost an EHR-to-Identity call and audit rows. The `/api/v1/ehr/` deny now covers it in every spelling (`404 API_ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`); the scanner calls ehr-api on its private service name when uploads ship (I7).
  - **Checks:** `scripts/tests/gateway-config.test.mjs` and the container-verifier contract (`scripts/lib/gateway-contract.mjs`, now 13 internal routes) check each behaviour against the nginx model, which now models `log_format`, named locations, `error_page` and the inherited `access_log`, `proxy_intercept_errors` and `client_max_body_size`. The new `scripts/verify-gateway-runtime.mjs` runs the shipped configuration under a real nginx against stub upstreams, including the error log after a refused upstream, an oversized body and a buffered body; with `--pinned-image` (`npm run verify:gateway-runtime:image`) its nginx is the one in the image `gateway/Dockerfile` pins, and it is the `gateway-runtime` step of `release/scripts/run-workspace-gates.mjs`, which the workspace job of `tuf-local-gates.yml` runs on every push and pull request with its log in the workspace evidence (`HID_NGINX_BINARY` runs a local binary). `infra/aws` tests and `verify-synth.mjs` check the rule order, the override, the label rule, the exempt-path expression against allowed and refused paths, and both rate rules in every non-sleep profile. The Worker tests check the policies on both launch hosts, both environments, API and edge answers.
  - **Compatibility:** Health-id 82b474e needs no change (its `Clinical.tsx:473` 100,000-character editor now saves; it loads only its own assets and Turnstile, calls only its own origin and never calls `scan-events`; it already branches on specific codes such as `502 LAB_HANDOFF_UNCONFIRMED` at `Clinical.tsx:233` and treats other 5xx generically). Gateway-log consumers that parsed the `main` format must read JSON. A Health-id release that adds a script, frame or connection origin (Sentry, Google sign-in, analytics) must change the policy in the same release. One 82b474e path calls another origin: the document upload `PUT`s the file to the presigned S3 URL (`packages/http/src/upload.ts:60`, from `apps/patient-web/src/staff/api.ts:1089`), which `connect-src` blocks; uploads are off at launch (D17), and the release that switches them on adds the documents bucket's S3 origin to `connect-src` for `hid-web` and `hid-admin` (`PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md` D5, I7). Downloads use `window.open` (`Clinical.tsx:890`), which `connect-src` does not govern.
  - **Not in this change:** the MFA failure bucket and the wave-2 rebase guard of A7 (Identity), A6 (refresh keeps cookies on a 5xx), `includeSubDomains`/preload, a page policy for the legacy product hosts, Cloudflare Logpush or ALB access logs (if ever enabled, they must exclude query strings for `/api/v1/admin/`), WAF logging, and an Identity `ip`-scope 429 metric (IP-THRESHOLDS).

- **Facility selection kept per sign-in (D-Eminence/hid-system#73, migration 0089; Identity and database)**. D-Eminence/hid-system#47 (ADR-046), written as 0080 and ported after the launch migrations; launch items C1 and FE-20, decision E7 (`PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md`); `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, "Working facility per sign-in"; `DATABASE.md`, "Session facility selection (0089)":
  - **Defect:** `POST /auth/facility` stored nothing, and staff session responses carried no `facilitySelection`. After a reload or refresh a clinician who works at several facilities was reported at the primary facility, and Health-id, which asks only when the field says so, moved them there without asking; encounters, notes and access requests could then be made at a facility they did not choose. Authorization still failed closed per request (`X-Facility-ID`).
  - **Kept per sign-in:** `auth.session_facility_selection` (0089) holds the choice per session family, written with its `auth.facility.select` audit in one transaction under the account lock (0073), and only while the presented session is a live staff session of the account; a database guard admits only an active membership of that account, refuses a move to another sign-in or account, and refuses deletes. Refresh keeps the choice; another sign-in of the account has its own. Staff responses report `selected`, `default` or `reselect` (the chosen membership ended: the user is asked again). The choice authorizes nothing: `X-Facility-ID` is still checked on every request.
  - **With staff MFA (0088):** the staff session issued by `POST /auth/mfa/verify` or `POST /auth/mfa/enroll/activate` reports `default`; the password step issues no session and reports nothing; a staff token without MFA assurance, `AUTH_MODE=oidc` included, is refused before anything is read (`403 STAFF_MFA_REQUIRED`).
  - **Refresh when the choice cannot be read:** `200` with the rotated cookies, the default facility and `facilitySelection: 'unavailable'`, which Health-id treats as unconfirmed and asks. #47 answered `503` with the rotated cookies, which the gateway's interception of upstream `5xx` (A8, removed by D-Eminence/hid-system#72) stripped, so the next refresh would have ended the sign-in as token reuse. `GET /auth/session` answers `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` with the cookies untouched.
  - **Unchanged:** the refresh's cookie clearing on a refused or failed rotation and its post-commit audit (A6 is a separate change, D-Eminence/hid-system#76); 0087's one active membership per facility and `409 FACILITY_MEMBERSHIP_AMBIGUOUS`.

- **Launch module permissions, patient-self binding, named grantee and PIN-use notice (D-Eminence/hid-system#75, migration 0091; database, EHR and Identity)**. Decisions D17 and D9 and the owner defaults (ADR-058; `PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md`, "Launch module permissions and patient access transparency"; `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, "Launch module set" and patient self-service; `DATABASE.md`, "Launch module withdrawal and patient access transparency (0091)")
  - **Defects:** 0084 withdrew only the Lab and Pharmacy writes, so clinical roles kept Lab, Pharmacy, OCR and Outreach reads and writes, `identity.break-glass.write` and `ehr.document.write`, and Health-id offered Laboratory, Pharmacy, Document OCR, Outreach, released Lab results, Extract with OCR, the upload card and emergency access, each failing (MOD-PERM-OCR-OUTREACH, FE-17, FE-15). The three launch controls were seeded on and only an operator turned them off (UPLOADS-DEFAULT-ON); upload completion did not check the control. The patient-self RLS context trusted transaction settings: a context naming another patient and a nonexistent session read that patient's completed encounters (PSELF-RLS). Patients approved and reviewed access seeing only a facility (CONSENT-GRANTEE), and were not told when their PIN was used, because only the notification worker, off at launch, consumed that event (NOTIF-PIN-NOTICE).
  - **Launch module permissions:** every `lab.*`, `pharmacy.*`, `ocr.*` and `outreach.*` permission, `identity.break-glass.write` and `ehr.document.write` is deleted from every role (105 rows on a seeded database); their routes answer `403 PERMISSION_DENIED` before any write, staff sessions lose them on their next request, and Health-id 82b474e hides or disables the surfaces (§3). The EHR's own permissions stay. The role assertions require it, on every bootstrap. A module is re-enabled, on staging too, only by its own reviewed migration, which changes the role assertions with it and, like every ledger migration, applies to production as well (ADR-058); no staging-only re-grant exists.
  - **Launch controls:** `uploads_enabled`, `outreach_portal_enabled` and `break_glass_enabled` are set off where each is still exactly as 0039 seeded it, with forced row-level security lifted for that update only (a non-superuser owner otherwise updates no row); no control event (no actor), P11 checks the result. `POST /ehr/documents/:id/complete` checks `uploads_enabled` first (`409`, before Identity, storage or any write).
  - **Patient-self binding:** `ehr.patient_self_context` (same signature, policies unchanged) is a `SECURITY DEFINER` check with `search_path pg_catalog, pg_temp` that requires the named patient to be the one `identity.patient_self_session` returns for the named subject and live session, and to belong to the named account; it is in the rehearsal's non-superuser owner check.
  - **Named grantee:** `GET /identity/me/access-requests` items carry `requesterDisplayName` and `requesterRole`; `GET /identity/me/access-history` items carry them too, and the response carries `truncated` (additive). The patient's list function is recreated, so its grant returns with the bootstrap (§5 step 31).
  - **PIN-use notice:** each new PIN grant places `PATIENT_ACCESS_PIN_USED` (resource `consent-grant`) in the patient's inbox, written by a trigger in the PIN transaction; a replay adds none.
  - **Deferred:** pending access requests still do not lapse (ACCESS-REQ-STALE): request creation reuses a pending request, so a lapse needs 0086's creation command, the approval and both lists changed together. Risk: a patient can approve an old request; mitigated by the named clinician, the recent sign-in approval needs, the expiry of an ended membership's requests (0087) and patient revocation.
  - **Residual:** Lab and Pharmacy data are unreadable through Health-id until re-granted (a launch database holds none). Health-id's ungated "Health ID Migrate" card still links to a refused page (cosmetic). The patient's request list answers `403` between 0091 and the bootstrap. No SMS or email notice of a PIN use until a notification channel is approved.
  - **With 0090 (D-Eminence/hid-system#74, ADR-056):** 0090's retraction guard requires `ehr.document.write` to mark an encounter entered in error while it holds an initiated or uploaded document, so once both are applied no role can do it (`403 ENCOUNTER_RETRACTION_NOT_PERMITTED`) until uploads are re-enabled; cancelling such an encounter is unaffected. A database on which no upload was ever started, as the launch production database is expected to be, holds no such document; a staging database might, and such an encounter then stays open or is cancelled. **Merge note:** 0090's `services/ehr-api/database/tests/encounter-retraction.integration.sql` expects the seeded nurse to hold `ehr.document.write` (its case "nurse marks entered in error encounter 9 (an uploaded document)"), so this change edits that suite, and has done so in its merge of 0090 (`d12a7cc`): in its rollback-only fixture, immediately before `set local session_replication_role = origin;`, add `insert into auth.role_permissions (role_code, permission_code) values ('nurse', 'ehr.document.write') on conflict do nothing;` with a comment that 0091 withdraws it from every role; add the case `(9, 'doctor', 'entered_in_error', 'an uploaded document', '42501: ENCOUNTER_RETRACTION_NOT_PERMITTED')` after the receptionist's case of encounter 9; and change its expected case count from 97 to 98. Without it the suite fails on the nurse case, and the rehearsal and CI with it; with it the suite passes 98 cases on a database built from empty through the real 0089, 0090 and 0091 (§8).

- **Identity and service error and authentication hardening (D-Eminence/hid-system#76, no migration; Identity, EHR, Outreach, Lab, OCR, Pharmacy and `@hid/api-client`)**. Launch review items A4, A5, A6, A9, A10, A16, A18, A19, A24 and LOGIN-DETAIL-ORACLE; ADR-059; decision NO-MEMBERSHIP-SIGN-IN (`PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md` §2, owner default (a)); `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, "Refusals and faults (ADR-059)":
  - **Defect:** Health-id signs out on `401` and retries a `5xx`, but Identity answered faults `401`: a database or pool fault in `SecurityGuard`, a runtime-control read that timed out, an unreachable OIDC key set (A4). Identity's refusal of a service's own workload credential was a `401` that EHR relayed to the browser (A5). `POST /auth/refresh` cleared the cookies on any failure, a `500` included, and wrote its audit row after the rotation committed (A6). A staff session on a patient-only Identity route failed in the handler and its audit, answering `503` with nothing recorded (A9). A disallowed `Origin` was a `500` in all six services (A19).
  - **Faults are `503`:** `AUTHENTICATION_UNAVAILABLE` (session verification, actor resolution, platform assurance, OIDC key set) and `PLATFORM_CONTROL_UNAVAILABLE` (any runtime-control read fault but a disabled control) in Identity, and the latter in Outreach. EHR's guard answers any fault with no status of its own `503 AUTHENTICATION_UNAVAILABLE` (it answered `401`). The guard audits the denial first; a refused credential stays `401`.
  - **Workload refusals:** Identity answers `503 WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_UNAVAILABLE`, `401 WORKLOAD_AUTHENTICATION_FAILED`, `403 WORKLOAD_CALLER_DENIED` (scanner: `401 DOCUMENT_SCANNER_AUTHENTICATION_FAILED`). EHR answers Identity's refusal of its workload as `503 IDENTITY_SERVICE_AUTH_UNAVAILABLE` (`isWorkloadRefusal` in `@hid/api-client`) and refuses to send an expired Identity workload token (`503 IDENTITY_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_UNAVAILABLE`, log `identity_workload_token_expired`). Lab, Pharmacy, OCR and Outreach still relay Identity's refusal (off at launch; follow-up).
  - **Refresh:** `/auth/refresh` and `/auth/admin/refresh` clear the cookies only for a `4xx` refusal of the refresh token; a `5xx` keeps them. The `auth.refresh` audit row is written in the rotation's transaction, so a failed audit write rolls the rotation back and the presented token stays live.
  - **Patient-only routes:** `@PatientOnly()` routes (`/identity/me*` self service, emergency contacts, account deletion, NIN self-verification, the patient's consent decisions) refuse any other session in the guard with `403 PATIENT_SESSION_REQUIRED`, audited without a facility. Any other facility-less staff failure is audited as an `auth.*` event instead of answering `503`.
  - **Staff without an active membership (A10, owner default (a)):** the right password of an account with a staff record or membership, none eligible, is `403 NO_ACTIVE_FACILITY_MEMBERSHIP` at the password step, before any MFA challenge; no session, a denied `login_failed` session event, not counted as a failed sign-in (like any right password it ends the account's own failure window). An account with neither keeps `401`; a live session whose membership ended stays `401`.
  - **Uniform sign-in detail:** every other refused patient or staff sign-in credential is `401` with the detail "Invalid credentials"; a refused Google sign-in is `401 GOOGLE_SIGN_IN_DENIED`.
  - **Runtime controls (A16):** only a platform session is exempt in Identity; EHR and Outreach exempt no session. An administrator's clinical session now gets `503 PLATFORM_MAINTENANCE` and `423 PLATFORM_PORTAL_DISABLED` like any clinician's.
  - **Delegated read (A18):** after a delegated read skip, `POST /identity/service/authorization/check` decides only `read_records`; any other scope is `403 CSRF_VALIDATION_FAILED`.
  - **Admin URLs (A24):** `POST /admin/principals/search` (standard policy `platform.principals.search`) and `POST /admin/principals/export` take terms and reason in the body. The `GET` forms stay for one release for the Health-id `82b474e` console. The gateway's access log has omitted query strings since D-Eminence/hid-system#72 (ADR-055).
  - **Not done here:** the OCR guard `catch` (still `401` for a fault with no status; OCR is off at launch, D17); the workload refusal mapping of Lab, Pharmacy, OCR and Outreach (off at launch); an alarm on `identity_workload_token_expired`.

- **Turnstile contract (launch/turnstile-contract, no migration; Identity only)**. Audit items TS-2, TS-3, TS-6, TS-8, TS-9, TS-10, TS-11 and TS-12; `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, "Turnstile verification":
  - **Already in place, unchanged:** Identity verifies every Turnstile token itself with Cloudflare Siteverify, on the eleven routes the contract lists, and accepts it only for the request's own host and the route's action. No route trusts the widget, and no route outside the list checks a token.
  - **Siteverify errors (TS-3):** a Siteverify answer of `missing-input-secret`, `invalid-input-secret`, `bad-request` or `internal-error` with HTTP 200 was answered `403 TURNSTILE_REJECTED`. Cloudflare sends the secret and request errors with HTTP 400, which was already `503`. Both are now `503 TURNSTILE_UNAVAILABLE`. Each refusal and outage writes one log line, `event=turnstile_verification`, with the outcome, the action and Cloudflare's error codes; an outage line also has a reason and the attempt count. Siteverify refusing Identity's own request (a 4xx, or one of the configuration codes) is `reason=siteverify_refused_configuration` at error level, read from the answer's body; Cloudflare's `internal-error` is `reason=siteverify_internal_error` at warning level. No line has the token, secret, `Origin` or client address. There was no Turnstile log before.
  - **One retry (TS-2):** a network failure, timeout or `5xx` from Siteverify is retried once with the same idempotency key, which Cloudflare answers with the first result; a `5xx` answer's body is released first. Before, one lost answer was `503` and the token was gone. Worst case is two timeouts (6 seconds with the default `TURNSTILE_TIMEOUT_MS`).
  - **Staff password step (TS-6):** `POST /auth/login` accepted any sign-in action but `patient-login`, so an `admin-login` token from the platform sign-in page was verified for the staff password step on `www`, which allows both actions. It now takes only the six workforce actions and answers any other `400 INVALID_LOGIN_ACTION` before Cloudflare is called.
  - **Missing token (TS-8):** the demo request, organization application, organization completion and patient and provider enrollment starts required the token in their DTO, so a missing token was `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` there and `400 TURNSTILE_REQUIRED` on the sign-in and recovery routes. All eleven routes now answer `400 TURNSTILE_REQUIRED`.
  - **Startup refusals (TS-9, TS-10):** the Turnstile refusals (mode `required`, a secret, Cloudflare Siteverify) applied only under `NODE_ENV=production`. They now also apply to any task with `HID_DEPLOYMENT_ENV=production`, whatever its `NODE_ENV`. A local or test run of the staging profile may still disable Turnstile. Such a task also refuses to start when a `CORS_ORIGINS` host has no Turnstile action in its deployment profile, since no check from that host could pass. A wrong `RootDomainName` now stops Identity at deployment instead of refusing every sign-in at runtime.
  - **Tests (TS-12):** `turnstile-routes.spec.ts` sends each of the eleven routes through the real validation pipe, problem filter and Turnstile service against a Siteverify stand-in that spends a token on first use. Every refusal (missing, empty, invalid, expired, reused, other action, other host, outage) stops before the service behind the route. The patient and provider enrollment runtime verifiers' Siteverify stubs now spend tokens too, and the patient verifier checks that a token that signed in cannot sign in again.
  - **Development profile:** the `development` regional profile sets `HID_DEPLOYMENT_ENV=production` with `development.*` hosts, which the allowlist does not name. No Turnstile check could pass there before; Identity now refuses to start there. Not a launch environment; it needs its own deployment profile before Identity runs there with Turnstile.
  - **Not changed:** the host and action allowlist (moved unchanged to `turnstile-actions.ts`), every success answer, the routes without Turnstile, and the Google routes' `patient-login` action (TS-1: with Google sign-in on, `/enroll` needs a `patient-login` token for the Google exchange and a `patient-enrollment` token for the start; Google is off at launch, P19, so this stays a documented follow-up).

- **Self-reported enrollment profile (D-Eminence/hid-system#82, migration 0092; Identity and database)**. ADR-061; `PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md` N1, note of 2026-10-14; `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, "A NIN record without a date of birth or gender"; `QOREID_VERIFICATION_CONTRACT.md`; `DATABASE.md`, "Self-reported enrollment profile (0092)":
  - **Defect:** a verified QoreID NIN record without a date of birth or gender could not enroll at all. The adapter required `nin.birthdate` and `nin.gender` and answered `502 QOREID_PROVIDER_RESPONSE_INVALID`; the service required both; and the activation command (0085) required both in the profile JSON, which must hash to the enrollment's `profile_sha256`, so Identity could not add them. The product owner's described flow (2026-10-14) expects QoreID to return only the name, the NIN and its reference.
  - **Adapter:** only the NIN (equal to the one submitted), first name and last name are required. A birth date or gender that is absent, null or empty is left out of the binding and the stored profile; one that is present but does not parse still fails closed. The signed-in NIN binding (`QoreIdNinBinding`) is unchanged.
  - **Progress:** the start answer (new and resumed), `GET .../current` before activation and the contact proof answer carry `requiredProfileFields` (`dateOfBirth`, `gender`, in that order; `[]` for a complete record), read from the stored profile. Never on `active`; never a profile value. From 0093 (ADR-062) no answer before the holder proof carries it, the start answer included: it comes first on the online holder proof answer (`POST .../holder/verify`), and after an in-person check (0094) on `GET .../current` at `verify_contact`, since the staff answer never carries it.
  - **Activation:** `POST .../activate` takes optional `dateOfBirth` (`YYYY-MM-DD`) and `gender`. After the stage, contact-age and budget checks and before argon2id, a first activation answers `400 ENROLLMENT_PROFILE_FIELD_VERIFIED` for a field the record gives, `400 ENROLLMENT_PROFILE_REQUIRED` for a missing one and `400 DATE_OF_BIRTH_INVALID` for a date that is not a real date from 1900-01-01 to today. Each refusal counts toward the activation budget and creates nothing. A replay ignores well-formed fields; a malformed one is still `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`.
  - **Database (0092):** the activation command is dropped and created with `requested_date_of_birth date` and `requested_gender text`, both default null (a 7-argument call still works). It refuses (`23514 PATIENT_IDENTITY_INVALID`) an entered value where the profile has one and a missing value where it has none, and stores the profile's or the entered value on the patient. The authoritative profile stays what QoreID returned; the activation audit event gains `selfReportedFields` (names only). Identity answers a database refusal its own checks missed `409 ENROLLMENT_STAGE_INVALID`.
  - **Grants:** dropping the function drops its grants; the bootstrap grants the new signature (§5 step 31). Between 0092 and the bootstrap no build can activate (`403 ENROLLMENT_DENIED`).
  - **Not changed:** the production refusal of `QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED` (Identity and `infra/aws`), Turnstile, rate limits, the QoreID lookup quota, the contact step, RLS and every other audit event. Production NIN-only enrollment stays off until the owner lifts the refusal (N1).
  - **Open follow-up:** the signed-in progressive NIN binding (Portal "Verify NIN", `QoreIdNinBinding`) still requires a registry date of birth. When QoreID returns none it answers `not_verified` if QoreID marks the date as not matching, otherwise `502 QOREID_PROVIDER_RESPONSE_INVALID`. The activation command still records `NIN_VERIFIED` assurance (0085), which N1 says contact-only enrollment should not (note of 2026-10-14).

- **NIN holder proof (D-Eminence/hid-system#84, migration 0093; database, Identity, notification-api and infrastructure)**. ADR-062; `PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md` N1, update of 2026-10-15; `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, "The NIN holder step"; `QOREID_VERIFICATION_CONTRACT.md`; `DATABASE.md`, "NIN holder proof (0093)":
  - **Defect:** a QoreID lookup proves only that a NIN exists, yet it and a contact of the caller's choice were enough to create a Health ID: anyone who knew a NIN could. Start also answered `409 NIN_ALREADY_REGISTERED` to anyone, which told a caller whether a NIN had a Health ID. The signed-in "Verify NIN" binds a NIN on a name and date-of-birth match, with no holder proof.
  - **Database (0093):** a new first state `verify_holder`; the holder columns, written only by `identity.record_public_patient_holder_proof`; the holder events table and the send, failure and distinct-NIN budgets (a send counts the attempts left on the NIN's open codes with its wrong codes); the activation command requires a holder proof; the prune locks expired enrollments before their codes, as the proof does. The Identity runtime gets column-level `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on the enrollment table, without the holder columns.
  - **Identity:** start answers `verify_holder` with `online` and `facility` availability and never `NIN_ALREADY_REGISTERED`; `POST .../holder` sends a six-digit code by SMS to the record's phone; `POST .../holder/verify` checks it, and only then answers whether the NIN has a Health ID (`409`, enrollment deleted, cookie expired). Availability answers `{ open, online, facility }`. With no holder route open, start answers `503 PATIENT_REGISTRATION_NOT_OPEN` before the QoreID gate. A QoreID `502`, `503` or `504` at start shows one fixed text. The signed-in binding answers `503 QOREID_PATIENT_NIN_BINDING_DISABLED` while its flag is off. Both new `503` answers keep their fixed patient text.
  - **notification-api:** the purpose `NIN_HOLDER_VERIFY`, SMS only (other channels `400`), never through the WhatsApp template, with its own wording that names no NIN, person, Health ID or link.
  - **Configuration (Identity and `infra/aws`, all off):** `NIN_HOLDER_SMS_ENABLED`, `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED`, `QOREID_PATIENT_SELF_NIN_BINDING_ENABLED`, and the four holder budgets. NIN-only enrollment needs a holder route; production refused the online route until 0094 replaced that rule (next entry) and refuses the signed-in binding; the regional stack refuses the online route on staging. These rules replace the blanket production refusal of NIN-only enrollment. With 0093 alone no deployed environment could open public patient enrollment.
  - **Not changed:** Turnstile, Origin, rate limits, the QoreID lookup quota, the contact step after the holder proof, RLS, sign-in and recovery. The facility route followed in 0094 (next entry).

- **Facility-assisted registration (D-Eminence/hid-system#85, migration 0094; database, Identity and infrastructure)**. ADR-063; `PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md` N2(d), revision of 2026-10-15; `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, "Facility-assisted registration"; `DATABASE.md`, "Facility-assisted registration (0094)":
  - **Gap:** after 0093 a patient without a usable registry phone, or whose record shows an age under 18, has no holder route, and someone who knows a NIN can close its online route. Production therefore refused the online route.
  - **Database (0094):** the designation of participating facilities and its events, set only by a platform command (step-up, reason, expected version, idempotency key); the public facility list; the permission `identity.patient.assisted_registration` on doctor, clinician and nurse; `auth.staff_session_is_mfa` and the attested-grant check; the in-person checks table, written only by the staff command, which charges 20 checks per staff member and 100 per facility a day and writes a staff audit row; the desk-code salt and issue count; the holder proof and activation accept `facility_in_person` for a fresh matched check by a staff member still authorized at a participating facility.
  - **Identity:** `GET .../participating-facilities` (public, no audit, 5 minutes), `POST .../facility-code` (the desk code, shown again only to the cookie holder), `POST /identity/assisted-verifications` (staff, MFA, the permission, `503` while off before the body, blind compare, `404`, `422` per field, `403`, `409`, `200`), `GET` and `POST /admin/facilities/:id/assisted-registration`. Availability's `facility` is real.
  - **Configuration (Identity and `infra/aws`):** `ASSISTED_VERIFICATION_STAFF_DAILY_MAX` (20) and `ASSISTED_VERIFICATION_FACILITY_DAILY_MAX` (100, not below the staff budget). Production runs the online route only with `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED` on; this replaces 0093's refusal. Every flag stays off; no facility is designated.
  - **Not changed:** the online route, its budgets, the contact step, activation for a phone proof, sign-in, recovery, Turnstile, Origin, CSRF and rate limits.

- **Provider enrollment answers (D-Eminence/hid-system#81, no migration; Identity only)**. Two provider self-signup blockers: an email conflict at activation and a sparse CAC record. A start answer that wrongly claimed the email had an account is corrected too. `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, "Provider enrollment answers a client should handle"; `QOREID_VERIFICATION_CONTRACT.md`, "Provider self-enrollment and a sparse record":
  - **Taken administrator email:** activation refuses an administrator email that already belongs to any account or staff record (0061, a patient account included) with SQLSTATE `23505` and the message "Administrator email already belongs to an account". Identity answered it `503 ORGANIZATION_COMPLETION_UNAVAILABLE` ("temporarily unavailable"), after the CAC lookup, the email code and the password. It now answers `409 PROVIDER_EMAIL_ALREADY_REGISTERED` ("The administrator email is already associated with an HID account") and creates nothing. Only that SQLSTATE with that exact message is mapped; any other unique violation keeps `503`. The governed completion steps, which share the general mapping, are unchanged. The answer comes only after the emailed code proved the mailbox; start still does not check accounts, so no one can test whether an email has an account.
  - **Unique violation at start:** start mapped every unique violation to `409 PROVIDER_EMAIL_ALREADY_REGISTERED`, although it makes no account check. Its only unique violation is a governed organization application for the same CAC and product committing between start's check and its insert (the two submits take different locks). It now answers `409 PROVIDER_ENROLLMENT_REQUIRES_REVIEW`, as a retry does. So `PROVIDER_EMAIL_ALREADY_REGISTERED` comes only from activation.
  - **Sparse CAC record:** when QoreID verifies the CAC but returns too little of its legal profile (the sandbox shape for RC and BN numbers), start answered `502 QOREID_PROVIDER_RESPONSE_INVALID`, which the browser saw as "The service could not complete the request." It now answers `409 PROVIDER_CAC_PROFILE_INCOMPLETE`: "We could not register your organization automatically from its CAC record. The record is missing details we need or does not show the company as active. Contact Health ID support." A complete record whose company is not active gets the same answer, so the detail is written to be true for both. A `4xx` detail reaches the browser unchanged (Identity's problem filter, the gateway and the Worker). The governed organization application can take a sparse record, but no launch page uses it, so the detail does not point to it. Nothing new is recorded, no email code is sent, and the CAC lookup quota is unchanged. A malformed verified answer keeps `502`.
  - **Not changed:** authentication, Origin and Turnstile checks, rate limits, the CAC lookup quota, the accepted products and organization types, D14 role rules, who may self-activate, every success answer.

- **Admin integration status for NIN-only enrollment (launch/signup-alignment, no migration; Identity only)**. `PROVIDER_INTEGRATION_MANAGEMENT.md`; ADR-062, ADR-063:
  - **Defect:** `GET /api/v1/admin/integrations` reported the QoreID gate `patient_nin_enrollment` as `closed` unless `NIN_HOLDER_SMS_ENABLED` was on. It ignored the facility route, so a production that opens sign-up only through a designated facility (P25) looked closed to operators. In production it also showed `open` with both flags on and no designation, while Identity closes both routes and answers every start `503 PATIENT_REGISTRATION_NOT_OPEN`. `activeCapabilities` listed `patient_nin` whenever `QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED` was on, whether or not a route was open, and left it out while only the signed-in binding used it.
  - **Fix:** the gate is `open` only while the QoreID gate and `QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED` are on, the enrollment keys are set and at least one holder route is open: the online route under the production rule, or the facility route with a participating facility. These are the keys and routes start and availability read; one module, `identity/ownership-routes.ts`, now holds both rules for the enrollment service and the status. The keys are `NIN_LOOKUP_HMAC_KEY_B64` and `NIN_ENCRYPTION_KEY_B64`, and `OTP_HMAC_KEY_B64` where `NODE_ENV` is `production`; without them a start answers `503 ENROLLMENT_CONFIGURATION_UNAVAILABLE`, and the gate shows `closed` with "The enrollment keys are not configured." The status says only whether the keys are set, never a value. It reads the facility list as the enrollment service does, in a system transaction (the 0094 function answers only system context; the grant exists), and only after the permission-checked platform read. Its detail names the open routes. If the read fails, the gate shows `closed` with "The holder routes could not be read.", and list, get, pause and enable keep working. `patient_nin` is listed while the signed-in binding gate or the enrollment gate is open.
  - **Not changed:** the response shape and the other two gates, start, availability, every refusal and every security check. No migration, grant, module wiring or Health-id change.

- **Novu receives the verified login email (D-Eminence/hid-system#90, migration 0095; database, notification worker and infrastructure)**. Merged, not deployed. ADR-064; owner decision 2 of 2026-10-16; `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, "Notification worker to Novu"; `DATABASE.md`, "Notification verified recipient (0095)". Ports the Novu subscriber sync of draft D-Eminence/hid-system#8 onto main without merging it:
  - **Database (0095):** `integration.notification_recipient_for_claim(event, claim token, patient)`, owned by `hid_event_delivery_commands` and executable by `hid_notification_worker` only, answers `email` with the verified login email or `withhold` for the event of the worker's live inbox claim; its private helper reads `identity.patients`, `auth.accounts` and `identity.patient_account_deletion_requests` as their owner. Eligible logins are those main lets sign in (0064's `identity.current_patient_account`): active, not suspended (`disabled_until`), no deletion due. No name, no table grant, no table, policy or role change.
  - **Worker:** `NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC=disabled|email`, default `disabled`, which runs main's worker code path (emails synced while it was on stay at Novu: §9). With `email`: an eligible patient's subscriber is upserted with exactly `{subscriberId, email}` and the lookup is asked again under the same claim; if it answers the same address, main's trigger goes whatever the upsert answered, otherwise the email is cleared again (an upsert can land at Novu after a concurrent event's clear). Any other patient (opted out, login not active, suspended, due for deletion or deleted, patient not active, phone-only, unbound, or a failed lookup) has the Novu email cleared first, and main's trigger follows a confirmed clear, otherwise the trigger that clears the email itself. Both subscriber calls are keyed by the attempt's claim token, because Novu replays a seen key's cached answer for 24 hours. No subscriber answer decides whether the in-app notice goes, delays it or fails the event. PHI-free disclosure rows and one `notification.worker.outcome` line per event attempt. A missing or non-executable lookup stops the worker at startup.
  - **Configuration (worker and `infra/aws`, all off):** the worker refuses `email` for a production deployment (`HID_DEPLOYMENT_ENV`, new, default `production`), without the live orchestrator, and under `NODE_ENV=production` without an approved Novu region origin. The synth input `HID_NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC_ENABLED` (default `false`) may be `true` only in staging with the worker on; the worker task gets `NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC` and `HID_DEPLOYMENT_ENV`.
  - **Not changed:** every HID route, main's trigger body and key, the inbox claim, completion and failure semantics, the runtime-role guard, RLS and every grant outside the two functions. The worker stays off in production (D17). Deleting the Novu subscriber when a login is deleted is a follow-up, not built; it is required before the production refusal is lifted (ADR-064).
- **Patient record release (owner decision 4 of 2026-10-16, migration 0096; database, EHR and infrastructure)**. ADR-065; `PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md` D7, D8; `INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, "Record summaries" and "Patient document download"; `DATABASE.md`, "Patient record release (0096)":
  - **Gap:** patients saw only completed encounters and their notes. The owner approved releasing diagnoses, prescriptions, Lab results, vitals and documents.
  - **Database (0096):** five PUBLIC patient-release policies on `ehr.vitals`, `ehr.diagnoses`, `ehr.prescriptions`, `ehr.documents` and `ehr.document_scan_events`, gated by the session-bound `ehr.patient_self_context`, a completed encounter matched on patient and facility, and the row's category in the transaction-local `app.patient_release`, which only `PatientRecordRelease` sets (to the categories whose flag is on): final, never corrected vitals; confirmed diagnoses; active, on hold and completed prescriptions; uploaded PHI documents attached to an encounter. The view `ehr.patient_released_lab_results` returns, when the transaction names `labResults`, each Lab result's latest released, not invalidated revision of an order still active, on hold or completed, of a completed encounter; it is owned by the technical role `hid_lab_release_reader` and readable by the EHR runtime and the test role only, so lab-api, the Lab runtimes and `ehr.patient_self_context`'s grants are unchanged. Indexes on `lab.result_releases`, `ehr.vitals`, `ehr.diagnoses` and `ehr.prescriptions` in each section's read order. Row-level security restricts rows, not columns (as for encounters and notes): EHR's `SELECT` lists withhold the diagnosis notes, storage locations and staff columns.
  - **EHR:** `PatientRecordRelease`, the one release set (later record features reuse it); the summary adds each released category, `labResults` only when released, and audits counts only; `503 PATIENT_RECORDS_UNAVAILABLE` if the database refuses a released read or it passes the statement timeout. Every patient-self transaction starts with no category released to the database, and the summary and the download name the configured ones first. `GET /api/v1/ehr/me/documents/:documentId/download` (patient only, facility-optional): one `404 DOCUMENT_NOT_AVAILABLE` for every refusal, the audit row before the URL, the exact version's size, SHA-256 and encryption checked (`503 DOCUMENT_INTEGRITY_UNAVAILABLE`), a 60 s attachment URL with `Cache-Control: private, no-store`.
  - **Configuration (EHR and `infra/aws`, all off):** `EHR_PATIENT_RELEASE_VITALS`, `_DIAGNOSES`, `_PRESCRIPTIONS`, `_LAB_RESULTS` and `_DOCUMENTS` (synth inputs `HID_EHR_PATIENT_RELEASE_*`). The EHR production profile refuses every one of them (Lab results while Lab is off, D17; the others until the clinical owner signs each off, P36), and `environmentConfig`, the regional stack and `verify-synth` refuse every input outside staging (the AWS development stack runs ehr-api in the production profile); EHR refuses documents without S3 storage.
  - **Not changed:** the patient-self binding, Identity's patient-self authorization (EHR only), delegated cookie reads, the staff summaries and the emergency summary, the staff document download, consent, purpose, MFA, Turnstile and rate limits; no Lab role gains a privilege. With every flag off the patient summary is the same as before.

## 3. Compatibility

| Identity build | Stage 3 console (Health-id `d5ce01c`) | Stage 4B console (`a180cea`) and later |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Before Stage 2A (no migration 0069; no platform MFA sign-in) | Not usable: platform sign-in needs Stage 2A. | Not usable for the same reason. Admin sign-in fails until Stage 2A Identity is deployed. |
| Stage 2A to Stage 3 (0069–0071) | Supported (the earlier release) | Works with less. Approvals list one capped page and the target audit filter returns `400`. Authenticator enrolment shows "Not reported" and the session-end notice is generic. See the Health-id Stage 4B release note. |
| Stage 4A (`deb6fba`) and later, including Stage 5 | **Not supported.** The console does not handle `STEP_UP_EXPIRED`, so every high-risk action is refused once the first step-up is five minutes old. | Supported, and verified end to end (§8). |

Stage 5 changes no request or response contract the console uses, so Stage 4B
and Stage 5 consoles both work with Stage 4A and Stage 5 Identity.

**Rule:** never run Identity at Stage 4A or later with a console older than Stage 4B.

**OCR (Stage 6):**
- The Stage 6 OCR API needs no schema change: its new audit events use the existing `audit.events` contract. It can be deployed before or after 0075.
- The outbox insert policy takes effect for every OCR API build as soon as 0075 is applied.
- A Stage 6 worker that runs before 0075 and the bootstrap cannot execute `ocr.worker_queue_metrics()`. It logs `ocr.queue.metrics_unavailable` and claims jobs exactly as earlier workers do. Earlier workers keep reading `ocr.jobs` directly after 0075 and keep logging that warning.

**Stage 7:**
- The Stage 7 Identity calls `auth.lock_account_for_mfa(uuid)`, so it must not start before 0076 and the role bootstrap: without them every platform MFA transaction (sign-in, enrolment, step-up, regeneration) fails with a database error (`5xx`). Earlier Identity builds do not call it. Stage 7 changes no request or response contract.
- 0076 works with every OCR API build: each writes the session's own account and membership, so its confirmations and validations pass when the compared values are present. Under a schema owner that cannot read the source document (P8), every confirmation is now refused by the database (`23514`), which the OCR API answers with `500`, instead of being accepted for any patient.

**Stage 8:**
- 0077 needs no service change and works with every build. The OCR worker always sends its claim token and the OCR API always names the session's member, so their writes pass as before. A worker's lease renewals now succeed. A worker that fails a job after its lease expired is now refused (`55000`) and logs `ocr.job.failure_persistence_failed`; the next claim's lease recovery takes the job over, as it already did for completions.
- The Lab, Pharmacy and Outreach APIs always set the full session, so their writes pass as before. A maintenance write to those tables from a superuser or migration session without a staff context is now refused; set a real staff context, or change the data under change control.
- The Outreach API can write only after the bootstrap's new grant (§5). Earlier, every Outreach write failed (`5xx`).

**Stage 9:**
- No request or response shape changes. One value changes with the OCR publication retry fix: a publication failure with a `5xx` status (an owning domain's `5xx`, or the code OCR's integration gives a call that got no answer) now carries `failureCode` `OWNING_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE`, in the publication API and in the `OcrPublicationFailed` event, where it carried that code; the code moves to `failureSummary` and the audit event (§9). The event payload keeps its keys, and `retryable` keeps its meaning. The commands of §4.2 answer their designed statuses (`201`, replay, `403`, `404`, `409`, `412`) where every call failed with `500`; the loser of a race now gets a replay or `409`/`412` instead of `500`.
- The Stage 9 Pharmacy, Lab, OCR and Outreach APIs work on 0077 and on 0078; so do earlier builds, which keep failing at their locks. Two commands also need 0078: Lab acceptance of an EHR order needs the repaired trigger, and the Identity NIN registration reviews need the self-NIN policy gone. Without it both still fail with `500`, as before.
- 0078 needs no grant change and changes nothing for earlier builds. The Stage 9 role assertions (`runtime-roles.integration.sql`, run by `db:bootstrap` and `db:verify-roles`) require the self-NIN policy to be gone, so the Stage 9 bootstrap and role verification run after 0078 (§5 step 31) and fail on a database without it: before it, every Identity runtime insert of a patient identifier already failed (`42501`), and Lab acceptance never succeeded. Patient self-NIN binding (`bind_my_verified_nin`) is unaffected: it never used the dropped policy.
- Campaign-linked Outreach registration now requires `outreach.campaign.write` as well as `outreach.registration.write` (the campaign lock applies the campaign `UPDATE` policy). Every shipped role holds both. A custom role with only the registration permission gets `403 OUTREACH_CAMPAIGN_ACCESS_DENIED` for a campaign-linked registration.
- Lab orders created on the EHR while Lab acceptance was failing have no Lab work item (§10); with D-Eminence/hid-system#56 a clinician can send each one again (§2).

**Pharmacy source prescription (D-Eminence/hid-system#48, 0080):**
- The EHR API keeps its place in §6 and may be rolled back: 0080 does not compare the prescription time or dates, so earlier EHR builds, which send the time at the millisecond, keep handing off. An earlier EHR build in a process east of UTC sends start and end dates a day early, which Pharmacy stores as it did before 0080; no service image or deployment sets `TZ`, so the processes run in UTC. The new EHR build works on 0079 and with earlier Pharmacy builds, which accept the exact texts (and store `prescribed_at` at the microsecond instead of the millisecond).
- Deploy the Pharmacy API with the `409` mapping before or with 0080. An earlier Pharmacy API on 0080 answers the same refusals with `500`; nothing is written either way. The new Pharmacy API on 0079 never meets these refusals.
- Pharmacy's idempotency digest covers the request text, so an acceptance sent by an earlier EHR build and sent again after the EHR upgrade, with the same key or for the same prescription, gets `409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` (the EHR passes it through); Pharmacy still has its work item. The reverse holds during a rolling EHR deploy or after an EHR rollback.
- When 0080 is applied, existing accepted work items without a dispensing whose prescription changed after acceptance (cancelled, entered in error, on hold, completed, or edited to a later version) become undispensable at once and answer `409 PHARMACY_PRESCRIPTION_NOT_DISPENSABLE`; this includes staging S4g items (§10 lists them). Work items accepted from an earlier EHR build keep dispensing: neither guard compares the prescription time or dates.
- Pharmacy can now delay an EHR write: a dispense or acceptance holds `FOR SHARE` on the prescription until it commits. A Pharmacy transaction that stalls after the guard (Pharmacy's 15 s `statement_timeout` bounds a statement, not an idle transaction) makes a prescriber's cancel or hold of that prescription wait until the EHR's 15 s `statement_timeout`, and the EHR command then fails with `500`.
- Health-id needs no change: its dispense form shows the problem detail of a `409`. It still shows the dispense form for work that can no longer be dispensed.

**Delegated cookie reads (D-Eminence/hid-system#38):**
- No schema, grant or browser contract change; Health-id needs no change.
- Same-origin cookie reads through a service work only once both that service and Identity run #38. Either side alone behaves exactly as before: an earlier Identity ignores `X-HID-Original-Method`, and an earlier service does not send it, so the read is still refused `403 ORIGIN_DENIED`. OCR's cookie reads (a job, find-by-document, extractions, validations and publications) also need EHR with #38, because OCR reads each job's source document through EHR's `GET /ehr/internal/ocr/documents/:id/source`, which EHR authorizes with the forwarded cookie. Deploy in any order; OCR reads work once OCR, EHR and Identity all run #38.
- Writes, bearer requests and Outreach behave the same with any combination of builds.

**EHR clinical list paging:**
- No schema, grant or response-shape change.
- The new EHR build refuses every cursor an earlier build issued, and an earlier build refuses the new cursors, both with `400 INVALID_CURSOR`. A browser that holds a cursor across the deploy, or meets both builds during a rolling deploy, gets that refusal on its next paged request.
- Health-id's clinical record (`apps/patient-web/src/staff/routes/Clinical.tsx` on Health-id `main`) then reloads the first page of that list and says that it restarted; it needs no change. The in-repo EHR app (`apps/ehr`) requests only first pages (`limit=50`, no cursor).

**EHR to Lab order time:**
- No schema, grant or response change; Lab is unchanged.
- An order that Lab accepted from an earlier EHR build (possible only when its stored microseconds were a whole millisecond) and that a client replays with the same `Idempotency-Key` at any time after the upgrade, while the order is still active, gets `409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`, because Lab's digest covers the `requestedAt` text and EHR's replay does not check the key's `expires_at` (§10). Lab still has its work item.
- During a rolling deploy, or after a rollback, the reverse also happens: an order Lab accepted from the new build and replayed through an earlier build gets `409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`.
- Active EHR orders that Lab refused before the upgrade still have no Lab work item; reconcile them with the §10 query.
- With D-Eminence/hid-system#56, the EHR create reports Lab's `409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` as `409 LAB_HANDOFF_REFUSED`, naming it.

**EHR to Lab handoff (D-Eminence/hid-system#56):**
- No schema or grant change.
- The EHR create and update responses gain `labHandoff`, and the update `labWorkItem`. A create whose handoff fails answers `409 LAB_HANDOFF_REFUSED` or `502 LAB_HANDOFF_UNCONFIRMED` instead of Lab's own status and code. Health-id `main` shows any failed create as an error, as before, with the problem detail. It offers lab-request status changes only for a draft (cancel, mark entered in error), which never reach Lab, has no control for the new route and does not read `labHandoff`. Its notes that a draft cannot be sent to Lab later and that activation never reaches Lab (`Clinical.tsx`) are now out of date.
- Lab answers `4xx` where it answered `500` only for EHR-order acceptance, whose only caller is the EHR (§2). Every other Lab command keeps answering a database refusal `500`. OCR treats a Lab `4xx` as a terminal refusal under Lab's code and a Lab `5xx` as the retryable `OWNING_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE`, so an OCR import that Lab's database refuses (a guard, a unique key, row-level security) is still stored as retryable and retried up to `max_attempts`, as before; no reviewed publication ends as terminal because of this change.
- Deploy Lab before EHR. With an earlier Lab, the new EHR reports Lab's `500` for another version of an accepted order as `failed` (update) or `502 LAB_HANDOFF_UNCONFIRMED` (create replay) instead of `already_accepted`, and Lab's `400` for a long code system, name or clinical information, or for an id that is not a version 4 UUID, as `409 LAB_HANDOFF_REFUSED`. An earlier EHR passes the new Lab `409` and `403` answers through as Lab's codes.

**Record summaries (D-Eminence/hid-system#52):**
- No schema, grant or request change. Response fields are added and none removed. Values that change: `limit` is 50 (was 100) on both summaries; emergency `documents` lists only available documents, with `status` `available` (was `uploaded`); and for a reader whose role lacks a category's read permission, that emergency category is empty and named in `withheld`, and `limits` and `truncated` name only the sections read. Every seeded role that can call the route (doctor, clinician and nurse) holds all seven reads.
- Health-id `main` (`1b17b5c`) keeps working unchanged. The patient portal reads the seven arrays and `limit` (`apps/patient-web/src/api.ts`, `routes/Portal.tsx`); it still shows "No vitals are available." and the like for each withheld category until it reads `withheld` (Health-id follow-up). The provider portal's emergency summary reads only `encounters`, `notes` and `limit` (`staff/api.ts`, `staff/routes/Clinical.tsx`); its "most recent records" notice now fires at 50, the real cap. The in-repo care portal (`apps/web`) reads encounters, notes and `limit`, and its "Up to {limit} of each" is now right.
- Deploy in any order; nothing else depends on it.

**Governed access:**
- The provider portal of D-Eminence/Health-id#19 works with any Identity build that serves the access-request list. It shows outcomes and offers the outcome filters only when Identity reports `effectiveStatus`. Otherwise it shows the request status, as before.
- An Identity build with the 0074 mapping selects the new columns by name, so it fails on the list (`5xx`) until 0074 is applied.
- Earlier Identity builds keep working after 0074 and the bootstrap. They ignore the new columns, and their request validation refuses the `active` and `closed` filters (`400`). The portal offers those filters only to an Identity that reports outcomes.

**Recovery start fix (D-Eminence/hid-system#55):**
- The Identity build of this change reads and writes `auth.otp_start_cooldowns`, so it must not start before 0081 and the bootstrap's grant (§5, §6): without the table every recovery start fails with `42P01`, without the grant with `42501`, both answered `500`. Earlier Identity builds ignore the table, so 0081 and the bootstrap can go first.
- No request or response shape changes; Health-id needs no change. Its recovery page keeps the `challengeId` of its latest start (`apps/patient-web/src/routes/Recovery.tsx` on Health-id `main`), and a retry with the same identifier inside the cooldown gets the same id back.
- During a rolling deploy, an earlier Identity answers a repeat start from its own rule (the live id for a real account), so the cooldown oracle stays open until every instance runs this change. Earlier builds neither read nor count the code budget.

**Sign-out and client kind (D-Eminence/hid-system#39, if released):**
- It needs no migration or grant beyond what Stage 5B already needs (0073 and the bootstrap's `auth.lock_account_sessions` grant): the new lookups read `auth.sessions` and `auth.accounts`, which the Identity runtime already reads.
- Health-id `main` sends `X-HID-Client` since `22e8664` (D-Eminence/Health-id#40): its cookie-mode client (`packages/http/src/client.ts`) names the portal, `patient-web` or `staff-web`, on `POST /auth/refresh` and `/auth/logout` only, and a caller cannot change it. Health-id builds before it sent none, so for them the kind check is inert. Its portals already handle the idle `401`: the patient portal refreshes and signs out again only for the same patient (`apps/patient-web/src/session.tsx`, `signOut`), and the provider portal restores its session and retries (`apps/patient-web/src/staff/api.ts`, `signOutStaffSession`). With #39 the first sign-out ends the sign-in by refresh cookie, without that refresh. With the header it ends a sign-in of the portal's own kind, another account's included (the patient portal's same-patient check does not apply on that path), or one refresh could not renew now; the other portal's renewable sign-in gets `403 SESSION_CLIENT_MISMATCH`, which the patient portal treats as its own session already gone and the provider portal as the end of its session (refresh) or a completed sign-out (Health-id `docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`). Without the header (an earlier Health-id build) it ends whichever staff or patient sign-in the refresh cookie holds. `packages/identity-browser-client` sends none.
- A portal that sends `X-HID-Client` works with earlier Identity builds on the same origin, which ignore the header. Their CORS allow-list lacks it, so a cross-origin request carrying it would fail its preflight.

**Refusals on session routes audited as session events:**
- No schema, grant or response-shape change. A staff refusal on a session route now answers its own `400`, `401` or `403` instead of `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`, and is recorded.
- Refusals raised after the `SecurityGuard` (by validation or the handler) on these six routes, patient ones and anonymous sign-outs included, are written as `auth.logout`, `auth.facility.select`, `auth.session.read`, `auth.service-session.read`, `auth.service-session.validate` and `auth.google.link` on resource type `session`, instead of `api.<method>.<route>` on `http-request`. Anything that filters audit rows by the old action or resource type must look for the new ones. The guard's own refusals (for example an anonymous `GET /auth/session`, or a patient's `POST /auth/facility`, refused as `PATIENT_SCOPE_DENIED`) keep their rows.
- Health-id needs no change: it never treats a `503` as a refusal, and it already handles `403 SESSION_CLIENT_MISMATCH` and `403 FACILITY_ACCESS_DENIED`.

**Enrollment contact-code budget (D-Eminence/hid-system#61):**
- No schema, grant or response-shape change; the Identity runtime already reads and writes `auth.otp_rate_limits`, whose `recipient` scope 0028 allows. Deploy in any order.
- A recipient blocked by the budget gets `422 OTP_INVALID` for a correct code and `429 ENROLLMENT_RATE_LIMITED` on the contact step for up to 15 minutes by default. Health-id shows the problem message for both and needs no change. An applicant who mistypes the code five times within 15 minutes, across resends, must wait.
- During a rolling deploy an earlier build neither counts nor checks the budget, so guesses through it are limited only per challenge.

**EHR and OCR guard refusals before a facility is bound (D-Eminence/hid-system#46):**
- No schema, grant, request or response-shape change. EHR and OCR can each be deployed alone.
- Guard refusals for staff without a bound facility are now `auth.authorization.denied` rows on `authentication`. They answer their own status and code instead of `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`: `400 FACILITY_REQUIRED`, `403 FACILITY_ACCESS_DENIED`, and in EHR `423 PLATFORM_PORTAL_DISABLED`, `503 PLATFORM_MAINTENANCE` and `503 PLATFORM_CONTROL_UNAVAILABLE`. The last now also answers a database fault in EHR's control check (a timeout, a lost connection, a missing grant). A client that treated these refusals as an outage now sees the refusal itself.
- For a patient, a database fault in EHR's control check answered `401 AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` and now answers `503 PLATFORM_CONTROL_UNAVAILABLE`. A patient client that signed out on that `401` now sees an outage instead.
- Patient refusals without a bound facility also change action. In EHR, `403 PATIENT_SCOPE_DENIED` and the runtime-control refusals (`423 PLATFORM_PORTAL_DISABLED` when `patient_portal_enabled` is off, `503 PLATFORM_MAINTENANCE`, `503 PLATFORM_CONTROL_UNAVAILABLE`) were already recorded, as `security.authorization.denied` on `http-request`, and are now `auth.authorization.denied` on `authentication` with `actorType` `patient`. OCR's facility refusals of a patient, which answered `503`, are now recorded the same way. A query or alert that counts guard refusals by action must include both actions.

**Document upload completion (D-Eminence/hid-system#59):**
- No schema, grant or response-shape change; the EHR API can be deployed alone.
- Health-id completes only from the session that created the intent, so its uploads are unaffected.
- The in-repo EHR app (`apps/ehr/src/api/client.ts`, `uploadDocument`) is unaffected. It completes from a module-level map of started uploads, which its sign-out and session expiry clear. An account change the page does not see (another tab in the same browser signing in as someone else) leaves the map, but the page still sends the first session's CSRF token, so Identity refuses the completion with `403 CSRF_VALIDATION_FAILED` before the EHR reads the document, as before this change.
- An intent its uploader never completed stays `initiated`: never available or downloadable, and no other member can complete it now. Upload the document again.

**Outreach cookie reads (D-Eminence/hid-system#43, if released):**
- No schema, grant or browser contract change; Health-id needs no change for the reads (its campaign write actions get `400`, as on `main`; §2).
- Same-origin cookie reads from Outreach work only once both Outreach and Identity run the change. Either alone behaves as before and the reads are refused: an earlier Outreach refuses them itself (`403 CSRF_VALIDATION_FAILED`), and an earlier Identity refuses the forwarded cookie on the authorize call (`403 ORIGIN_DENIED`). Both refusals were reproduced locally (§8). Deploy in any order.
- Campaign reads need the change in Identity: an earlier Identity refuses `outreach.campaign.read`, so with it both campaign reads answer `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` for a bearer request, or for a cookie request that carries an allowed `Origin` and the session's CSRF token. A same-origin cookie read of campaigns (no `Origin`, no CSRF) is refused before that: `403 CSRF_VALIDATION_FAILED` from an earlier Outreach, `403 ORIGIN_DENIED` from an earlier Identity. The three campaign writes answer `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` with any combination of builds, because no Identity build authorizes `outreach.campaign.write` (§2, ADR-045).
- Registration-case writes and bearer registration-case requests behave the same with any combination of builds.

**Identity MFA key (D-Eminence/hid-system#49):**
- ECS resolves every JSON field a task definition references before it starts a task. Until the environment's Identity sensitive secret has the `mfaSecretKeyB64` field, no Identity task starts from the new task definition: on a first deployment every sign-in (patient, staff and platform) is down; on an update the deployment fails and the circuit breaker rolls the service back to the previous task definition. Add the field first (P3).
- The two variables do not constrain the Identity build: Stage 2A and later builds use them; earlier builds do not read them.
- No platform authenticator can have been enrolled in an AWS environment deployed from this stack, because enrolment needs the key. `aws-v1` is therefore the first version there: every factor and recovery code enrolled from now on records it, and changing the version or the key later invalidates them (P3).
- The staging preflight receipt keeps schema `hid.staging-external-preflight/v1`: `required_fields_present` for `/hid/staging/identity-sensitive` gains `contactLookupHmacKeyB64`, and that secret's entry gains `canonical_32_byte_base64`. Both are additive. A secret that held a hex or unpadded key and passed the old presence check now reports `false` there. Identity cannot have started with such a key, so nothing was ever keyed by it; replace it before deploying.

**Released Lab results (D-Eminence/hid-system#51):**
- No schema, grant or permission change; deploy the Lab API alone, in any order with the other services.
- A reader without `lab.execution.read` now gets fewer history revisions once a released result is corrected, and `currentVersion` and `status` refer to the latest released revision. Lab staff responses are unchanged apart from the additive `releasedAt`.
- Health-id `main` needs no change: its only Lab result view (`ResultHistory` in `apps/patient-web/src/staff/routes/Operations.tsx`) shows results listed by the specimen execution read, which needs `lab.execution.read`, so its readers keep the full history. Showing results to clinicians needs a Health-id change that calls the order route; an earlier Lab API answers that route `404`.

**Enrollment and session hardening (D-Eminence/hid-system#50):**
- No schema, grant or success-body change. The activation budget uses the existing rate table and statements (0051) that start and contact already use; the renewability lookups read staff tables the Identity runtime already reads and execute `identity.current_patient_account`, already granted (`runtime-grants.sql`).
- One new refusal: activation answers `429 ENROLLMENT_RATE_LIMITED` after its budget. Health-id's enrollment page (`apps/patient-web/src/routes/Enrollment.tsx` on Health-id `main`) shows the problem's message for any activation error, and its HTTP client never retries a `POST`.
- Each sign-in, refresh and session-clearing response carries one more `Set-Cookie` (the expired `hid_enrollment`). Health-id `main` redirects a signed-in browser away from the enrollment page and reads the HID from `/identity/me`, so it needs no change; see §2 for an enrollment interrupted before activation.
- The renewability change applies on deploy: Health-id `main` (`22e8664`) sends `X-HID-Client` on cookie refresh and sign-out (§3 #39 above). For a sign-in whose actor refresh can no longer resolve (a lapsed staff membership, staff record, organization or facility; a patient no longer the account's active patient), a refresh naming the other portal now gets `401` with every cookie cleared, and a sign-out naming it ends the sign-in (`204`), instead of `403 SESSION_CLIENT_MISMATCH` with the cookies kept. Both portals already treat those answers as signed out: Health-id's client hands a refused refresh (`401`) to the portal's sign-out handling, and a `204` completes a sign-out. Test it in staging (S4i). An earlier Identity build keeps answering `SESSION_CLIENT_MISMATCH` for those sign-ins.

**Purpose-of-use vocabulary (0082, D-Eminence/hid-system#58):**
- No request, response, schema-shape or grant change; 0082 works with every build. From its commit, direct-care access requests, approvals, grant closes, PINs and direct-care reads and writes work on a database where nobody inserted the code by hand.
- Where an operator already inserted codes, 0082 leaves each row as it is, an inactive `direct-care` included, which then keeps refusing every direct-care grant, request and PIN (P10 shows it).
- `emergency` and `healthcare-operations` behave as before 0082: refused unless an operator inserted them (§2).

**Governed workforce provisioning (0087, D-Eminence/hid-system#70):**
- The Identity build of this change calls `auth.facility_role_is_effective` on every staff sign-in, refresh and request. Run 0087 and the role bootstrap before it starts, or every staff sign-in and request fails with a database error (`5xx`). Earlier Identity builds keep working after 0087: they do not call it, and only an attested role can lapse.
- `auth.membership_has_permission` keeps its signature and grants; it refuses only roles whose attestation lapsed, which only this change creates.
- New routes only under `/admin/workforce`. Staff routes gain one refusal, `409 FACILITY_MEMBERSHIP_AMBIGUOUS`, which cannot occur once 0087 is applied.
- `actor.facilities[].roles`, `actor.roles`, `actor.role` and the access token's `roles` claim now list the membership's effective role assignments instead of its stored `membership_role` and `app_role` labels. The shape is unchanged, and for a membership whose labels match its live roles so are the values; they differ for a revoked or lapsed role (now absent) and for a legacy label the promotion normalized (now the role code). Nothing authorizes by these fields: permissions already came from the role assignments. Health-id shows them only as badges, with `Staff` when the list is empty.

**Purpose of use and OCR workload refusals (D-Eminence/hid-system#60):**
- No schema, grant or response-shape change. Identity, EHR, OCR, Lab and Pharmacy can each be deployed alone, in any order.
- A request with a missing or unknown `X-Purpose-Of-Use` now gets `400 PURPOSE_OF_USE_REQUIRED` instead of `500 INTERNAL_ERROR` from the routes listed in §2 (OCR's reads, Lab and Pharmacy, EHR's document and internal OCR routes, Identity's registration-case read), and no server error is logged for it. Health-id always sends the header. EHR and OCR send Lab and Pharmacy, and OCR sends EHR's internal OCR routes, the purpose they validated themselves, from the same three-value allow-list, so no internal call changes. This matters for OCR's source reads and import calls: a `400` from EHR, Lab or Pharmacy there would be stored as a terminal publication failure where a `500` was retryable, and that cannot happen between builds of this repository.
- On every OCR route that calls EHR, Lab or Pharmacy, a refusal of OCR's workload identity now answers `503 <SERVICE>_SERVICE_AUTH_UNAVAILABLE` instead of the owning service's `401` or `403`. A browser that signed in again on that `401` now sees an outage; Health-id does not refresh its session for a `503`.
- A publication that such a refusal fails is now `failed` with `failureCode` `OWNING_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` and `retryable: true` in its `OcrPublicationFailed` event, where it was terminal under the owner's code. No code value is new. Publications stored as terminal under a workload code before the upgrade stay terminal; nothing rewrites them, and they still answer `409 OCR_PUBLICATION_TERMINAL`.
- Identity's workload refusals, and every refusal of the user's own credential, facility or permission, answer and are stored as before (§2).

**EHR clinical safety and launch module set (D-Eminence/hid-system#64, 0084):**
- No grant change. Deploy the EHR API of this change immediately after 0084 (§6). An earlier EHR build on 0084 answers the database's refusals (`55000`) with `500` instead of `409` and nothing is written; it does not cascade a retraction, so the records of an encounter it marks entered in error stay as they are (P12 lists them), and it refuses `completed` to `entered_in_error` (`409 INVALID_ENCOUNTER_TRANSITION`). It still creates no clinical note (`500`, `42P08`).
- The new EHR build on a database without 0084 refuses the same writes itself, but the patient-self policies still release notes of encounters that are not completed to a patient session reading the tables directly (the services' own summary query does not).
- During a rolling deploy, an earlier EHR instance locks an order row before its encounter when it activates an order, the inverse of a new instance's retraction; such a pair can deadlock, and PostgreSQL cancels one command (`40P01`, answered `500`), which can be retried. Nothing is half applied.
- The withdrawn permissions take effect for every build at 0084's commit: Identity resolves permissions from `auth.role_permissions` on each authorization, so the routes of §2 answer `403 PERMISSION_DENIED` at once. Health-id `main` hides, without these permissions, the lab-request form and status actions, Send to Pharmacy, the accession form and the dispense form (`apps/patient-web/src/staff/routes/Clinical.tsx` and `Operations.tsx`); it has no action of its own for Lab's direct acceptance.
- Health-id `main` shows a `409 ENCOUNTER_NOT_OPEN` or `409 DIAGNOSIS_NOT_AMENDABLE` as an error with the problem detail. It offers `entered_in_error` only from encounter statuses that allowed it before this change, so a completed encounter can be retracted through the API, not yet from Health-id (frontend follow-up). Its encounter status warning (`ORDERS_NOT_CANCELLED` in `Clinical.tsx`) says that cancelling or retracting an encounter does not change its prescriptions or lab requests; the EHR now does, so that sentence is out of date (Health-id follow-up). Its other sentence, that Pharmacy and the Laboratory are not told, still holds.
- Identity: deploy in any order. An earlier build answers break-glass without an `emergency` purpose `400 INVALID_CONSENT_COMMAND`, the new one `423 BREAK_GLASS_DISABLED`; Health-id shows the problem detail for both. Break-glass is off at launch, so neither is expected in production.

**EHR retraction integrity (D-Eminence/hid-system#74, 0090):**
- Run the role bootstrap right after 0090 (§5 step 31), then deploy the EHR API of this change (§6). Until the bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on `auth.membership_has_permission`, a close of an encounter that holds records fails in the database for every build: the new EHR answers `403 ACCESS_DENIED`, an earlier one `500`; nothing is written. A close of an empty encounter does not call the function.
- An earlier EHR build on 0090 answers the new database refusals (`42501 ENCOUNTER_RETRACTION_NOT_PERMITTED`, `55000` closed encounter, closed order and retracted vital) with `500` instead of `403` or `409`, and nothing is written. No EHR build changes a retracted document, so its guard reaches no client. Its retraction does not cover vitals, completed orders or documents, so on 0090 a doctor's retraction through it leaves them as they are, and an encounter's vitals stay `final` (P12 lists open records of closed encounters). Its vital and prescription projections omit `status`.
- The new EHR build on a database without 0090 fails every vital read and every retraction to entered in error, which sets the vitals' status (`42703`, answered `500`, nothing written): deploy it only after 0090.
- Health-id `82b474e`: the encounter status form (`apps/patient-web/src/staff/routes/Clinical.tsx:53-60`, gated on `ehr.encounter.write` at `:1482-1484`) still offers Cancel and Mark entered in error to every role holding `ehr.encounter.write`; a refused close now answers `403 ENCOUNTER_RETRACTION_NOT_PERMITTED`, which `changeEncounterStatus` (`:1341-1362`) shows as an error with the problem detail (it treats only `409` and `412` as a stale encounter); the detail is the fixed sentence "Cancelling or retracting this encounter changes records you may not write", naming no permission or record kind. Its warning `ORDERS_NOT_CANCELLED` (`:64`) has been wrong since 0084 and is wrong about vitals and completed orders too since 0090 (frontend follow-up). It sends no field edit of a prescription or encounter and offers no transition from a closed status (`:53-71`), so `PRESCRIPTION_NOT_AMENDABLE` and the new `ENCOUNTER_NOT_OPEN` cases are reachable only through the API. It ignores the additive `cascade` field and vital `status`: a retracted vital still shows its values without a marker in the Vitals tab (`VitalPanel`, `:661-700`), and its correction form gets `409 VITAL_NOT_AMENDABLE` (frontend follow-up: show the status and hide the form). The emergency summary page does not show vitals (`:1016-1020`), so leaving retracted ones out changes nothing it displays. The patient portal's `GET /ehr/me/records` (`apps/patient-web/src/api.ts:235`) is unchanged for a patient; a staff session there gets `403 PATIENT_SESSION_REQUIRED` instead of a retried `503`.
- Audit readers (`GET /audit/events`, Health-id's Audit page) see `details.status` and `details.code` on refusal rows and `denied` where `failure` was recorded, and new `denied`/`failure` rows under the command's own action for refused clinical commands. A query that counts refusals by outcome or action must include them.

**Production workload identity (D-Eminence/hid-system#54, ADR-047 accepted):**
