- An Identity API image from before the change carries an agent that refuses `production`; a production token task with it never starts its application. Staging works with either image.
- The issuer Lambda changes code in staging too; redeploy it and repeat the staging §7 workload proof.
- Production parameter files must drop the eight workload parameters; the template no longer declares them. Without `WorkloadIssuerUrl` and `WorkloadJwksUrl`, production can no longer point Identity at an issuer that mints a document-scanner subject, so `POST /ehr/documents/scan-events` cannot authenticate until DEP-4-SCAN (DOC-3) is decided, as in staging.
- The receivers' token contract narrows: Identity, Notification, Lab, Pharmacy and the EHR refuse an RS256 token, a token without `exp` or `iat`, one older than 300 s or issued more than 5 s in the future, and one without `token_use: workload`, with the `401` codes they used for any invalid token. The in-stack issuer mints only conforming tokens; development's external issuer must mint the same shape.
- Identity's emergency-contact alert whose Notification token is missing is now retried (`unknown`) instead of failing at once on Notification's `401`; an OTP still fails at once.
- An unhealthy token agent no longer stops its task (D2). A task whose agent never becomes `HEALTHY` still never starts its application.
- No request or response contract changes.
**Enrollment gate, windows and lookup quota (0085, D-Eminence/hid-system#68):**
- 0085 replaces the activation command with the same signature and grants and adds a table and a command. An earlier Identity calling the replaced command gets its two new refusals, which it does not handle: a replay more than 15 minutes after activation (`P0002`, which an earlier build answers `503 ENROLLMENT_UNAVAILABLE` instead of `404`; its `GET /current` still shows the HID until the enrollment expires) and an activation with a contact proof more than 30 minutes old (`23514`, answered `409 ENROLLMENT_STAGE_INVALID`). Neither writes anything, but the second strands the enrollment: an earlier build cannot send it back to the contact step (its contact step refuses a `set_password` enrollment with `409 ENROLLMENT_STAGE_INVALID`, a start of the same NIN with another key is `409 NIN_ALREADY_REGISTERED`, and the same key resumes at `set_password`), so that patient cannot activate until the enrollment expires, 24 hours after its start, and then starts again. This happens between 0085 (§5 step 18) and the Identity rollout (§6 step 6), and after an Identity rollback (§9); deploy Identity right after 0085 and the bootstrap to keep the window short.
- The Identity build of this change needs 0085 and the role bootstrap first: without the command or its `EXECUTE` grant, every start that reaches QoreID answers `503 VERIFICATION_QUOTA_UNAVAILABLE` (§5, §6). Resumes, duplicates and gate refusals do not touch it.
- The Identity build refuses to start with `QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED=true` in production. No CDK stack sets it today; staging may keep it.
- Health-id `main` needs changes to handle the new answers well (`apps/patient-web/src/routes/Enrollment.tsx` on the export): route `409 ENROLLMENT_CONTACT_REVERIFY_REQUIRED` from the password step (`submitPassword`, line 177) back to the contact step instead of showing the message on the password field; a reload already lands there. `429 VERIFICATION_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` and the gate's `503` at the NIN step are shown with the problem's message (`displayError`, line 137), as any start error is. After a successful activation the browser has no enrollment cookie, so a reload of the completion screen shows the start step instead of the HID (Health-id shows the HID from the activation response, line 175, and reads `GET /current` only on load, line 80). The in-repo `apps/web` enrollment page answers any other `409` with "Refresh the page and try again" (`PatientEnrollment.tsx`), which lands on the contact step.
- FE-4 adds `GET /identity/patient-enrollments/availability` and changes only the `detail` of `503 QOREID_DISABLED` and `503 QOREID_NIN_ONLY_DISABLED`; statuses, codes and every body shape are unchanged, and no database object changes, so Identity can be deployed or rolled back alone. Health-id 82b474e works unchanged: it never calls the new route, and its enrollment page shows the start error's message (`apps/patient-web/src/routes/Enrollment.tsx:153`, from the problem's `detail`, `packages/http/src/envelope.ts:71`), which becomes the registration-not-open sentence instead of "The service could not complete the request.". Through the gateway on `main`, which replaces every upstream 502/503/504 with `503 UPSTREAM_UNAVAILABLE` ("A required platform service is temporarily unavailable."), the patient still sees that text until ADR-055 (D-Eminence/hid-system#72) passes upstream errors through; the availability read is a `200` either way. Health-id follow-ups: read availability before showing the NIN step (`Enrollment.tsx:159-172`, `submitNin`) and before the "Get your Health ID" link (`apps/patient-web/src/routes/SignIn.tsx:80`), and show a "registration is not open" notice when the answer is not `200 { "open": true }`; register the route (`packages/contracts/src/registry.ts:62-67`, `apps/patient-web/src/api.ts:216-226`; `api.ts:181` already sends enrollment paths with optional auth); and ask the patient to save the Health ID on the completion screen (`Enrollment.tsx:299`, ENR-2). Until Health-id reads availability, each production start attempt is a 5xx counted by the gateway target group's `TargetErrorAlarm` (ADR-057).

**Account recovery and patient search hardening (0086, D-Eminence/hid-system#69):**
- The Identity build of this change calls `auth.recovery_account_audience` on every recovery start and the two registration functions on every NIN registration, so it must not start before 0086 and the bootstrap's grants (§5, §6): without them each recovery start answers `500` and registration reviews fail. An earlier Identity build works on 0086 except for registration: its duplicate checks and candidate search read `identity.patients` directly and now see only patients linked at the facility, so it can miss a duplicate. Deploy this Identity build right after the bootstrap.
- Health-id (`main`) changes needed, none of which weakens a check if left undone: one message for every refused lookup (`PATIENT_NOT_AVAILABLE`) where it tells `403` and `404` apart; no use of `patientId` from an access-request create, and, optionally, a label such as "shown once access is granted" where the clinician's request list carries `patientIdWithheld: true` (its `patientId` is then `withheld-<last four characters of the request id>`, a string, so the current rendering keeps working); handling of `403 PATIENT_RECENT_AUTH_REQUIRED` and `403 PATIENT_RECENT_RECOVERY_HOLD` on approval, PIN set and emergency-contact add or update; the two `429` codes; copy on the recovery pages that a code goes only to a verified contact of that page's kind of account. A lookup success is `200` instead of `201`; any `2xx` handling keeps working.
- FE-10 needs no Health-id change: the clinical page's lookup before each read and write of a granted patient is never counted, and is refused `429` only while the clinician's or the facility's budget is blocked by refused lookups (Health-id then shows the detail and does not send the save, as for any non-`403`/`404` error).
- FE-8: Health-id (`82b474e`) shows the approval refusal's detail as it is, which now tells the patient to sign out, sign in again and approve within 10 minutes. Recommended follow-up: map `PATIENT_RECENT_AUTH_REQUIRED` on Approve as `pinProblem` does for the PIN, and offer a "Sign in again" action that signs out and returns to `/access` after sign-in.
- The recovery hold applies from the Identity deploy to recoveries completed in the 24 hours before it (it reads consumed challenges).
- Rate-limit rows of the four new scopes stay in `auth.otp_rate_limits` after a rollback and are ignored by earlier builds.

**Staff MFA (0088, D-Eminence/hid-system#71):**
- The Identity build of this change writes `session_kind` and the proof columns of `auth.mfa_login_challenges`, so it must not start before 0088 (§5, §6). It needs no new grant. An earlier Identity build works on 0088: it writes platform challenges only, and the defaults fit.
- From the deploy of this Identity build, every staff session without MFA assurance (all of them at first) is refused `403 STAFF_MFA_REQUIRED` on every authorized route and ended on refresh. Plan the deploy as a forced staff sign-out, outside clinic hours, and tell staff they will set up an authenticator app at their next sign-in.
- **Health-id must ship the staff MFA sign-in first (frontend dependency; references are to Health-id `main` at `0eda90f`, `apps/patient-web/src`).** Until it does, no staff member can sign in to the provider portal:
  - `staff/api.ts:860-866` (`staffApi.login`) expects a `StaffSession` from `POST /api/v1/auth/login`; it now gets `{ status, expiresAt }` and a challenge cookie. It must return the challenge, and new calls must post `/api/v1/auth/mfa/verify` (`{ code }` or `{ recoveryCode }`), `/auth/mfa/enroll/email-code`, `/auth/mfa/enroll/start` (`{ emailCode }`) and `/auth/mfa/enroll/activate` (`{ code }`), each with `auth: 'none'` inside `inRefreshTurn`; verify and activate answer the `StaffSession` (activate adds `recoveryCodes`).
  - `staff/App.tsx:302-312` (`login`) reads `session.actor` from the password step; it must drive the challenge steps and start the session only after verify or activate.
  - `staff/routes/SignIn.tsx:70` (`submit`) must show the authenticator step, the email-code and enrolment steps (QR from `otpauthUri`, setup key, first code) and the recovery codes once. The admin console already has these steps for platform sign-in: `admin/SignIn.tsx:76` (`CredentialsStep`), `:114` (`VerifyStep`), `:162` (`EnrollStep`), `:21` (`formatSetupKey`), and `admin/api.ts:102` (`PlatformLoginChallenge`) and `:275-282` (verify, enrolment start and activation); reuse them with the staff routes and the email-code step added.
  - `staff/api.ts:827-834` (`ProviderEnrollmentActivation`) and `:853-856` (`providerEnrollmentApi.activate`), and `staff/routes/ProviderEnrollment.tsx:105-118` (`activate`): activation now answers `{ activated, organizationId, facilityId, accountId, status, expiresAt }` with no `actor` and no session; continue with `/auth/mfa/enroll/start` (no email code) and `/auth/mfa/enroll/activate` before navigating to `/provider`.
  - `staff/session.ts:173-175` (`endsStaffSession`, `401` only), `staff/api.ts:627` and `staff/api.ts:658-659` (`refreshStaffSession`): a `403 STAFF_MFA_REQUIRED`, from any staff route or from refresh, means the sign-in is over (refresh has already ended it). Treat it like a refused refresh: report the session ended and send the user to sign in again, with a message that the authenticator is now required.
  - New error copy: `MFA_INVALID_CODE`, `MFA_RATE_LIMITED`, `MFA_CHALLENGE_INVALID` (start again), `MFA_ENROLLMENT_PROOF_REQUIRED`, `MFA_ENROLLMENT_PROOF_COMPLETE`, `MFA_ENROLLMENT_PROOF_UNAVAILABLE` (contact your administrator), `MFA_ENROLLMENT_CODE_UNDELIVERABLE` and `MFA_UNAVAILABLE`.
  - `staff/api.ts:860-866` (`staffApi.login`) and `staff/routes/SignIn.tsx:70` (`submit`): the password step can also answer `403 STAFF_MFA_FACTOR_UNPROVEN` (the account's authenticator was set up at platform sign-in). No challenge follows; show that a platform administrator must reset the authenticator, after which the account sets one up at staff sign-in.
  - Platform sign-in: `admin/api.ts:277-279` (`adminApi.startEnrollment`) and `admin/SignIn.tsx:175` (`EnrollStep.begin`) can get `409 MFA_ENROLLMENT_STAFF_SIGN_IN_REQUIRED` for an administrator who also holds a staff membership; `admin/SignIn.tsx:51-54` (`mfaFailure`) should restart to the credentials step with that message, and `admin/problems.ts:24-32` (`ADMIN_PROBLEMS`) needs its copy ("This account also signs in as staff: set up your authenticator at staff sign-in, then use it here"). `packages/contracts/src/registry.ts:54-55` (`auth.adminMfaEnrollStart`) should name the refusal.
  - No change: the patient Google exchange already sends only `patient-login` (`api.ts:196`); the staff portal has no Google sign-in.

**Facility selection (0089, D-Eminence/hid-system#73):**
- The Identity build of this change reads and writes `auth.session_facility_selection`, so it must not start before 0089 and the bootstrap (§5 step 31): without them every staff `GET /auth/session` answers `503`, every staff refresh answers `unavailable` and `POST /auth/facility` a `5xx`. An earlier Identity build works on 0089 and ignores the table. It ships with staff MFA (0088): its staff sessions are the ones issued after the second factor.
- Health-id `82b474e` needs no change (references to `apps/patient-web/src`): `staff/facilitySelection.ts:18-25` (`facilityChoiceNeeded`) asks nothing for `selected` or a missing field, asks for `default` only with more than one facility (`unchosen`), asks again for `reselect`, and treats any other value, `unavailable` included, as `unconfirmed`, asking before any page opens (`staff/App.tsx:603`, `FacilityChoice` at `:454-523`); `staff/App.tsx:315-325` (`selectFacility`) posts `/api/v1/auth/facility` and takes the answer for the facility it sent as `selected`; `staff/api.ts:931-939` treats a `401` from that post as a session that ended; `staff/App.tsx:268-297` (`refresh`) shows a `503` from `GET /auth/session` at load as an error; the portal keeps sending the facility it works at as `X-Facility-ID` (`staff/api.ts:866-868`, `facilityHeaders`) and does not use the stored choice to authorize anything. Its `FacilitySelection` type (`staff/api.ts:44`) does not name `unavailable`; the field is read as unknown on purpose (`facilitySelection.ts:19-20`). Copy only: the comments at `staff/api.ts:34-43` and `staff/facilitySelection.ts:15-17` name migration 0080; it is 0089.
- Health-id `82b474e` cannot sign staff in on a backend with staff MFA until its staff MFA sign-in ships (above); that dependency belongs to 0088. Once it ships, the verify and activation answers it reads carry `facilitySelection: 'default'`, which `facilityChoiceNeeded` already handles.

**Launch module permissions, patient-self binding, named grantee and PIN-use notice (0091, D-Eminence/hid-system#75):**
- The permission withdrawal and the controls apply to every build from 0091's commit: the withdrawn routes answer `403 PERMISSION_DENIED`, and staff sessions lose the permissions on their next request (Identity reads them per request). The patient-self binding applies to every EHR build: the EHR already sets the patient, account, subject, session and correlation settings from Identity's answer (`database.service.ts` `withPatientTransaction`), so a patient's own records read as before.
- Earlier EHR builds complete an upload without checking `uploads_enabled`, but no role can reach the route after 0091. Earlier Identity builds keep working: they select the patient request list's columns by name and pass the access history through, so they simply do not return the new fields. Between 0091 and the bootstrap every Identity build answers a patient's `GET /identity/me/access-requests` with `403` (§5 step 24).
- The Identity build of this change needs 0091: it selects the patient request list's `requester_display_name` and `requester_role`, which an earlier schema lacks, and passes that database error (`42703`) on, so on a schema before 0091 (including after a restore of a pre-0091 backup, §9) a patient's `GET /identity/me/access-requests` answers `500`. Start it only after 0091 and the bootstrap (§6 step 6), and roll it back with the schema. The EHR build of this change works on an earlier schema: `uploads_enabled` exists since 0039.
- Health-id 82b474e needs no change (contract changes are additive or remove permissions it already gates on):
  - `apps/patient-web/src/staff/App.tsx:532-535` (navigation by permission) and `staff/routes/Dashboard.tsx:12-14` (tiles) hide Laboratory, Pharmacy, Document OCR and Outreach; `staff/routes/Operations.tsx:538-544`, `:619-621`, `:999` and `:1203` show their "unavailable" states if opened directly. The ungated "Health ID Migrate" card (`staff/routes/Dashboard.tsx:43`) still links to `/provider/ocr`, which then shows the OCR page's unavailable state (cosmetic; a later Health-id change may hide it).
  - `staff/routes/Clinical.tsx:45` and `:1488` (`canWrite` from `ehr.document.write`, `canOcr` from `ocr.job.read`) hide the upload card and Extract with OCR; `:1060` and `:1522-1523` hide released Lab results without `lab.result.released.read`.
  - `staff/routes/Patients.tsx:28-52` (`METHOD_ROUTES`, `actionUnavailable`) and `:195` disable "Activate emergency access" with the permission explanation; its stale `400 INVALID_CONSENT_COMMAND` mapping (`:160-161`) is no longer reached.
  - `routes/Portal.tsx:333-335` and `:345` keep rendering the facility for requests and grants and ignore `requesterDisplayName`, `requesterRole` and `truncated` (types at `api.ts:80-83` and `:146-149`); showing them is an optional Health-id change. `routes/Portal.tsx:62-68` titles `PATIENT_ACCESS_PIN_USED` "An update about your Health ID account" (generic fallback) under the "Access grant" subject; a specific title is an optional Health-id change.

**Identity and service error and authentication hardening (D-Eminence/hid-system#76, no migration):**
- No database change: any order against 0088. Deploy Identity and EHR together or Identity first: an earlier EHR relays Identity's new workload codes as they are (`401` or `403` with a workload code, still a refusal), and the new EHR maps both the old and the new codes to `503`. Outreach, Lab, OCR and Pharmacy builds are independent.
- Health-id `82b474e` (`apps/patient-web/src`) needs no change: `session.tsx:91` signs out only on `401` and `:146-152` shows "We could not check your session" on anything else, so the new `503` codes keep the patient signed in; `packages/http/src/client.ts:399` marks every `5xx` retryable (`errors.ts:96-98`) and `:444`, `:459` act on `401` only; `staff/api.ts:712-725` reports only a `403 CSRF_VALIDATION_FAILED` or another portal's sign-in as an ended session and treats a `5xx` refresh as temporary; `staff/routes/SignIn.tsx:71` and `routes/SignIn.tsx:32` show the problem detail ("Invalid credentials", or the `403 NO_ACTIVE_FACILITY_MEMBERSHIP` text); `admin/problems.ts:22` already has copy for `ORIGIN_DENIED`.
- Health-id follow-up, not blocking: `admin/api.ts:354-357` (`exportPrincipals`, reason in the query) and `:374-376` (`principals`, terms in the query) should move to `POST /api/v1/admin/principals/export` and `POST /api/v1/admin/principals/search` (JSON body, CSRF header as for other admin commands) before the `GET` forms are removed. Optional copy: `PATIENT_SESSION_REQUIRED`, `IDENTITY_SERVICE_AUTH_UNAVAILABLE`, `AUTHENTICATION_UNAVAILABLE`.

**Turnstile contract (launch/turnstile-contract, no migration):**
- No database change. Identity only; deploy in any order.
- Health-id `077b85c` needs no change. Its staff sign-in sends `staffLoginAction(hostname)` (`apps/patient-web/src/productHostname.ts:58-70`, `staff/api.ts:923-924`): `staff-login` or a product's own action, never `admin-login` or `patient-login`. The admin console signs in through `/auth/admin/login` with `admin-login` (`admin/api.ts:271-272`). Every gated call sends a token, so the `TURNSTILE_REQUIRED` change shows only to a client that omits it; `authSteps.ts:39` already treats `TURNSTILE_REQUIRED` as not about the user's input. A `503 TURNSTILE_UNAVAILABLE` for a server-side Siteverify error is treated as any `5xx`.
- Frontend follow-ups (Health-id, not blocking): reset the widget after every submission, success or failure, since a token is good for one request; map the `403 TURNSTILE_*` codes in `admin/problems.ts`; render one shared Turnstile script loader. The contract section lists every code and what the browser does.
- Configuration: a production or staging task whose `CORS_ORIGINS` names a host without Turnstile actions no longer starts (§9).

**Self-reported enrollment profile (0092, D-Eminence/hid-system#82):**
- Order: 0092, then the bootstrap, then the Identity build of this change. That Identity calls the 9-argument command, which does not exist before 0092: every activation then answers `503 ENROLLMENT_UNAVAILABLE` and writes nothing. Between 0092 and the bootstrap no build can activate (`403 ENROLLMENT_DENIED`).
- An earlier Identity build works on 0092 unchanged: its 7-argument call resolves to the new command (both new arguments default to null), and it never starts a record without a date of birth or gender (its adapter answers `502`).
- Health-id keeps working unchanged for a record with both fields: the activation body is the same. For a record without a date of birth or gender, activation answers `400 ENROLLMENT_PROFILE_REQUIRED` ("Enter your date of birth and gender", shown as sent), and the patient cannot activate until Health-id asks for the fields ("Health-id alignment" row 6 below).
- No other client changes: the start body, the contact step, the codes and the sign-in are unchanged.

**NIN holder proof (0093, D-Eminence/hid-system#84):**
- Order: P28, then 0093, then the bootstrap, then the notification-api build of this change, then the Identity build (P24). Until the bootstrap, no holder code can be sent or checked (`403 ENROLLMENT_DENIED`).
- An earlier Identity build on 0093 fails closed. Its start inserts a row that starts at `verify_holder`, and its contact step refuses that state (`409 ENROLLMENT_STAGE_INVALID`); its in-place reuse of an expired row is refused (the runtime may no longer update those columns); its activation is refused for want of a holder proof (`409 ENROLLMENT_STAGE_INVALID`). It still answers `409 NIN_ALREADY_REGISTERED` at start, as before, until the Identity build of this change replaces it. Sign-in and recovery are unchanged.
- An earlier notification-api refuses the purpose `NIN_HOLDER_VERIFY` (`400`): the Identity build of this change then answers the holder send `503 OTP_DELIVERY_UNAVAILABLE` and no code is sent.
- Health-id `55a50b0` cannot register a patient on 0093 and fails closed: it shows the contact step for `verify_holder`, and `POST .../contact` answers `409 ENROLLMENT_STAGE_INVALID`. Sign-in is unchanged. Before registration opens anywhere it needs "Health-id alignment" rows 1, 2, 6, 8 and 9 below (P33).
- Health-id calls the signed-in "Verify NIN" (`POST /identity/me/verification/nin`). With its flag off (the default) it answers `503 QOREID_PATIENT_NIN_BINDING_DISABLED` with its own detail, "Verifying a NIN from your account is not available", which the gateway passes through (ADR-055). Health-id should hide that action until the owner decides (P27; "Health-id alignment" row 13 below).
- With every new flag off (the default), registration stays closed: every start answers `503 PATIENT_REGISTRATION_NOT_OPEN` with the not-open detail, before the QoreID gate, whatever `QOREID_ENABLED` and `QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED` say, and `GET .../availability` answers `{"open":false,"online":false,"facility":false}`. Staging cannot open the online route (email only, P26), and with 0094 production runs it only while a facility takes part (P25): neither opens without a designated facility.

**Facility-assisted registration (D-Eminence/hid-system#85, 0094):**
- 0094 replaces the holder proof and the activation command with the same signatures. An earlier (0093) Identity keeps working on 0094 and answers `facility: false`.
- The Identity of this change needs 0094 and its bootstrap before it starts: on 0093 its designation routes fail (`5xx`), and with `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED` on so do availability, start and the facility list. With the flag off it reads nothing new on the enrollment routes.
- In production the Identity and the regional stack of this change accept `NIN_HOLDER_SMS_ENABLED=true` only with `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED=true` (P25). An environment that kept the online route on under 0093 (none in production) must turn the facility flag on, or the online route off, before the new task starts.
- Health-id `55a50b0` has no facility list, desk code, staff check or designation screen, and its HTTP client drops the `fields` member of the staff `422`. It fails closed on the facility route (P31; "Health-id alignment" rows 1, 3, 4, 5 and 7 below). Its staff MFA sign-in (FE-2) matches the backend.
- Nothing changes for an enrollment proven by a registry-phone code, for sign-in or for recovery.

**Novu receives the verified login email (D-Eminence/hid-system#90, 0095, ADR-064):**
- 0095 adds two functions and their grants, nothing else. An earlier notification worker works on 0095 unchanged: it never calls them.
- The worker of this change with `NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC=disabled` (the default, and the only value production accepts) runs main's code path and works on 0094 or 0095, before or after the bootstrap.
- With `NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC=email` it needs 0095 and the bootstrap (§5 step 31): without them it refuses to start (the lookup is missing or not executable), so its rollout fails and no event is handled. Staging only, after P35 (§6 step 5).
- Turning the flag off, or rolling the worker back to an earlier build, does not clear the emails it synced: §9 first.
- No HID route or Health-id contract changes.

**Provider enrollment answers (D-Eminence/hid-system#81, no migration):**
- No database change. Identity only; deploy in any order.
- Health-id `55a50b0` shows both answers as sent (`staff/routes/ProviderEnrollment.tsx:72-75`, `errorMessage`) but gives no next step ("Health-id alignment" rows 11 and 12 below). Start no longer answers `PROVIDER_EMAIL_ALREADY_REGISTERED`. Starting again with another email for the same CAC answers `409 PROVIDER_ENROLLMENT_REQUIRES_REVIEW`, so Health-id must not suggest it. Both refusals send the applicant to Health ID support, which no document names yet (`PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md` §2, SUPPORT-CONTACT).

**Admin integration status (launch/signup-alignment, Identity only, no migration):**
- Deploy in any order. Only the `state` and `detail` of `patient_nin_enrollment`, and whether `activeCapabilities` lists `patient_nin`, can differ. Health-id (`admin/Integrations.tsx:123`, `:127-128`) and the in-repo admin console (`apps/admin/src/Integrations.tsx`) show them as sent and need no change. The state stays `open` or `closed`.

**Patient record release (ADR-065, 0096):**
- 0096 only adds policies, a view and four indexes. Each new policy and the view admit a category only to a patient-self transaction that names it in `app.patient_release`, which no earlier EHR build sets: earlier builds (including one from before D-Eminence/hid-system#52, whose patient summary still queried every category) read nothing new from the patient context and keep working on it; the new policies admit nothing to staff contexts.
- The EHR build of this change with every flag off (the default) names no category, so neither it nor the database releases anything new, and it answers exactly as before. The production profile refuses every flag (P36). Run the bootstrap after 0096 before any flag is set: until it runs, the view has no reader, and a configured Lab results section answers `503 PATIENT_RECORDS_UNAVAILABLE`.
- Health-id renders none of the new sections or the download (its patient record page was not reviewed in this change); keep the flags off until it does (P36).

**Health-id alignment for sign-up (read-only review; Health-id `main` `55a50b0`, hid-system `main` `ae00f0c`):**
- Health-id was not changed. This review compared Health-id `55a50b0` with hid-system `ae00f0c` (D-Eminence/hid-system#81, #82, #84 and #85). Line numbers are at those commits; launch/signup-alignment moves some lines of the service (its route rule moved to `identity/ownership-routes.ts`). Health-id paths are under `apps/patient-web/src` unless they start with `packages/` or `docs/`. hid-system paths are under `services/identity-api/src`, and "service" is `identity/patient-enrollment.service.ts`.
- These match the backend and need no change: patient sign-in with an email or HID; password recovery; staff sign-in with an authenticator (FE-2), including the hand-off from provider activation; the provider answers of #81, shown as sent; the availability gate and closed notice; the sign-out guard; the stale-contact re-check; the activation limit; and the HID shown at the end.
- Every gap fails closed: no account is created and nothing leaks. The cost is that patients cannot register, and each start that passes the gate spends a paid QoreID lookup (service `:212-216`). With every flag at its default, staging and production answer every start `503 PATIENT_REGISTRATION_NOT_OPEN`, which Health-id shows as its closed notice.
- P0 rows block patient sign-up (P31, P33). P1 rows leave a patient or applicant without a next step. P2 rows do not block. Rows 8 and 9 are P1, but P33 requires them, so they block launch with P33.

## 4. Prerequisites and pre-release checks

| # | Check | Owner | How |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P1 | The production migration ledger's last applied version is known. | Database operator | `npm run db:plan` as the migration administrator, as in `MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6. It applies nothing, but its ledger bootstrap statements need the administrator's privileges, so it cannot run with read-only credentials. The local rehearsal covers 0028 → 0091; any other starting point needs its own rehearsal. After this release the last applied version is `0096_patient_record_release.sql` (ADR-065), after `0095_notification_verified_recipient.sql` (ADR-064). Before 0087, on any database the legacy promotion or SQL has written, run operator plan I4 (`PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md` §3): 0087 refuses to apply while an account holds two active, unheld memberships at one facility. |
| P2 | A restorable backup of the target database exists and its restore was tested. | Database operator | Snapshot or `pg_dump` taken immediately before the window. The rehearsal checks backup and restore integrity, but the real restore must be proven on staging. |
| P3 | `MFA_SECRET_KEY_B64` is provisioned for Identity: 32 random bytes, base64, from the secret store. `MFA_KEY_VERSION` is set. | Security owner and infrastructure owner | The infrastructure code does this since D-Eminence/hid-system#49 (§2): the Identity task reads `MFA_SECRET_KEY_B64` from the `mfaSecretKeyB64` field of the Identity sensitive secret (`IdentitySensitiveSecretArn`) and runs with `MFA_KEY_VERSION=aws-v1`. Before that task definition is deployed in an environment, add `mfaSecretKeyB64` to that environment's secret: 32 bytes from a cryptographically secure generator in canonical padded base64 (44 characters), keeping every other field, never printed or committed, and generated separately for each environment. Until the field exists, ECS starts no Identity task from that task definition, which stops every sign-in, not only platform sign-in (§3). On staging, `python3 scripts/staging-external-preflight.py --cloud-read-only` reports whether the field is present and whether it has that format (`canonical_32_byte_base64`); both must be true. Every other field the task reads from that secret is required the same way; the `IdentitySensitiveSecretArn` description in the synthesized template names them, including the two NIN keys a QoreID staging task reads. Without the key, platform sign-in fails closed with `503 MFA_UNAVAILABLE`, and a malformed key stops Identity at start-up. Never reuse a staging key in production. The key and `MFA_KEY_VERSION` are fixed when the first authenticator is enrolled: changing either later invalidates every enrolled authenticator unless a re-encryption release exists. |
| P4 | Identity `CORS_ORIGINS` lists only the exact HTTPS console origins, with no paths. | Platform operator | Enforced at start-up in production. With same-origin `/api/v1`, CORS is not used by the console. |
| P5 | The sizes of `audit.events`, `auth.sessions`, `auth.session_events` and `identity.consent_grants` are known, for the lock windows of 0067, 0069, 0072 and 0074. | Database operator | `select count(*)` and `pg_total_relation_size(...)` for each table on a recent snapshot. See §5. |
| P6 | Two Super Admins can sign in and confirm. | Security owner | Two-person approval needs a second Super Admin. Facility suspension needs at least one reachable Super Admin (0072). On a first deployment of Stage 2A (no platform sign-in yet), check this after step 6, when the Super Admins enrol their authenticators. |
| P7 | The staging acceptance in §7 passed on the exact builds being released. | Release owner | Evidence attached to the release record. |
| P8 | The definer paths below work under the role that owns the schema objects (the migration administrator), which on RDS is not a superuser and is not relied on to bypass `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`; paths that do not stay out of the release (launch decision I3). Since D-Eminence/hid-system#63 the rest of the chain needs no superuser: the administrator holds `SET ON PARAMETER plpgsql.variable_conflict` only for a run that applies 0015, revoked after 0083, the role bootstrap runs as it, and `platform.control_enabled` no longer depends on its attributes (§2). | Database operator | Document scanning, the OCR pipeline and Outreach campaign registration depend on it, because their security-definer commands and triggers read row-level-secured tables as their owner. The rehearsal migrates as a superuser, and its non-superuser owner check runs only the additional suites, which do not cover these paths. Since D-Eminence/hid-system#63 it also migrates and bootstraps on a second cluster as a non-superuser administrator (`verify-non-superuser-database.mjs`); that stage covers the platform controls and the OCR claim's settings, not these paths. Run locally on a copy with every function and table owned by a `NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS` role (§8), `schema.integration.sql` failed in turn at each of these, and passed in full once all of them were returned to a superuser owner:<br>• `ehr.append_document_scan_event` (`Document not found`);<br>• the `ocr.record_job_event` trigger (row-level security on `ocr.job_events`);<br>• `ocr.claim_worker_job` (`permission denied to set parameter "plpgsql.variable_conflict"`, set by 0015; since 0083 the option is in its body and needs no privilege);<br>• `ocr.validate_job_write`, `ocr.complete_worker_job`, `ocr.validate_extraction_insert` and `ocr.fail_worker_job`;<br>• `ocr.validate_patient_confirmation`, whose wrong-patient check failed open because it compares with a document it cannot see. Under such an owner this path is unreachable, because claims already fail. Since 0076 the guard fails closed instead: under such an owner every confirmation is refused;<br>• `lab.validate_imported_evidence`, whose OCR publication branch accepted another facility's validation and publication because it could not read them (reproduced locally). Since 0077 it refuses them; a Lab import of the session facility's own OCR evidence still passes;<br>• `outreach.validate_registration_campaign_write` (`Exact Outreach campaign membership is required`).<br>Since 0080 `pharmacy.validate_work_item` and `pharmacy.validate_dispensing` also read and share-lock the EHR prescription as their owner. Under such an owner the lock applies the EHR select and update policies with the pharmacist's session, so it passes only with a write consent for the patient, which Pharmacy's own insert policies already require; a prescription the owner cannot lock is refused (`pharmacy-source-prescription.integration.sql` probes it under the rehearsal's non-superuser owner). Those policies call `ehr.context_allows`, which the same owner owns, so no grant is needed; a dedicated owner (§4.1) would need `EXECUTE` on it.<br>The list covers what `schema.integration.sql` exercises; other definer paths may share the dependency. It missed `platform.control_enabled`, which under such an owner raised `PLATFORM_CONTROL_UNAVAILABLE` for every patient and provider request (`503` from the Identity, EHR and Outreach guards); since D-Eminence/hid-system#63 it has the technical owner `hid_platform_control_reader` (§2). Before the release, confirm the owner's attributes as the migration administrator (`select rolsuper, rolbypassrls from pg_roles where rolname = current_user`). The role bootstrap needed a superuser until D-Eminence/hid-system#63: it re-asserted `NOSUPERUSER … NOBYPASSRLS` with `ALTER ROLE` on every run, which PostgreSQL 16 allows only to a superuser, even when nothing changes; it now creates the roles and checks their attributes (§2). On a managed service whose administrator is not a superuser, such as Amazon RDS, 0015 does not apply as it stands: a non-superuser migration administrator is refused `SET plpgsql.variable_conflict` (`permission denied to set parameter`) unless it holds `GRANT SET ON PARAMETER plpgsql.variable_conflict` (verified locally, §4.1). Until 0083 the grant had to stay, because `ocr.claim_worker_job` applied 0015's setting as its owner on every call; since 0083 it is needed only for the migration run and is revoked after it (`MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6.1). Arrange it before the staging rehearsal (S1), and confirm there that 0015 applies, that the grant is revoked after 0083, and that an OCR claim and a patient confirmation succeed (S4b). If RDS refuses the grant, the fallback is a reviewed ledger exception for 0015, not a superuser-like role (§10). The alternative is dedicated technical owners for these commands, proposed in §4.1 and ADR-041; it is not implemented. The new `ocr.worker_queue_metrics()` depends on none of this. |
| P9 | No OCR job's source document lacks an exact clean scan, or each one found is accounted for. | Database operator | Before applying 0077, run the read-only check below on staging and then production as the migration administrator; it writes nothing, and `row_security = off` makes it fail instead of reporting zeros if the role cannot see every row. The worker never claims a job it lists, and after 0077 such a job cannot be started either. One whose `latest_scan_event` is `<none>` can still be failed, retried and validated; one with a scan that is not clean cannot be completed or validated. Either kind can be cancelled after 0077 (before it, only a `<none>` job could). The OCR API has no cancel route: cancel with a reviewed direct update under change control (`status = 'cancelled'`, `row_version = row_version + 1`), or record why a job stays. A queued job left in place keeps counting in `oldestQueueAgeSeconds`, so `OcrQueueAgeAlarm` keeps firing. Verified locally on synthetic data: it lists every job of a document without a scan event. |
| P10 | The purpose-of-use codes before and after 0082 are recorded, and `break_glass_enabled` is in its intended launch state. | Database operator and security owner | Run the read-only check below as the migration administrator on staging and then production, before and after 0082, and attach both outputs. After 0082 `direct-care` must be listed and active. A row that was there before (inserted by hand) is kept as it is, display, source and `active` included; an inactive `direct-care` refuses every direct-care grant, request and PIN, so reactivate it by a reviewed change or record why. `emergency` and `healthcare-operations` are not seeded (pending product decisions, §2): insert either only by a reviewed change after its decision, and `emergency` only once `break_glass_enabled` is set to its intended launch state, because the code makes break-glass usable at once. Retire a code with `active = false`, never by deleting it. The check reads `break_glass_enabled` through `platform.control_enabled`, as every guard does: `platform.control_settings` forces row-level security, which hides every row from the migration administrator (not a superuser and `NOBYPASSRLS` on RDS, P8) and refuses it a read with `row_security = off` (`query would be affected by row-level security policy`), while the function reads the flag as its technical owner and raises an error if the row is missing. `row_security = off` makes the purpose-code read fail instead of hiding rows if that table is ever put under row-level security. `verify-non-superuser-database.mjs` runs this check, taken from this document, as such an administrator. At launch break-glass is off (owner default, deny by default; D-Eminence/hid-system#64): `break_glass_enabled` must be `false` (since 0091 set by the migration where it was still seeded; otherwise through `POST /admin/controls`, P11) and no `emergency` row may exist; break-glass then answers `423 BREAK_GLASS_DISABLED` whichever is missing. Since 0091 no role holds `identity.break-glass.write` either, so it answers `403 PERMISSION_DENIED` first. |
| P11 | The launch module set is in force: `outreach_portal_enabled`, `uploads_enabled` and `break_glass_enabled` are `false`, no `emergency` or `healthcare-operations` purpose of use exists, and no role holds a permission 0084 or 0091 withdraws. | Database operator (check); platform operator only if a control is still on | 0039 seeds the three controls on. Since 0091 (D-Eminence/hid-system#75, ADR-058) the migration sets each off where it is still exactly as seeded, so no manual step is needed on a database where no administrator changed them: such a row reads `false`, version 2, `updated_by` null and the reason "Off at launch until its module is enabled by a reviewed decision (0091, D17)", with no control event (a migration has no actor; the ledger records when 0091 ran). A control an administrator had already changed keeps its value: if one reads `true`, a platform administrator with `platform.control.manage` sets it `false` with `POST /admin/controls` (a critical action: MFA platform session, TOTP step-up within five minutes, a reason and `If-Match`), which writes a control event naming that administrator. Run the read-only check below as the migration administrator after 0091 and its bootstrap, on staging and then production, and attach the output: the three controls `false`, no purpose rows, no permission rows, and for each control either no control event (0091 turned it off) or events whose latest names the administrator who set it `false`. The check reads the controls through `platform.control_enabled`, as every guard does, because `platform.control_settings` forces row-level security, which hides its rows from the migration administrator (P8, P10); the other tables have no row-level security. `verify-non-superuser-database.mjs` runs this check, taken from this document, as a `NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS` administrator. To see each control's version and reason as well, a platform administrator with `platform.control.read` reads `GET /api/v1/admin/controls` (platform session): a control 0091 turned off reads `version` 2 and the reason above. The ECS desired counts and edge routes of the disabled Lab, Pharmacy, OCR and Outreach services are infrastructure work outside this check; since D-Eminence/hid-system#66 the regional stack pins their desired counts at 0 (ADR-053) and the gateway already refuses their routes (D-Eminence/hid-system#65). Setting `uploads_enabled` back to `true` later also needs, in the same reviewed release, the documents bucket's S3 origin in the portal `connect-src` of `hid-web` and `hid-admin`: Health-id uploads straight to the presigned URL, which the launch policy blocks (ADR-055; `PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md` I7). |
| P12 | Open records of closed encounters are reviewed. | Database operator and clinical owner | 0084 changes no existing record (a migration has no actor for the version trigger). Before and after 0084, run the read-only check below on staging and then production, and attach the output: notes, diagnoses and open orders of encounters entered in error, and open orders of cancelled encounters, written before the cascade existed. The clinical owner decides for each whether it is retracted through the EHR (a note or order by its own status change; a diagnosis by setting `verificationStatus` `entered_in_error`) or kept, and records why. A lab request to be retracted must be retracted before 0084: 0084 withdraws from every role `ehr.lab-request.write`, which the only route that changes a lab request's status (`PATCH .../lab-requests/:requestId`) requires, and a cancelled or entered-in-error encounter has no further transition, so no cascade reaches its lab requests. Run the check last in the release window, immediately before 0084; retract each `lab_request` row it lists that the clinical owner does not keep (`status` `cancelled` or `entered_in_error`, with a change reason) while the permission is still held; run it again and apply 0084 only when every `lab_request` row it lists is one the clinical owner decided to keep. A lab request listed only after 0084 (of an encounter closed after that run, or by an earlier EHR build on 0084, §3 and §9) can then be changed only by a reviewed data change that names the acting clinician, with an active membership in the record's facility, and the change reason; the version trigger refuses an update without them. An empty result needs no action. |
| P13 | Production has a working workload issuer and token delivery that passed the staging workload proof (`AWS_DEPLOYMENT_RUNBOOK.md` §7, I5), including the key-policy, edge-allowlist, 30-minute flood and issuer-outage checks. Today production has none, so every service-to-service call fails closed. | Security owner and infrastructure owner | ADR-047 is accepted (D1-D3, D-Eminence/hid-system#54). Obtain a staging deployment authorization and run I5; then, with the production unlock, a production change approval and a deployment authorization, follow `AWS_DEPLOYMENT_RUNBOOK.md` §12 (I6, not approved). Never use local-secret mode. |
| P14 | The launch frontend artefacts are staged and verified (ADR-052): for each launch host (staging: `staging`, `admin.staging`; production: `www`, `admin`) a Health-id `apps/patient-web` build from the release's reviewed Health-id commit is staged with `stage-health-id-build.mjs`, and `verify:launch:<environment>` passes. No Worker Custom Domain is bound on the application hosts that are not deployed. | Release owner and Cloudflare operator | In Health-id at that commit: `npm ci`, then per host `npm run build:patient-web` with `VITE_HID_ENVIRONMENT` the environment, `VITE_HID_API_BASE_URL` and `VITE_HID_APP_ORIGIN` both `https://<host>`, `VITE_HID_TURNSTILE_SITE_KEY` the environment widget's site key, and no `VITE_HID_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` or `VITE_HID_SENTRY_DSN` (`infra/cloudflare/README.md`). In hid-system at the release commit, `npm --prefix infra/cloudflare ci` (no hid-system frontend build is needed: `verify:launch` skips the legacy builds of the Workers that are not deployed). Stage each build with the 40-character commit into its default staged directory (the script refuses to replace anything but an earlier artefact of the same Worker and environment), run `npm --prefix infra/cloudflare run verify:launch:<environment>` and `dry-run:<environment>`, and attach both outputs and each `provenance.json` to the release record. Confirm with the staging plan's Custom Domain reads (`expect_no_binding`; production: the same reads for the production hosts) that `ehr`, `lab`, `pharmacy`, `migrate`, `outreach` and `ocr` have no binding. The staging widget's site key, the `ORIGIN_AUTH_TOKEN` of each deployed Worker and the account and zone identifiers come from the operator. |
| P15 | The production external preflight's launch profile passes (`scripts/production-external-preflight.py --cloud-read-only`, `AWS_DEPLOYMENT_RUNBOOK.md` §13). | Infrastructure owner and security owner | Operator step, not done. Run it with the approved `HID_AWS_ACCOUNT` and `HID_AWS_REGION` and an existing read-only session; attach the value-free receipt. `launch_profile.passed` must be `true`: every auth and Identity sensitive field present, the five Identity keys canonical 32-byte base64, `googleOidcClientIds` present and empty, the notification secret exactly `sesFromAddress`, `termiiBaseUrl`, `termiiApiKey`, `termiiSenderId`, SES production access, sending and DKIM, and the alarm topic (P16). The session also needs `kms:DescribeKey` and `kms:GetKeyPolicy` on the topic's key. Never print a value. |
| P16 | The production operational alarm topic `hid-production-operational-alerts` exists, uses a customer-managed KMS key whose policy lets `cloudwatch.amazonaws.com` use it, and has a confirmed subscription for every on-call recipient the operations owner names (ADR-053). | Operations owner (recipients) and infrastructure owner (topic) | Operator step, not done. The recipients are an external dependency. Create the key and topic (§13 step 1), confirm each subscription, publish a test message and record that each recipient received it; the preflight (P15) reports whether the key is customer managed, enabled and in the topic's account and region, whether its key policy grants `cloudwatch.amazonaws.com` `kms:Decrypt` and `kms:GenerateDataKey`, and the confirmed count, not who received the message. Tell the recipients that the five running-task alarms will notify ALARM on the first deploy (P20). Synthesize production with this topic's exact ARN in `HID_OPERATIONAL_ALARM_TOPIC_ARN`. |
| P17 | SES in the production account has production access (out of the sandbox) and a DKIM-verified domain identity for `healthidentitydirectory.com`, and `sesFromAddress` is on that domain. | Infrastructure owner | Operator step, not done (external: an AWS SES production-access request and DNS DKIM records). The preflight (P15) reports `production_access_enabled`, `sending_enabled`, `domain_verified_for_sending` and `dkim_status_success`. |
| P18 | A Termii account has an approved Nigerian sender ID, and `termiiSenderId` in the production notification secret is that ID. | Product owner and infrastructure owner | Operator step, not done (external: Termii account and sender-ID approval). Not readable from AWS: attach Termii's approval record. Then a synthetic SMS OTP after deployment must log `notification.otp.outcome` with outcome `accepted` and provider `termii`. |
| P19 | Google sign-in is off: `googleOidcClientIds` is present and empty in the production Identity sensitive secret, and the Health-id build has no `VITE_HID_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` (REC-GOOGLE). | Security owner and frontend release owner | Operator step, not done. Depends on S4m (6): staging evidence that ECS starts an Identity task whose `googleOidcClientIds` is the empty string (production injects the field from a required secret reference; if ECS refused an empty value, no Identity task would start). The preflight (P15) reports `google_sign_in_enabled: false`; the frontend build record shows no Google client ID. After deployment, a Google exchange and a link both answer `503 GOOGLE_SIGN_IN_UNAVAILABLE`. |
| P20 | The reviewed production parameter file (I6) deploys first with every `*DesiredCount` at 0, then raises only enabled services (Identity, Notification API, EHR, the event dispatcher and the gateway), each to at least the live minimum in its description. | Infrastructure owner and release owner | Operator step, not done. The template defaults to 0 (ADR-053) and refuses any count for Lab, Pharmacy, OCR, Outreach and the Notification Worker but 0. Expected: from the first deploy until each enabled service runs its live minimum, its running-task alarm (`GatewayRunningTaskAlarm`, `IdentityApiRunningTaskAlarm`, `EhrApiRunningTaskAlarm`, `NotificationApiRunningTaskAlarm`, `EventDispatcherRunningTaskAlarm`; missing data is breaching) is in `ALARM` and has notified the topic; it notifies `OK` after the scale-up. Tell the on-call recipients beforehand (`AWS_DEPLOYMENT_RUNBOOK.md` §9, §12); one still in `ALARM` after its service was scaled is a real failure. |
| P21 | The Brevo fallback is paused in production: `GET /api/v1/admin/integrations/brevo` shows it paused, so Identity plans no OTP fallback (ADR-053). | Platform operator | Operator step, not done. Migration 0047 seeds Brevo enabled as the email and SMS fallback, and production injects no Brevo field, so an unpaused Brevo answers `not_configured` after every definitive SES or Termii failure (and becomes the primary if SES or Termii is paused). After the migrations and before ingress, a platform administrator with `platform.integration.manage` calls `POST /api/v1/admin/integrations/brevo/pause` (a critical action: MFA platform session, TOTP step-up, a reason, `If-Match` and `Idempotency-Key`), which writes the integration audit with that administrator as actor (`AWS_DEPLOYMENT_RUNBOOK.md` §13 step 5). Attach the response and the audit entry. |
| P22 | Staff MFA (0088): clinical accounts are enrolled before clinical roles are enabled, enrolments after a recovery are reviewed, and dual-role administrators are reset. | Security owner and facility administrators | Release-blocking for enabling clinical roles, because an account without an authenticator is as strong as its mailbox (§2, Staff MFA). Run the three read-only queries in `MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6 as the migration administrator after 0088: (1) the count of active clinical memberships whose account has no authenticator enrolled at staff sign-in must be zero for every facility whose clinical roles are enabled, with each enrolment done by the staff member in person or confirmed through a channel other than their mailbox; (2) every administrator whose factor predates 0088 and who holds an active staff membership has a two-person MFA reset approved, and enrols at staff sign-in; (3) the staff enrolments by emailed code that followed a password recovery of the same account are reviewed daily during the launch period and each confirmed with the staff member outside the mailbox. Owner notices (`MFA_NOTICE_SINK`, `ACCOUNT_RECOVERY_NOTICE_SINK`) are not bound yet, and a notice to the mailbox could not warn of the mailbox's own compromise. |
| P23 | QoreID confirms in writing what a production NIN lookup returns (DEP-1): whether `nin.phone` is present, what it means (the holder's own phone or a contact), when it was captured, whether a SIM change is flagged, and that HID may send an SMS to it. Production sample responses, with identity data removed, are replayed through the adapter in a unit test, a name, NIN and reference only response among them. | Product owner and security owner | Operator step, not done (external). Release-blocking for `QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED` and for both holder routes in production (ADR-062): every route starts from the same lookup. If a production response lacks `status`, `summary.nin_check` or the numeric `id`, the adapter refuses it (`502`) and neither route launches until the adapter is changed. Without a usable `nin.phone` the online route cannot work. |

An earlier notification-api refuses the unknown purpose (`400`), so Identity answers the holder send `503 OTP_DELIVERY_UNAVAILABLE` and no code is sent. |
| P25 | At least one active participating-facility designation and the facility route (ADR-063) are live before `NIN_HOLDER_SMS_ENABLED` is set in production. | Product owner and facility operations | With 0093 alone Identity and the regional stack refuse `NIN_HOLDER_SMS_ENABLED=true` in production outright. D-Eminence/hid-system#85 (0094) replaces that: production accepts it only with `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED=true`, and Identity opens the online route in production only while at least one facility takes part (P30). With no active designation, or after the last one is suspended, production answers `online: false` and `facility: false` and refuses the code send and check. Operator check: `GET .../availability` answers `facility: true` and `online: true` after the online route is enabled, and Admin Settings → Integrations shows QoreID `patient_nin_enrollment` open with "Open holder routes: online and facility." The facility route is the remedy for a holder whose online route someone else closed, and the only route for a person without a usable registry phone. |

While off, `POST /identity/me/verification/nin` answers `503 QOREID_PATIENT_NIN_BINDING_DISABLED`. |
| P28 | Production holds no active public enrollment before 0093. | Database operator | Run the read-only check below as the migration administrator before 0093. It must return `0`; otherwise stop: 0093 would mark those rows `none_before_0093`, Health IDs created without a holder proof, and the owner decides what happens to them first. Staging may hold synthetic ones. |
| P29 | The owner and legal sign off the in-person procedure (ADR-063): one method, `government_photo_id_in_person`; one original photo ID of four types (national ID card, international passport, driver's licence, voter's card; no NIN slip); the five attestations; one verifying staff member; minors not refused. The sign-off accepts these residual risks (ADR-063): HID checks only what staff type against the record and captures no document data; with a record that has no date of birth (the shape the owner reported for production QoreID) the system checks the two names only, so the photo-ID check rests on one staff member's attestation; the per-field `422` results let a staff member holding a desk code, including one they issued on their own phone, test name and date guesses within the budgets; and the time to answer can differ before the SQL authorization. The mitigations are the budgets, the staff audit row and the weekly review query (§10); a second approver is an owner decision. The facility operations owner writes the desk procedure and trains the staff who check. | Product owner, legal, facility operations | Release-blocking for `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED` anywhere a real patient can use it. Not done. |
| P30 | Each participating facility is designated through `POST /admin/facilities/:id/assisted-registration` (If-Match `0`, a reason, a fresh step-up) with the address line, town, LGA, state and hours patients are shown, only after the facility and its organization are verified and active. Suspend a designation to stop the route at that facility at once. In production, suspending the last active designation also closes the online holder route (P25): registration then stops until a facility takes part again. | Platform administrators and facility operations | No facility is designated by any migration. Without a designation the facility route stays closed whatever the flag says. |
| P31 | The Health-id staff MFA sign-in (FE-2), the staff check screen, the desk-code display, the facility list page and the admin designation screen are live before `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED` is set. The patient's device reads `GET .../current` again after the staff check to continue at `verify_contact`, and answers its `404 ENROLLMENT_NOT_FOUND` there by following the staff member's direction (account recovery when staff were told the NIN has a Health ID), not only "start again" (`INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, "Facility-assisted registration"). | Frontend owner | Health-id `55a50b0` has the staff MFA sign-in (FE-2), which matches the backend, but none of the other four (read-only review; §3 "Health-id alignment" rows 1, 3, 4 and 5); with the flag on and no screens, staff cannot check and patients cannot see a desk code. The patient screens of P33 are needed as well. |
| P32 | Every staff member who checks holds a clinical grant (doctor, clinician or nurse) at that facility through governed workforce provisioning with a current credential attestation (FE-3), or the operator follows a written procedure for those grants. | Platform administrators and security owner | A legacy or onboarding role never qualifies: the staff command answers `403 ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_NOT_AUTHORIZED`. A lapsed attestation closes the route for that staff member at once. |
| P33 | The Health-id patient sign-up screens for 0092 to 0094 are live before `QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED` is set with either holder route (`NIN_HOLDER_SMS_ENABLED` or `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED`) anywhere a real patient can use it: the browser route registry and the patient-web grant name the holder, holder-verify, facility-code and participating-facilities routes; the page handles `verify_holder`, and before `NIN_HOLDER_SMS_ENABLED` also the code sent to the phone on the NIN record (send, entry, the resend wait, and `NIN_HOLDER_ROUTE_UNAVAILABLE` and `NIN_HOLDER_ROUTE_LIMITED` leading to the facility route); the password step asks for the date of birth and gender that `requiredProfileFields` lists (nothing for `[]`) and shows `ENROLLMENT_PROFILE_FIELD_VERIFIED`, `ENROLLMENT_PROFILE_REQUIRED` and `DATE_OF_BIRTH_INVALID` on those fields; `409 NIN_ALREADY_REGISTERED` (after a correct code or at activation) leads to account recovery, sign-in and Health ID support with the reference, not back to the NIN form; and `409 ENROLLMENT_HOLDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` at activation starts again from the NIN step with a new `Idempotency-Key` (`INTERFACE_CONTRACT.md`, "A NIN record without a date of birth or gender" and "The NIN holder step"). | Frontend owner | Health-id `55a50b0` has none of them (read-only review; §3 "Health-id alignment" rows 1, 2, 6, 8 and 9). It shows the contact form at `verify_holder`, which `POST .../contact` refuses `409 ENROLLMENT_STAGE_INVALID`. It activates with the password only, so a record without a date of birth or gender (the shape the owner reported for production QoreID, P29) can never activate. After `ENROLLMENT_HOLDER_PROOF_REQUIRED` the same NIN resumes the dead enrollment with the kept key. Every gap fails closed and no account is created, but each start spends a paid QoreID lookup. Run S4v with these screens. |
| P34 | Before patient or provider self-sign-up opens in production, the owner has decided ENR-2-LAUNCH and SUPPORT-CONTACT (`PHASE_3_LAUNCH_DECISIONS.md` §2), and the support channel is published where the refusals send people. | Product owner | Not done. Without them, three groups are told to contact a support channel nobody can reach: a phone-only patient who loses the HID, a person whose NIN someone else registered, and a provider applicant refused for a taken email or an incomplete CAC record. |
| P35 | Before `HID_NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC_ENABLED` is set in staging, or `NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC=email` anywhere outside a local synthetic run (ADR-064): the DPO and legal sign-off of Novu as the processor of the verified login email (the data processing agreement, the region, US or EU, and the NDPA transfer basis), recorded in ADR-064; the Health-id privacy notice names the processor and what it receives (the verified sign-in email and an opaque patient identifier), and the account deletion screen says email updates stop and the provider may keep the address until it is removed on request; an owner named for the subscriber deletion follow-up and for manual deletions until then; S4w's prerequisites (the staging workflow's Email step and send authorizations); and the P35 check below as the migration administrator: the claim function owned by `hid_event_delivery_commands` and executed by `hid_notification_worker` only, the helper owned by the migration administrator and executed by `hid_event_delivery_commands` only, both `SECURITY DEFINER`. | DPO and legal, product owner, frontend owner, database operator | Not done. Until then no email reaches Novu: the flag is off by default, the worker and `infra/aws` refuse it in production, and the worker is off in production (D17). Lifting the production refusal is a reviewed change after this row, not a setting, and needs the subscriber deletion follow-up built first (ADR-064). Once the flag has been on anywhere, turning it off follows §9 ("Notification worker after the Novu verified email change"). |
| P36 | The patient record release (ADR-065, 0096) stays off in production until each category is ready: no `EHR_PATIENT_RELEASE_*` flag (synth inputs `HID_EHR_PATIENT_RELEASE_*`) is set in production until (1) Health-id renders that section (and, for documents, the download) and the clinical owner has signed off its wording and the proposed defaults in ADR-065 (no review gate before abnormal results or sensitive diagnoses, no withdrawn marker, corrected vitals withheld while D8 is open); (2) for Lab results, Lab is re-enabled by its own reviewed change (D17) and S4x (4) passed on staging; (3) for documents, uploads are re-enabled (D5, D13, I7) and S4x (5) passed; (4) for vitals, diagnoses and prescriptions, S4x (1) to (3) and (6) passed. The EHR production profile refuses every flag and `infra/aws` refuses every input outside staging (the AWS development stack runs ehr-api in the production profile), so a category reaches production only through a reviewed change that records its sign-off and lifts its refusal. On every database, 0096 is followed by the role bootstrap and `npm run db:verify-roles` before any flag is set. | Product owner, clinical owner and database operator | Before setting a flag, run the read-only check below as the migration administrator on staging and then production and attach the output: it must list exactly the 0030, 0084 and 0096 patient policies and no other policy that calls `patient_self_context` (a database built from the PR #8 branch may carry `*_patient_self_read` policies on `ehr.diagnoses`, `ehr.vitals`, `ehr.vital_corrections`, `ehr.documents`, `ehr.document_scan_events` or Lab tables, which would widen the release; drop them by a reviewed change first). Each 0096 policy and the view must follow `app.patient_release`. The view `ehr.patient_released_lab_results` must be owned by `hid_lab_release_reader` with `SELECT` for `hid_ehr_runtime` and the test role only, and no entry of its owner. |

P36 check (read-only), before setting a patient release flag:

```sql
begin transaction isolation level repeatable read read only;
select schemaname || '.' || tablename as relation, policyname,
  qual ilike '%app.patient_release%' as follows_release_flags
from pg_policies
where qual ilike '%patient_self_context%' or with_check ilike '%patient_self_context%'
order by 1, 2;
select c.relname, pg_get_userbyid(c.relowner) as owner, c.relacl, c.reloptions,
  pg_get_viewdef(c.oid) ilike '%app.patient_release%' as follows_release_flags
from pg_class c join pg_namespace n on n.oid = c.relnamespace
where n.nspname = 'ehr' and c.relname = 'patient_released_lab_results';
rollback;
```

Expected: exactly eight rows, the 0030/0084 policies
`encounters_patient_self_read`, `clinical_notes_patient_self_read` and
`note_revisions_patient_self_read` (`follows_release_flags` false), and the
0096 policies `diagnoses_patient_release_read`,
`document_scan_events_patient_release_read`, `documents_patient_release_read`,
`prescriptions_patient_release_read` and `vitals_patient_release_read`
(`follows_release_flags` true); nothing else. The view is owned by
`hid_lab_release_reader`, its ACL is exactly
`{hid_ehr_runtime=r/hid_lab_release_reader,hid_schema_test_runtime=r/hid_lab_release_reader}`
(no entry of the owner itself; the bootstrap leaves it so from its first
run), its options are `{security_barrier=true}` and `follows_release_flags` is
true (checked locally on databases built from empty to 0096 and bootstrapped
once and twice).

P28 check (read-only), before 0093:

```sql
begin transaction isolation level repeatable read read only;
set local statement_timeout = '120s';
set local lock_timeout = '5s';
-- identity.public_patient_enrollments forces row-level security and admits
-- only the system:auth actor: the migration administrator (NOBYPASSRLS on
-- RDS) reads it as that actor, in this read-only transaction only.
select set_config('app.actor_subject', 'system:auth', true);
select count(*) as active_public_enrollments
  from identity.public_patient_enrollments
 where state = 'active';
rollback;
```

P35 check (read-only), after 0095 and its bootstrap:

```sql
begin transaction isolation level repeatable read read only;
set local statement_timeout = '120s';
set local lock_timeout = '5s';
select p.oid::regprocedure as function, p.proowner::regrole as owner, p.prosecdef as security_definer,
       coalesce((select string_agg(case when privilege.grantee = 0 then 'PUBLIC'
                                        else privilege.grantee::regrole::text end, ',')
                   from aclexplode(p.proacl) privilege
                  where privilege.grantee <> p.proowner and privilege.privilege_type = 'EXECUTE'), '') as executors
  from pg_proc p
 where p.oid in ('integration.notification_recipient_for_claim(uuid,uuid,uuid)'::regprocedure,
                 'integration.notification_recipient_state(uuid)'::regprocedure)
 order by 1;
rollback;
```

P9 check (read-only):

```sql
begin transaction isolation level repeatable read read only;
set local statement_timeout = '120s';
set local lock_timeout = '5s';
set local row_security = off;
with latest_scan as (
  select distinct on (scan.document_id) scan.document_id, scan.event_type, scan.object_version_id,
         scan.object_sha256_hex, scan.binding_migration_hold_reason
    from ehr.document_scan_events scan
   order by scan.document_id, scan.created_at desc, scan.id desc)
select job.id as job_id, job.facility_id, job.document_id, job.status, job.attempt_count,
       coalesce(latest_scan.event_type, '<none>') as latest_scan_event, job.created_at, job.updated_at
  from ocr.jobs job left join latest_scan on latest_scan.document_id = job.document_id
 where latest_scan.document_id is null
    or latest_scan.event_type <> 'clean'
    or latest_scan.object_version_id is distinct from job.source_object_version_id
    or latest_scan.object_sha256_hex is distinct from job.source_sha256_hex
    or latest_scan.binding_migration_hold_reason is not null
 order by job.status, job.created_at, job.id
 limit 500;
rollback;
```

P10 check (read-only), before and after 0082:

```sql
begin transaction isolation level repeatable read read only;
set local statement_timeout = '120s';
set local lock_timeout = '5s';
-- platform.control_settings forces row-level security: the migration
-- administrator (NOBYPASSRLS on RDS) sees none of its rows and may not read it
-- with row_security off. platform.control_enabled reads the flag as its
-- technical owner, as every guard does, and raises an error for a missing row.
select 'break_glass_enabled' as control_key, platform.control_enabled('break_glass_enabled') as enabled;
-- No forced row-level security below: row_security off makes the check fail

  from identity.purpose_of_use_codes
 order by code;
rollback;
```

P11 check (read-only), after 0091 and its bootstrap (and any control change):

```sql
begin transaction isolation level repeatable read read only;
set local statement_timeout = '120s';
set local lock_timeout = '5s';
-- platform.control_settings forces row-level security: the migration
-- administrator (NOBYPASSRLS on RDS) sees none of its rows and may not read it
-- with row_security off. platform.control_enabled reads the flag as its
-- technical owner, as every guard does, and raises an error for a missing row.
select control_key, platform.control_enabled(control_key) as enabled
  from (values ('break_glass_enabled'), ('outreach_portal_enabled'), ('uploads_enabled')) launch(control_key)
 order by control_key;
-- No forced row-level security below: row_security off makes the check fail
-- instead of hiding rows if that ever changes.
set local row_security = off;
select code, active, source_system, created_at
  from identity.purpose_of_use_codes
 where code in ('emergency', 'healthcare-operations')
 order by code;
select role_code, permission_code
  from auth.role_permissions
 where permission_code like 'lab.%' or permission_code like 'pharmacy.%'

 order by role_code, permission_code;
select control_key, previous_enabled, enabled, actor_account_id, correlation_id, occurred_at
  from platform.control_events
 where control_key in ('outreach_portal_enabled', 'uploads_enabled', 'break_glass_enabled')
 order by sequence_id desc
```
