P12 check (read-only), before and after 0084:

```sql
begin transaction isolation level repeatable read read only;
set local statement_timeout = '120s';
set local lock_timeout = '5s';
set local row_security = off;
select kind, record_id, record_status, encounter_id, encounter_status, facility_id, patient_id
  from (
    select 'clinical_note' as kind, n.id as record_id, n.status as record_status, e.id as encounter_id,
           e.status as encounter_status, e.facility_id, e.patient_id
      from ehr.clinical_notes n
      join ehr.encounters e on e.id = n.encounter_id and e.patient_id = n.patient_id and e.facility_id = n.facility_id
     where e.status = 'entered_in_error' and n.status in ('draft', 'signed', 'amended')
    union all
    select 'diagnosis', d.id, d.verification_status, e.id, e.status, e.facility_id, e.patient_id
      from ehr.diagnoses d
      join ehr.encounters e on e.id = d.encounter_id and e.patient_id = d.patient_id and e.facility_id = d.facility_id
     where e.status = 'entered_in_error' and d.verification_status <> 'entered_in_error'
    union all
    select 'prescription', p.id, p.status, e.id, e.status, e.facility_id, e.patient_id
      from ehr.prescriptions p
      join ehr.encounters e on e.id = p.encounter_id and e.patient_id = p.patient_id and e.facility_id = p.facility_id
     where e.status in ('cancelled', 'entered_in_error') and p.status in ('draft', 'active', 'on_hold')
    union all
    select 'lab_request', r.id, r.status, e.id, e.status, e.facility_id, e.patient_id
      from ehr.lab_requests r
      join ehr.encounters e on e.id = r.encounter_id and e.patient_id = r.patient_id and e.facility_id = r.facility_id
     where e.status in ('cancelled', 'entered_in_error') and r.status in ('draft', 'active', 'on_hold')
  ) open_record
 order by facility_id, patient_id, encounter_id, kind, record_id
 limit 500;
rollback;
```

### 4.1 P8 follow-up: dedicated technical owners (proposal, Stage 7C)

This is a design proposal, recorded in full in ADR-041. Its
`plpgsql.variable_conflict` item and the bootstrap part of its "no superuser
schema owner" item are implemented by D-Eminence/hid-system#63 (0083 and the role bootstrap, §2;
ADR-041 addendum); the technical owners below are not. It would remove the
run-time superuser requirement of P8 for the functions listed, using the
pattern of `hid_ocr_queue_metrics` (0075), `hid_event_delivery_commands` and
`hid_platform_control_reader`.

- **Functions and owners.** Five non-login, non-inherited, non-bypass roles:
  - `hid_ocr_worker_commands` owns the four worker commands (claim, renew,
    complete, fail), and the test-only wrappers `ocr.claim_next_job`,
    `ocr.record_extraction` and `ocr.fail_job` that call them;
  - `hid_ocr_job_guard` owns the five OCR insert and job guards
    (`validate_job_write`, `validate_extraction_insert`,
    `validate_validation_insert`, `validate_patient_confirmation`,
    `validate_publication_write`);
  - `hid_ocr_job_events` owns `record_job_event`;
  - `hid_document_scan_commands` owns `ehr.append_document_scan_event`;
  - `hid_outreach_campaign_guard` owns
    `outreach.validate_registration_campaign_write`.

  `ocr.worker_queue_metrics()` is already done. These are not all the
  definer functions involved: of the 175 that the migration administrator
  owns, 65 name a table with `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and 101 name any table
  with row-level security, most outside these schemas. The other 36 name only
  identity tables with row-level security but no `FORCE` (`identity.patients`,
  `patient_identifiers`, `access_requests`, `consent_grants`,
  `consent_directives`, `consent_directive_versions`), whose policies do not
  apply to their owner but would apply to a technical owner. Each must
  pass `schema.integration.sql` and the runtime verifiers under the
  non-superuser owner before the requirement is dropped, or get a technical
  owner too.
- **Minimum privileges.**
  - Each role gets `USAGE` on its schemas, grants for the columns its
    functions read, insert or update, and `EXECUTE` on the helpers it calls.
  - The guards read whole rows (`select *`) of `ocr.jobs`, `ocr.validations`
    and `ocr.patient_confirmations`, so they need every column of those
    tables, unless the implementing migration rewrites those reads.
  - A row lock needs `UPDATE` on at least one column.
  - No role owns a relation, and no runtime role is granted a technical role.
- **Row-level security.**
  - Each role gets exact cross-facility policies `TO <role> USING (true)`,
    which is what today's superuser owner sees. The worker, the scanner and
    the job triggers inside worker transactions name no facility.
  - Locking roles get a `FOR UPDATE` policy.
  - A lock-only role also gets a restrictive `FOR UPDATE … WITH CHECK (false)`
    policy. A permissive one would not stop an update that an existing `PUBLIC`
    update policy admits.
  - `ocr.job_events` gets its first insert policy, for `hid_ocr_job_events`
    only.
- **`plpgsql.variable_conflict`.** A new migration replaces
  `ocr.claim_worker_job` with `#variable_conflict use_column` on the first
  line of its body and no 0015 setting. Verified locally on PostgreSQL 16.15:
  - a non-superuser-owned definer that applies 0015's `SET` fails with
    `permission denied to set parameter "plpgsql.variable_conflict"`;
  - the in-body option works under such an owner;
  - a non-superuser can replace the function this way, but cannot
    `ALTER FUNCTION … RESET` the setting.

  This does not let a non-superuser apply 0015 itself, which is also refused
  to it. A database migrated from 0001 by a non-superuser administrator, as on
  RDS, stops at 0015 unless that administrator holds `SET` on the parameter
  (`GRANT SET ON PARAMETER`, PostgreSQL 15 and later). The grant must then
  stay: `ocr.claim_worker_job` applies 0015's setting as its owner on every
  call, so OCR claims fail once it is revoked, until the in-body replacement
  ships. A superuser must grant it, or the RDS master if RDS allows it, which
  is unverified. **Implemented (D-Eminence/hid-system#63):** 0083 is that replacement, so the
  grant is held only for the migration run that applies 0015
  (`MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6.1); whether RDS allows it stays open.
- **No superuser schema owner** (each point verified locally with a
  `NOSUPERUSER CREATEROLE` administrator):
  - the bootstrap must create roles with their attributes and check them, not
    `ALTER ROLE … NOSUPERUSER`;
  - transferring a function needs `SET` on the technical role, and `CREATE` on
    the schema for that role, granted and revoked around the transfer;
  - a later `CREATE OR REPLACE` of a technical-owned function runs as the
    technical role (`SET ROLE`), again with `CREATE` granted for that
    statement only.
  - An `INHERIT` membership would also work, but every technical policy would
    then apply to the migration administrator and to the definer functions it
    still owns. `runtime-roles.integration.sql` would allow exactly the
    administrator's `SET`-only memberships.
  - **Implemented for the bootstrap (D-Eminence/hid-system#63):** roles are created and checked;
    the transfers hold `CREATE` on the schema only around them. The bootstrap
    needs the owner's privileges for its grants and revokes as well (a
    `SET`-only membership is refused them), so it holds `INHERIT` and `SET`
    membership in the technical owners only inside its own transaction, which
    reads no table, and revokes it before committing. No persistent
    membership is granted, so `runtime-roles.integration.sql` allows only the
    creator's `ADMIN OPTION`-only row, and only when the creator is the
    administrator running the check. A later `CREATE OR REPLACE` of a
    technical-owned function is still as proposed here.
- **Migration, rollback, compatibility.**
  - Order: one behaviour-neutral migration (the claim body; explicit columns
    in the scanner command and, if chosen, in the guards), then the bootstrap.
  - No API, worker or contract change.
  - Rollback: return ownership to the migration owner and drop the technical
    policies.
  - The rehearsal's non-superuser owner check would also run
    `schema.integration.sql` and assert each owner. S1 repeats it on RDS.

### 4.2 Release blockers found in Stage 8 (fixed in Stage 9)

**Runtime roles could not run their own locking statements.** `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` and `FOR SHARE` need `UPDATE` privilege on each locked table and, under row-level security, a matching `UPDATE` policy. The runtime roles have only `SELECT` and `INSERT` on most evidence tables, so these statements failed with `42501 permission denied for table ...` before any row was read, and the whole command failed (`5xx`). The defects date from the baseline; unit tests mock the database and no runtime verifier exercised these commands. Stage 9 fixes every path without a new `UPDATE` grant or policy (§2, ADR-042). Line numbers are those on `main` before Stage 9 (`54f79d2`).

| Service, command | Statement (`54f79d2`) | Locked table, runtime role | Why it failed | Stage 9 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Pharmacy: EHR prescription acceptance replay | `pharmacy.service.ts:77` `FOR UPDATE` | `pharmacy.work_items`, `hid_pharmacy_api_runtime` | No `UPDATE`; the table is append-only (trigger) | Lock removed; unique keys per prescription and per key |
| Pharmacy: dispense | `:144`, `:154` `FOR UPDATE` | `work_items`, `dispensings` | No `UPDATE`; append-only | Locks removed; one dispensing per work item |
| Pharmacy: dispensing reversal | `:197`, `:205` `FOR UPDATE` | `dispensings`, `dispensing_reversals` | No `UPDATE`; append-only | Locks removed; one reversal per dispensing |
| Pharmacy: OCR import replay | `:237` `FOR UPDATE` | `imported_medication_evidence` | No `UPDATE`; append-only | Lock removed; one import per publication |
| Lab: import replay | `lab-imports.service.ts:71` `FOR UPDATE` | `lab.imported_evidence`, `hid_lab_api_runtime` | No `UPDATE`; append-only | Lock removed |
| Lab: EHR-order acceptance | `lab-work-items.service.ts:44` `FOR UPDATE` | `lab.work_items` | No `UPDATE`; append-only. Behind it, the work-item child trigger failed with `42703`, and behind that the EHR's millisecond `requestedAt` (`23514`) | Lock removed; trigger repaired by 0078; `requestedAt` sent at the microsecond (EHR to Lab order time, §2) |
| Lab: accession | `lab-accessions.service.ts:42` `FOR UPDATE` | `lab.accessions` | No `UPDATE`; append-only | Lock removed; one accession per work item |
| OCR: validation replay | `ocr.service.ts:258` `FOR UPDATE` | `ocr.validations` (with `ocr.jobs`), `hid_ocr_api_runtime` | No `UPDATE` and no `UPDATE` policy on `validations`; append-only | Lock removed; the job lock taken first serializes |
| OCR: patient confirmation replay | `:346` `FOR UPDATE` | `ocr.patient_confirmations` | No `UPDATE`; append-only | Lock removed; the job lock serializes |
| OCR: publication request | `:561` (`findValidation`) `FOR UPDATE OF validation` | `ocr.validations` | As above | Lock removed; the job lock serializes; publication lock order fixed and terminal failures refused at the claim (§2) |
| Outreach: campaign-linked registration | `outreach.service.ts:270` `FOR SHARE` | `outreach.campaign_members` (with `campaigns`), `hid_outreach_api_runtime` | No `UPDATE` and no `UPDATE` policy on `campaign_members` | `FOR SHARE OF campaign` only |
| Identity: NIN registration review (approve-new, link-existing) | `nin-registration.service.ts:569` `FOR UPDATE` | `identity.registration_cases` with `LEFT JOIN identity.patients`, `hid_identity_api_runtime` | `FOR UPDATE cannot be applied to the nullable side of an outer join` (`0A000`, every role); with an inner join instead, `42501` on `patients`. Behind it, every identifier insert failed on the 0058 policy (`42501`) | Locks the case alone, reads the patient after; the self-NIN policy is dropped by 0078 |

A column-level `UPDATE` grant would not have helped: without an `UPDATE` policy, row-level security hides every row from the lock, so a replay would fall through as if nothing had been stored. Every row these statements locked and no longer lock is insert-only, so no transaction can change it after it becomes visible, with two exceptions: the Identity patient (the projection of the registration case, which is still locked, alone) and the Outreach campaign membership, which no API command changes or deletes and which the registration trigger locks as its owner. The mutable rows some of them also locked stay locked: the OCR job (taken first by every OCR command), the Outreach campaign (`FOR SHARE OF campaign`) and the Identity registration case. Of a row that cannot change, the lock serialized nothing that the unique constraints and `SERIALIZABLE` do not already decide: it cannot lock a row a concurrent request has not committed. The remaining locks of the services are on rows their runtime role may update (for example OCR jobs, Lab specimens, executions and results, Outreach campaigns and registration cases, Identity registration cases); `scripts/verify-runtime-locking-privileges.mjs` checks each one in CI.

## 5. Migration order and lock windows

1. Apply migrations in ledger order with `npm run db:migrate`. Every migration runs in one transaction with `lock_timeout 5s` and `statement_timeout 120s` (each statement), and a failed migration rolls back completely. Never edit an applied migration; checksums are verified. On RDS, a run that starts below 0015 needs `SET ON PARAMETER plpgsql.variable_conflict` granted to the migration administrator by the RDS master for that run, and revoked once 0083 is applied (`MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6.1); without it the run stops at 0015.
2. **0067** adds `access_scope` with a `CHECK` to `audit.events`, then replaces three checks and validates them. This runs in one transaction under an `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock, held to commit, with full-table validation scans. **Reads and writes of `audit.events` are blocked for the whole migration**, which stops nearly every audited request.
3. **0069** drops and re-adds validated `CHECK` constraints on `auth.sessions` and `auth.session_events` under `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE`. Sign-in, refresh and session checks wait while it scans.
4. **0072 builds `audit_resource_uuid_sequence_idx` without `CONCURRENTLY`.** This is possible only inside the runner's transaction. The build holds a `SHARE` lock on `audit.events`:
   - every audited write waits until the build finishes, and that includes most authenticated requests;
   - reads continue.
5. Each of these statements must finish within 120 s, or its migration rolls back and nothing changes. The 5 s `lock_timeout` also fails a migration that cannot get its lock, so stop traffic to Identity, or keep it low, during the window. Measured locally for 0072 only (Stage 5, PostgreSQL 16, synthetic rows, development container, not production hardware): over 1,000,000 audit rows (414 MB, two thirds with a UUID target), the index statement ran in 1.1 s under the runner's settings and produced a 35 MB index. 0067, 0069 and 0074 (step 7) were not measured. Measure all four in the staging rehearsal (S1), scale by the P5 sizes with a wide margin for production I/O, and size the window on the combined cost.
6. **0073** replaces two functions and adds one. It takes no table lock and needs no window. At run time, each session revocation, sign-out and expiry holds its account row lock until it commits, usually milliseconds. Sign-ins, refreshes and sign-outs of that one account wait for that long. Once every Identity instance runs Stage 5B, every path that revokes sessions takes the account row before any session row, so they cannot deadlock with each other. Pre-existing, and unchanged: two administrators changing each other's accounts at the same moment (status, platform role) can deadlock, because each command locks its target and then references its actor. A session revocation can join that cycle when the target's account id sorts before the actor's. PostgreSQL cancels one command, which fails and can be retried; nothing is half applied.
7. **0074** has two effects:
   - It builds `consent_grants_request_idx` without `CONCURRENTLY`, under a `SHARE` lock on `identity.consent_grants`. New grants wait until the build finishes, that is, a patient's approval and a clinician's PIN access. Reads continue. Not measured; measure it with the others in S1.
   - It drops and recreates `identity.list_my_staff_access_requests(text)`, because its result type changes.
8. **0075** creates `ocr.worker_queue_metrics()` (no table lock) and the policy `ocr_outbox_staff_insert`. `CREATE POLICY` takes an `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock on `ocr.outbox_events` for the catalog change only. It waits for in-flight OCR transactions on that table within the 5 s lock timeout. The bootstrap likewise recreates `ocr_jobs_queue_metrics_read` on `ocr.jobs` under a brief `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock, as it already does for the outbox delivery policies.
9. **0076** replaces the trigger functions `ocr.validate_patient_confirmation()` and `ocr.validate_validation_insert()` (`CREATE OR REPLACE`) and creates `auth.lock_account_for_mfa(uuid)`. It takes no table lock and needs no window; an OCR confirmation or validation in flight keeps the definition it started with.
10. **0077** replaces 18 functions (`CREATE OR REPLACE`): two OCR worker commands, the OCR job, job-event and publication triggers, and the Lab, Pharmacy and Outreach guards. It takes no table lock and needs no window. Run P9 first.
11. **0078** drops the policy `patient_identifiers_self_nin_insert` on `identity.patient_identifiers` and replaces `lab.validate_work_item_child()` (`CREATE OR REPLACE`). The policy change takes an `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock on `identity.patient_identifiers` for the catalog change only, waiting for in-flight transactions on that table within the 5 s lock timeout; no table is scanned or rewritten.
12. **0079** (D-Eminence/hid-system#45) replaces `identity.access_patient_with_pin(text, text, integer)` (`CREATE OR REPLACE`). It takes no table lock, needs no window, no bootstrap and no service change. A PIN check in flight keeps the definition it started with. From then on a correct PIN no longer clears the actor's failure window, so a clinician who mistypes PINs for several patients within 15 minutes can reach the 15-minute actor lock (`DATABASE.md`, "Patient access PIN actor window (0079)").
13. **0080** (D-Eminence/hid-system#48) replaces `pharmacy.validate_work_item()` and `pharmacy.validate_dispensing()` (`CREATE OR REPLACE`). It takes no table lock, needs no window and no bootstrap. A Pharmacy command in flight keeps the definition it started with. At run time a dispense or acceptance takes `FOR SHARE` on its EHR prescription row, so an EHR prescription update waits for an in-flight dispense or acceptance, milliseconds. Deploy the Pharmacy API with the `409` mapping before or with it; the EHR API order is unchanged (§3, §6).
14. **0081** (D-Eminence/hid-system#55) creates the table `auth.otp_start_cooldowns` with its primary key. It locks no existing table, scans nothing and needs no window. The bootstrap (step 31) grants the Identity runtime `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on it; only then may the Identity build of that change start (§3, §6).
15. **0082** (D-Eminence/hid-system#58) inserts `direct-care` into `identity.purpose_of_use_codes` (`ON CONFLICT (code) DO NOTHING`). It takes a `ROW EXCLUSIVE` lock on that table only and needs no window, no bootstrap and no service change. Run P10 before and after it. Direct-care access requests, grants and PINs work from its commit; `emergency` and `healthcare-operations` stay unseeded until their decisions (§2).
16. **0083** (D-Eminence/hid-system#63) replaces `ocr.claim_worker_job(text, integer)` (`CREATE OR REPLACE`) with the column-resolution option in its body instead of 0015's setting. It takes no table lock and needs no window, bootstrap or service change; a claim in flight keeps the definition it started with. On RDS the migration administrator needs no parameter privilege for it, and the RDS master revokes the privilege granted for 0015 once it is applied (step 1).
17. **0084** (D-Eminence/hid-system#64) runs in one transaction and holds each lock to its commit, normally well under a second:
    - Before it, run P12 and retract the lab requests it lists that the clinical owner does not keep, through the EHR while `ehr.lab-request.write` is still held; after 0084 no shipped role can change a lab request's status (P12).
    - It deletes the five withdrawn permissions from `auth.role_permissions` (`ROW EXCLUSIVE`; ten rows on a seeded database). From its commit those routes answer `403 PERMISSION_DENIED` for every build (§3).
    - It creates three functions (no table lock) and eight triggers. `CREATE TRIGGER` takes `SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE` on `ehr.clinical_notes`, `ehr.vitals`, `ehr.diagnoses`, `ehr.documents`, `ehr.prescriptions` and `ehr.lab_requests`: reads continue, writes wait.
    - It drops and recreates the two patient-self policies, which takes `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` on `ehr.clinical_notes` and `ehr.clinical_note_revisions` for the catalog change: reads of notes wait too. Nothing is scanned or rewritten.
    - Each lock waits for in-flight transactions on its table within the 5 s lock timeout; a timeout rolls 0084 back whole, and it can be run again. It needs no bootstrap grant. Run P11 and P12 after it, and deploy the EHR API of this change right after it (§6).
18. **0085** (D-Eminence/hid-system#68) replaces `identity.activate_public_patient_enrollment` (`CREATE OR REPLACE`, same signature and grants) and `platform.prune_verification_quotas()`, and creates `platform.public_patient_nin_quota_counters` with its index, policies and the command `platform.consume_public_patient_nin_quota`. It takes no lock on an existing table and needs no window; an activation in flight keeps the definition it started with. The bootstrap (step 31) grants the Identity runtime `EXECUTE` on the command; only then may the Identity build of that change start (§3, §6).
19. **0086** (D-Eminence/hid-system#69) replaces `auth.complete_recovery_otp` and `identity.create_access_request` (`CREATE OR REPLACE`, owner and ACL kept), and `identity.list_my_staff_access_requests` (`CREATE OR REPLACE`, owner and ACL kept), drops and creates again the policy `patients_registration_read`, creates four functions, re-adds `otp_rate_limits_scope_check` and builds `otp_challenges_account_consumed_idx`. Locks, in that order, each held until 0086 commits: `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` on `identity.patients` (every HID sign-in, recovery start, registration read, patient lookup and access request waits); `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` on `auth.otp_rate_limits` while the check validates every row (OTP starts, MFA attempts and enrollment budgets wait); `SHARE` on `auth.otp_challenges` while the index builds (recovery starts, verifications and completions wait; one row per challenge, so it should be short; measure it in S1). The order matches an OTP start of the serving Identity, so a start cannot deadlock with it; a recovery completion caught inside `auth.complete_recovery_otp` between its challenge update and its patient read still can, and PostgreSQL then cancels one of the two (`40P01`): the completion fails and the owner submits it again, or 0086 rolls back whole and `migrate` is run again. A lock wait over the runner's 5 s `lock_timeout` (`55P03`) also rolls 0086 back whole; run `migrate` again. Keep Identity traffic low. The bootstrap (step 31) grants the four functions; only then may the Identity build of this change start (§3, §6). Before it, record the count of §10 ("Patient accounts without a verified contact").
20. **0087** (D-Eminence/hid-system#70) first takes a `SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE` lock on `identity.staff_facility_memberships` and checks for two active, unheld memberships of one account at one facility; if any exist it raises `23505 MEMBERSHIP_DUPLICATES_FOUND` and changes nothing (run I4 first, P1). It then builds the unique index `staff_memberships_one_active_per_facility_uq` and drops and recreates `staff_memberships_scoped_uq`: the drop takes an `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock on the memberships table, which every staff request reads, until commit, so apply it in the window; the table is small and the builds take milliseconds. It creates three empty tables and the workforce functions, adds a trigger to the memberships table, and replaces `auth.membership_has_permission` (`CREATE OR REPLACE`, same signature). The bootstrap (step 31) grants the commands and `auth.facility_role_is_effective` to the Identity runtime; only then may the Identity build of this change start (§3, §6).
21. **0088** (D-Eminence/hid-system#71) adds `session_kind` and five proof columns, with two checks, to `auth.mfa_login_challenges`; adds `enrollment_proof` and `enrollment_challenge_id`, with one check and the trigger `mfa_factors_enrollment_proof_guard`, to `auth.mfa_factors`; and replaces `auth.guard_mfa_login_challenge_change` and `auth.guard_session_assurance_change` (`CREATE OR REPLACE`, owner, ACL and triggers kept). It holds `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` locks on `auth.mfa_login_challenges` and then `auth.mfa_factors` until it commits: the columns need no rewrite, the checks scan the tables (the challenges and factors of platform sign-in so far). Platform sign-in steps wait meanwhile. No grant or bootstrap change (the new trigger function is revoked from `PUBLIC` in the migration). Then run the P22 queries. Deploy the Identity build of this change after it (§3, §6); from that deploy every existing staff session is refused and ended (§2).
22. **0089** (D-Eminence/hid-system#73, written as 0080 in D-Eminence/hid-system#47) creates `auth.session_facility_selection`, its guard function and trigger. Creating its four foreign keys takes `SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE` on `auth.sessions`, `auth.accounts`, `identity.facilities` and `identity.staff_facility_memberships` until 0089 commits (catalog changes only, so briefly): sign-ins, refreshes, sign-outs and workforce changes wait for it, and it waits for in-flight writes to those tables within the 5 s lock timeout, after which it rolls back whole and can be run again. No row changes and nothing is scanned. The bootstrap (step 31) grants the Identity runtime `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE`; only then may the Identity build of that change start (§3, §6).
23. **0090** (D-Eminence/hid-system#74) runs in one transaction and holds each lock to its commit, normally well under a second:
    - It adds `ehr.vitals.status` with a constant default and a check constraint: `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` on `ehr.vitals` for the catalog change and a scan that validates the check, without rewriting the table (no version is recorded). Reads and writes of vitals wait.
    - It replaces `ehr.keep_recorded_vital()` (`CREATE OR REPLACE`), creates five functions, and creates six triggers: `SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE` on `ehr.encounters`, `ehr.prescriptions`, `ehr.lab_requests`, `ehr.vital_corrections` and `ehr.documents` (reads continue, writes wait).
    - Each lock waits for in-flight transactions on its table within the 5 s lock timeout; a timeout rolls 0090 back whole, and it can be run again. It changes no row. Run the bootstrap (step 31) right after it, then deploy the EHR API of this change (§6).
24. **0091** (D-Eminence/hid-system#75, ADR-058; numbered after 0089 and 0090 of other launch changes, which the ledger must hold first) runs in one transaction and holds each lock to its commit, normally well under a second:
    - It lifts forced row-level security on `platform.control_settings` (`ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` until commit: every service's control read waits, so keep traffic low), sets `uploads_enabled`, `outreach_portal_enabled` and `break_glass_enabled` off where each is still exactly as 0039 seeded it, and forces row-level security again. It takes this lock first, in the order of a break-glass activation and the role bootstrap. No control event is written (no actor); P11 checks the result.
    - It deletes every `lab.*`, `pharmacy.*`, `ocr.*` and `outreach.*` role permission, `identity.break-glass.write` and `ehr.document.write` (`ROW EXCLUSIVE` on `auth.role_permissions`; 105 rows on a seeded database). From its commit those routes answer `403 PERMISSION_DENIED` for every build, and staff sessions lose the permissions on their next request (§2, §3).
    - It replaces `ehr.patient_self_context` and `identity.patient_self_access_history` (`CREATE OR REPLACE`, owner and ACL kept) and drops and recreates `identity.list_my_access_requests` and `identity.list_my_patient_access_requests`: until the bootstrap (step 31) grants `EXECUTE` again, every Identity build answers a patient's `GET /identity/me/access-requests` with `403`.
    - It creates the trigger `pin_use_notification_inbox` on `identity.outbox_events` (`SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE`: Identity's outbox writes, such as a registration, PIN access or break-glass, wait to commit) and its function (revoked from `PUBLIC`).
    - Each lock waits for in-flight transactions within the 5 s lock timeout; a timeout rolls 0091 back whole, and it can be run again. Run P11 after the bootstrap. Deploy the EHR and Identity builds of this change after it (§6); earlier builds keep working (§3).
25. **0092** (D-Eminence/hid-system#82, ADR-061; numbered after 0091) drops `identity.activate_public_patient_enrollment(uuid,char(64),text,text,text,text,char(64))` and creates it again with two more arguments, `requested_date_of_birth date` and `requested_gender text`, both default null, with 0085's owner, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path`, result and body apart from the profile check, and revokes it from `PUBLIC`. It takes no table lock and needs no window; an activation in flight keeps the definition it started with. Dropping the function drops its grants: until the bootstrap (step 31) grants `EXECUTE` on the new signature, every Identity build answers an enrollment activation `403 ENROLLMENT_DENIED` and writes nothing, so run the bootstrap right after it. Deploy the Identity build of this change only after both (§3, §6).
26. **0093** (D-Eminence/hid-system#84, ADR-062; numbered after 0092). Before it, run P28: production must hold zero active public enrollments, or stop. It runs in one transaction and holds each lock to its commit:
    - `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` on `identity.public_patient_enrollments` and then `identity.public_patient_enrollment_otps`, for the catalog changes, the backfill and the scans that validate the new checks. Every enrollment request waits. The tables hold only unfinished enrollments of the last 24 hours and their codes, so the scans are short; measure them in S1.
    - It drops the unique constraint on the enrollment NIN, adds the plain and partial unique indexes, the holder columns and checks, the code `purpose`, the table `identity.public_patient_holder_events` and three commands, and replaces the prune and the 9-argument activation command (`CREATE OR REPLACE`, owner and ACL kept).
    - Backfill, with forced row-level security lifted on the two tables for it only: open codes of unfinished enrollments are invalidated, those enrollments go back to `verify_holder` with their contact cleared, and active ones are marked `none_before_0093`. A patient half way through enrollment starts the holder step again.
    - Each lock waits for in-flight transactions within the 5 s lock timeout; a timeout rolls 0093 back whole, and it can be run again.
    - Lock order at run time: the proof command, the activation command and the Identity build of this change take the NIN advisory lock (0092's key, `patient-nin:` and the NIN lookup HMAC) before the enrollment row lock. The send and failure commands take the row and then the holder budget locks; Identity holds the NIN lock before it calls them. The prune at every start locks expired enrollments, skipping any another transaction holds, before it deletes their codes, the proof's order. Two proofs, a proof and an activation, or a proof and a prune of one NIN wait for each other for milliseconds and do not deadlock. An earlier Identity build never waits for the NIN lock while it holds an enrollment row lock, and it calls no holder command, so it cannot join that cycle.
    - Until the bootstrap (step 31) grants `EXECUTE` on the three commands and replaces the runtime's table-level `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on the enrollment table with column lists, no holder code can be sent or checked. Run the bootstrap straight after it, then deploy the notification-api build of this change, then the Identity build (§6).
27. **0094** (D-Eminence/hid-system#85, ADR-063; numbered after 0093). It runs in one transaction and holds each lock to its commit:
    - `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` on `identity.public_patient_enrollments` while it adds `desk_code_salt` and `desk_code_issues` and three checks. No rewrite; the checks scan the table, which holds only enrollments of the last 24 hours. Every enrollment request waits.
    - It creates the designation table and its events, the in-person checks table and its guard trigger, the participation, attestation and staff-session helpers, the facility list, the staff command and the two designation commands; inserts one permission and three role grants (`ROW EXCLUSIVE` on `auth.permissions` and `auth.role_permissions`); and replaces the holder proof and the 9-argument activation command (`CREATE OR REPLACE`, owner and ACL kept).
    - Each lock waits for in-flight transactions within the 5 s lock timeout; a timeout rolls 0094 back whole, and it can be run again. It changes no existing row.
    - Lock order at run time: the in-person proof takes the NIN lock before the enrollment row, as 0093. The staff command takes a staff advisory lock and then a facility advisory lock; the designation command takes a designation lock and then the facility row `FOR SHARE`.
    - Until the bootstrap (step 31) grants `EXECUTE` on the four new commands, the facility list, the staff check and the designation routes fail for the Identity of this change. An earlier Identity keeps working: it calls the replaced commands with the same signatures and never the new ones. Run the bootstrap straight after it, then deploy the Identity build (§6).
28. **0095** (D-Eminence/hid-system#90, ADR-064). It first refuses (`55000 BRANCH_0063_NOTIFICATION_RECIPIENT_PRESENT`) a database that ran the D-Eminence/hid-system#8 branch's 0063, whose recipient functions return names: rebuild or reconcile such a database first, never edit the guard. It creates two functions, takes no table lock, changes no row and needs no window.
    - Until the bootstrap (step 31) moves the claim function to `hid_event_delivery_commands` and grants it to the worker, a notification worker with `NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC=email` refuses to start. With the flag off, the default and the only value production accepts, every worker build is unaffected.
29. **0096** (the patient record release, ADR-065; owner decision 4 of 2026-10-16; numbered after 0095, the Novu verified email change). It runs in one transaction and holds each lock to its commit:
    - `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` on `ehr.vitals`, `ehr.diagnoses`, `ehr.prescriptions`, `ehr.documents` and `ehr.document_scan_events`, one after the other, for each `CREATE POLICY`: every EHR read and write of that table waits. No scan and no rewrite.
    - `CREATE VIEW ehr.patient_released_lab_results` takes `ACCESS SHARE` on the seven Lab tables, `ehr.lab_requests` and `ehr.encounters`; `CREATE INDEX lab_result_releases_patient_idx` takes `SHARE` on `lab.result_releases` and scans it (no rows on a launch database, Lab is off).
    - `CREATE INDEX vitals_patient_release_idx`, `diagnoses_patient_release_idx` and `prescriptions_patient_release_idx` take `SHARE` on `ehr.vitals`, `ehr.diagnoses` and `ehr.prescriptions` and scan each table: reads go on, but every EHR write of those tables (recording a vital, a diagnosis or a prescription, and their corrections and retractions) waits until 0096 commits. On a launch database these tables are small; estimate the scan from their sizes before the release (step 30).
    - Each lock waits for in-flight transactions within the 5 s lock timeout; a timeout rolls 0096 back whole, and it can be run again. It changes no existing row.
    - Until the bootstrap (step 31) gives the view to `hid_lab_release_reader` and grants the EHR runtime `SELECT` on it, an EHR build of this change with the Lab results flag on answers `503 PATIENT_RECORDS_UNAVAILABLE`. With every flag off (the default) no build reads anything new. Run the bootstrap straight after it, then P36's check, then deploy the EHR build (§6).
30. If any estimate approaches 120 s, stop and re-plan before the release. 0072 cannot be edited or worked around in place: an index built beforehand under the same name makes 0072 fail. Do not raise the timeouts ad hoc.
31. **Immediately after the migrations, run `npm run db:bootstrap`, then `npm run db:verify-roles`, then `npm run db:verify-logins` with every runtime credential (`MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6.2, §6.3).**
   - 0072 drops and recreates `audit.list_platform_events` with eleven arguments. Until the bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on the new signature, any running Identity is refused on the platform audit list.
   - The bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on `identity.list_my_staff_access_requests(text)` to the Identity runtime (D-Eminence/hid-system#26). Before that grant existed, the clinician list was refused (`403`) everywhere. 0074 recreates the function, so after 0074 the list is refused for every Identity build until the bootstrap runs.
   - The bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on `auth.lock_account_sessions(uuid)` (0073) to the Identity runtime. A Stage 5B Identity running before this, or before 0073, fails with a database error (`5xx`) on every sign-out, every refresh of an expired session, an administrator's own-session revocation and refresh-token reuse; a reused family is then not revoked. Earlier Identity builds do not call it.
   - Between this step and step 6, and during a rolling Identity deploy, an earlier Identity runs against 0073. Its sign-out, expired refresh, refresh-reuse revocation and own-session revocation lock session rows before the account row, so each can deadlock with an administrator's revocation of the same account at the same moment. PostgreSQL cancels one of the two, which fails with an error and can be retried (§9). Keep that interval short.
   - The bootstrap creates `hid_ocr_queue_metrics`, makes it the owner of `ocr.worker_queue_metrics()`, creates its policy and grants the OCR worker `EXECUTE` (0075). Until then a Stage 6 worker logs `ocr.queue.metrics_unavailable`, as every worker does today.
   - The bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on `auth.lock_account_for_mfa(uuid)` (0076) to the Identity runtime. A Stage 7 Identity running before this, or before 0076, fails every platform MFA transaction with a database error (`5xx`). Earlier Identity builds do not call it.
   - The bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on `auth.account_id_for_subject(text)` to the Outreach runtime (Stage 8). Until then every Outreach write fails with `42501`, as it always has.
   - The bootstrap grants `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on `auth.otp_start_cooldowns` (0081) to the Identity runtime, and no `DELETE`. An Identity with the recovery start fix running before this, or before 0081, fails every recovery start (`5xx`).
   - The bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on the five workforce commands and `auth.facility_role_is_effective(uuid)` (0087) to the Identity runtime, and its assertions require them. An Identity with governed workforce provisioning running before this, or before 0087, fails every staff sign-in and request (`5xx`).
   - The bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on `auth.recovery_account_audience`, `identity.registration_nin_holder`, `identity.registration_candidates` and `identity.patient_lookup_audit_subject` (0086) to the Identity runtime only. An Identity with the recovery and patient search hardening running before this, or before 0086, fails every recovery start, NIN registration and refused patient lookup (`5xx`).
   - The bootstrap grants `SELECT`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on `auth.session_facility_selection` (0089) to the Identity runtime, and no `DELETE`; on a database without 0089 that grant fails and the bootstrap stops, so run it after the migrations. An Identity with the facility selection port running before this, or before 0089, answers every staff `GET /auth/session` `503`, every staff refresh `200` with `unavailable` (the portal asks where the user works at each reload) and `POST /auth/facility` a `5xx`.
   - The bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on `platform.consume_public_patient_nin_quota(char,integer,integer,integer)` (0085) to the Identity runtime, and nothing on its counters. An Identity with the enrollment gates change running before this, or before 0085, answers every enrollment start that reaches QoreID `503 VERIFICATION_QUOTA_UNAVAILABLE`, and calls QoreID for none of them.
   - The bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on `auth.membership_has_permission(text, uuid, uuid, text)` to the EHR runtime (0090). Until then, after 0090, cancelling or retracting an encounter that holds records fails in the database for every EHR build (`403 ACCESS_DENIED` or `500`; nothing written).
   - The bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on the recreated `identity.list_my_patient_access_requests(text,uuid)` (0091) to the Identity runtime again; until then every Identity build answers a patient's request list with `403`.
   - The bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on the recreated `identity.activate_public_patient_enrollment(uuid,char,text,text,text,text,char,date,text)` (0092) to the Identity runtime again, and to no other runtime; until then every Identity build answers an enrollment activation `403 ENROLLMENT_DENIED`.
   - The bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on `identity.record_public_patient_holder_send`, `_failure` and `_proof` (0093) to the Identity runtime only, gives it no privilege on `identity.public_patient_holder_events`, and replaces its table-level `INSERT` and `UPDATE` on `identity.public_patient_enrollments` with column lists that leave out the holder columns. The blanket revocations at the top of `runtime-grants.sql` remove the earlier table-level grants. An Identity of this change running before this answers every holder send and check `403 ENROLLMENT_DENIED`.
   - The bootstrap grants `EXECUTE` on `identity.list_participating_facilities`, `identity.record_assisted_verification`, `identity.admin_facility_assisted_registration` and `identity.admin_set_facility_assisted_registration` (0094) to the Identity runtime only, adds `desk_code_salt` and `desk_code_issues` to its `UPDATE` column list, and gives no role a privilege on the designation, its events or the in-person checks. An Identity of the facility-assisted change running before this answers the facility list and the designation routes `5xx`, and a desk code `5xx`.
   - The bootstrap moves `integration.notification_recipient_for_claim(uuid,uuid,uuid)` (0095) to `hid_event_delivery_commands` and grants `EXECUTE` on it to `hid_notification_worker` only, and on `integration.notification_recipient_state(uuid)` to `hid_event_delivery_commands` only; its assertions require exactly those holders and no worker privilege on `identity.patients` or `auth.accounts`. Until it runs, a worker with `NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC=email` refuses to start; with the flag off nothing changes.
   - The bootstrap creates `hid_lab_release_reader` (`NOLOGIN`, no member, no schema `USAGE`), grants it column `SELECT` on what the view reads, creates its eight policies `patient_release_reader_read` on the seven Lab tables and `ehr.lab_requests` (on its first run only: `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` briefly on each, after the platform-control policy; a rerun replaces only a missing or different one), makes it the owner of `ehr.patient_released_lab_results` inside a `CREATE` window on schema `ehr`, revokes the owner's own entry on the view (left by the migration's `REVOKE ... FROM PUBLIC`), and grants `SELECT` on the view to the EHR runtime and the test role only (0096), so the first run already leaves P36's expected ACL; its assertions require exactly these privileges, that ACL with no owner entry, and that `ehr.patient_self_context` is still executable by the EHR runtime and the test role only.
   - The bootstrap also runs the runtime-role assertions. From Stage 9 they require 0078 (no self-NIN insert policy), from the recovery start fix the 0081 table, from D-Eminence/hid-system#64 that no role holds a permission 0084 withdraws, from the enrollment gates change the 0085 command, from the recovery and patient search hardening the 0086 functions, from governed workforce provisioning the 0087 commands, and from the launch module permissions (0091) that no role holds a `lab.*`, `pharmacy.*`, `ocr.*` or `outreach.*` permission, `identity.break-glass.write` or `ehr.document.write`, that `ehr.patient_self_context` is a pinned security-definer check, and the PIN-use trigger, so run the bootstrap and `db:verify-roles` only after the migrations. Before 0091 they fail with "a role holds a permission withdrawn by the launch module set". Before 0084 they fail with "a role holds a write permission withdrawn by the launch module set".
   - Since D-Eminence/hid-system#63 the bootstrap runs as the non-superuser migration administrator on RDS; it must be the role that created the HID roles (`MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6.2). It creates `hid_platform_control_reader`, makes it the owner of `platform.control_enabled(text)` and creates its policy `platform_controls_definer_read`. That `CREATE POLICY` takes an `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock on `platform.control_settings`, before any other table lock of the bootstrap, and holds it until the bootstrap's transaction commits. The 5 s lock timeout bounds only the bootstrap's own wait for it; the guards' control reads (every Identity, EHR and Outreach request that is not a platform administrator's) queue behind the lock until the commit, within the services' 15 s statement timeout. Locally, on a synthetic database, the rest of the transaction took about 0.1 s; run that first bootstrap with traffic low. Because the lock comes first, a break-glass activation in flight (control read, then `identity.outbox_events`) and the bootstrap wait for each other in one order and do not deadlock. Later runs find the policy and take no lock on `platform.control_settings`. Run `db:verify-roles` as the same administrator: it allows the creator's `ADMIN OPTION`-only row only for the role running it (`MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6.2). Create the runtime LOGINs as that administrator too: only it may grant the aggregate roles, and the RDS master is refused (`MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6.3). Until the bootstrap has run, a schema owner without `BYPASSRLS` answers `PLATFORM_CONTROL_UNAVAILABLE` (`503`) to every patient and provider request, as before.
   - `db:verify-logins` must pass for all ten runtime credentials before any service starts against the database; in production each service also refuses to start over a superuser, `BYPASSRLS` or owner connection (§2).

## 6. Deployment order

1. Pass the pre-release checks P1–P12 (P11 after 0091 and its bootstrap; P12 after 0084 and also before it), P15–P20 before any production deploy of the regional stack (ADR-053; P16 before its synth; P19 after S4m (6)), P21 after the migrations and before ingress, and P13 before any production service-to-service call is expected to work (ADR-047, accepted; I5 and I6 not run). The §4.2 blockers are fixed by Stage 9; release the Stage 9 builds of the services that had them. P22 (staff MFA enrolment) runs after 0088 and gates enabling clinical roles, not the deploy.
2. Deploy the **admin console** (Health-id `main`): the Health-id build staged for admin.* (P14) on `hid-admin`, and the same release's builds for www or staging on `hid-web` (ADR-052). If the running Identity is Stage 2A or later, it keeps working with it (§3). If it is older, admin sign-in fails until step 6 whichever console runs; deploy the console with Identity instead.
3. Take the backup (P2).
4. Apply the **migrations** (§5). When the release includes 0080, deploy the Pharmacy API of step 7 first if possible: it works on the earlier schema; otherwise, until step 7, the 0080 refusals answer `500` instead of `409` and nothing is written. The EHR API keeps step 9: 0080 does not compare the prescription time or dates, so earlier EHR builds keep handing off (§3). When the release includes 0084, deploy the EHR API of step 9 immediately after the migrations: until it runs, the refusals of 0084 answer `500` instead of `409`, retracting an encounter does not cascade, and no clinical note can be created (§3). When the release includes 0091, run P11 after the bootstrap (step 5): 0091 turns the three launch controls off where they were still seeded; set any that is still on false through `POST /admin/controls`. Then run P12. Deploy the EHR and Identity builds of 0091 at steps 6 and 9 (§3).
5. Run the **role bootstrap** and verify it, then verify every runtime credential with `db:verify-logins` (§5 step 31). The notification worker of the Novu verified email change (ADR-064) deploys in any order with `NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC=disabled`; in staging, deploy it with `HID_NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC_ENABLED=true` only after this step and P35, or it refuses to start (§3). Since the non-superuser database chain a service in production refuses to start over a connection that is a superuser, bypasses row-level security or has an owner's privileges, and exits if its database is unreachable at start-up (§2).
6. With the NIN holder proof change (D-Eminence/hid-system#84), first deploy **notification-api** with that change: it adds the `NIN_HOLDER_VERIFY` purpose, SMS only, and an earlier notification-api refuses it (P24). It needs no migration and changes no other purpose. Then deploy **Identity**, with `MFA_SECRET_KEY_B64`, `MFA_KEY_VERSION` and `CORS_ORIGINS`. The regional stack supplies all three since D-Eminence/hid-system#49; the `mfaSecretKeyB64` field must be in the Identity sensitive secret before that stack is deployed, or no Identity task starts (P3). A Stage 5B or later Identity must not start before steps 4 and 5 (0073 and its grant), a Stage 7 Identity not before 0076 and its grant, an Identity with the governed-access outcome mapping must not start before 0074 and the bootstrap, an Identity with the recovery start fix not before 0081 and the bootstrap, and an Identity with the enrollment gates change not before 0085 and the bootstrap; it also refuses to start with `QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED=true` in production, and an Identity with the recovery and patient search hardening not before 0086 and the bootstrap, and an Identity with governed workforce provisioning not before 0087 and the bootstrap: it calls `auth.facility_role_is_effective` on every staff sign-in and request, and an Identity with staff MFA not before 0088 and not before the Health-id provider portal with the staff MFA sign-in is live (§3); that deploy signs every staff member out (§2); and an Identity with the facility selection port not before 0089 and the bootstrap (§3). An Identity with the launch module permissions change (0091) must not start before 0091 and the bootstrap: on an earlier schema a patient's request list answers `500` (§3). An Identity with the self-reported enrollment profile change (D-Eminence/hid-system#82) must not start before 0092 and the bootstrap: before 0092 every enrollment activation answers `503 ENROLLMENT_UNAVAILABLE` (§3); in production NIN-only enrollment stays off, so no activation is reached there. An Identity with the NIN holder proof change (D-Eminence/hid-system#84) must not start before 0093, the bootstrap and the notification-api build of that change (P24): on 0092 its enrollment routes fail at their first query of a column or command 0093 adds (`5xx`), the database does not yet require a holder proof, and an earlier notification-api refuses the holder purpose. Its configuration rules replace the production refusal above: it starts in production with NIN-only enrollment on only with a holder route, refuses `NIN_HOLDER_SMS_ENABLED` there until the facility route ships (ADR-063; `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED` has no effect before then), and refuses `QOREID_PATIENT_SELF_NIN_BINDING_ENABLED` there (P25, P27). With that Identity every start on staging and production answers `503 PATIENT_REGISTRATION_NOT_OPEN` (§3). An Identity with facility-assisted registration (D-Eminence/hid-system#85) must not start before 0094 and the bootstrap: on 0093 its designation routes fail (`5xx`), and with `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED` on so do availability, start and the facility list. Its production rule replaces the refusal above: `NIN_HOLDER_SMS_ENABLED` needs `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED` (P25). Switch `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED` on only after P29 to P33, and designate facilities after the switch is ready (§10).
7. Deploy the **Pharmacy API**, **Lab API** and **Outreach API** (Stage 9). They are independent of the other steps; Lab acceptance of an EHR order works once 0078 is applied, and the Outreach API writes once the Stage 8 bootstrap grant is in place. The Pharmacy API with the 0080 `409` mapping goes before or with 0080 (step 4).
8. Deploy the **OCR API** and the **OCR worker** (Stage 6 and 9). The API is independent of the other steps. The worker reports queue metrics once steps 4 and 5 (0075 and its grant) are done.
9. Deploy the **EHR API**, with the clinical list paging fix and D-Eminence/hid-system#38, alongside the Pharmacy, Lab and OCR APIs of steps 7 and 8, which carry #38 too. It needs no migration or bootstrap (§3), and is otherwise independent of the other steps, except that an EHR API with D-Eminence/hid-system#56 deploys after the Lab API of step 7 that carries it: before that Lab, the new EHR reports some Lab answers as `failed`, `502` or `409 LAB_HANDOFF_REFUSED` instead of `already_accepted` or a work item (§3). With #38 they report the method of the request they serve; a service's same-origin cookie reads work once it and Identity both run #38, and OCR's once EHR does too (§3).
10. If the Outreach API is deployed (step 7), deploy it with the Outreach cookie-read change (D-Eminence/hid-system#43), and deploy Identity (step 6) with the same change. Neither needs a migration or bootstrap beyond §5. Outreach's same-origin cookie reads work only once both run it; either alone behaves as before, and the reads are refused (§3). Its campaign reads need the change in Identity. Its campaign writes answer `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` with either build until the campaign governance decision (§2, ADR-045).
11. Deploy the **edge and gateway change** (D-Eminence/hid-system#65) in this order. (a) Every deployed Cloudflare frontend Worker that proxies `/api/v1`: at launch only web and admin (ADR-052, `infra/cloudflare/launch-deployment.json`); the EHR, Lab, Pharmacy, OCR and Outreach hosts are not deployed. They only set `X-Real-IP`, which an earlier gateway does not read. Confirm from each deployed Worker's deployment record that it runs this release before (b). (b) The gateway image together with the regional stack that sets `VPC_CIDR`, `GATEWAY_DNS_RESOLVER`, `HID_REQUIRE_CLIENT_IP=true` and `HID_ENABLED_API_ROUTES` (production: synthesized without the synth input, so `identity,ehr`; staging: with the synth input naming every module the acceptance run needs, `identity,ehr,lab,pharmacy,ocr,outreach` for S4b, S4c, S4d and S4g, otherwise those rows answer `404 API_ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`). Behind any Worker from before (a), not redeployed or rolled back, this gateway answers `403 CLIENT_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` to every API request from that host; it never trusts a caller-supplied address. Right after (b), on each deployed host, send an API request carrying `X-Real-IP: 192.0.2.1`: it must not answer `403 CLIENT_ADDRESS_REQUIRED`, and Identity's request log must show your public address, not `192.0.2.1` and not a `VPC_CIDR` address. (c) Identity with the change, only after (b): behind an earlier gateway every public IP-keyed request in production answers `503`. A module outside the enabled list answers `404 API_ROUTE_NOT_FOUND` from (b) on, whatever its desired count.
    With D-Eminence/hid-system#72 (ADR-055), after #65 is in place: (d) the two launch Workers (`hid-web`, `hid-admin`; HSTS, the portal Content-Security-Policy, the API policy, `X-HID-Client-Network`). The only request change is that header, which the WAF of (e) reads and an earlier gateway passes on unread; deploy them with the release's staged Health-id builds and open each host's sign-in page with the browser console open: no Content Security Policy violation, and the Turnstile widget renders. (e) The gateway image and the regional stack together (the WAF rules, the body bound, the access-log format and error-log level, the scanner-callback denial, the pass-through of backend 5xx). The per-client WAF rule needs the Workers of (d) (they set `X-HID-Client-Network` with `X-Real-IP`): deploy (d) before (e); behind a Worker that does not set it, those requests are counted only by the backstop. Keep Cloudflare Pseudo IPv4 off or in "add header" mode. A log pipeline that parsed the `main` format must read the `hid_api` JSON lines from (e) on.
12. Run the smoke checks:
    - a Super Admin signs in with TOTP;
    - a high-risk action asks for step-up;
    - `GET /admin/audit/events?resourceType=facility` answers `200`;
    - `GET /admin/approvals` returns `{ items, nextCursor }`;
    - a clinician's `GET /identity/access-requests` answers `200`, and each item has `effectiveStatus`;
    - with #38: a clinician's same-origin cookie `GET /ehr/patients/:id/encounters`, for a patient whose grant is active, answers `200` and not `403 ORIGIN_DENIED`; a cookie write with an allowed `Origin` and no `X-CSRF-Token` is still refused `403 CSRF_VALIDATION_FAILED`;
    - with the EHR paging fix, as a clinician with active access to a patient who has at least two encounters in the facility, sending `X-Facility-ID` and `X-Purpose-Of-Use: direct-care`: `GET /ehr/patients/:id/encounters?limit=1` returns a `nextCursor`, and the same request with that `cursor` returns the next encounter. The same cursor sent with another patient's ID answers `400 INVALID_CURSOR`; EHR checks the cursor before it asks Identity to authorize the read, so this needs no access to that patient;
    - CloudWatch shows no `MFA_UNAVAILABLE`;
    - once the OCR worker has claimed a job, its `ocr.job.claimed` logs carry `queueDepth` and `oldestQueueAgeSeconds`, and no `ocr.queue.metrics_unavailable` follows a claim. A worker logs neither while it claims nothing;
    - if the Outreach API is deployed and, with the edge and gateway change, `outreach` is in the gateway's `HID_ENABLED_API_ROUTES` (staging only until MOD-1 is decided; otherwise these routes answer `404 API_ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`): a registration without a campaign and a campaign-linked registration (`POST /outreach/registration-cases`) answer `2xx`, and PostgreSQL logs no `permission denied for function account_id_for_subject` or `for table campaign_members`;
    - with the Outreach cookie-read change, if the Outreach API is deployed and its routes are enabled as in the previous check: a staff member with the Outreach permissions, signed in with a cookie session and sending `X-Facility-ID`, gets `200` on a same-origin cookie `GET /outreach/registration-cases` and `GET /outreach/registration-cases/campaigns`, not `403`; a cookie write with an allowed `Origin` and no `X-CSRF-Token` is still refused `403 CSRF_VALIDATION_FAILED`.
    - once a NIN registration case needs review: an approve-new or link-existing review answers `201` (S4h), and PostgreSQL logs no `permission denied for table verification_evidence` and no `42501` on `identity.patient_identifiers`;
    - with the recovery start fix: two `POST /auth/otp/start` for the same unknown synthetic address within 60 s answer `202` with the same `challengeId`, and PostgreSQL logs no `relation "auth.otp_start_cooldowns" does not exist` or `permission denied for table otp_start_cooldowns`;
    - with D-Eminence/hid-system#64: a doctor's `POST /ehr/patients/:id/encounters/:encounterId/lab-requests` with the body `{}`, for a patient they have access to, answers `403 PERMISSION_DENIED`. The permission guard runs before body validation, so nothing can be written; a `400` instead means the permission is still held: stop and check P11. The write and retraction checks run on staging only (S4j);
    - with the edge and gateway change: a recovery start from a browser through the Worker answers `202`, and a synthetic sign-in through the Worker records the client's public address, not a `VPC_CIDR` address, as `source_ip` in `auth.session_events`; `GET /api/v1/lab/work-items` answers `404 API_ROUTE_NOT_FOUND` while Lab is not enabled; `POST /api/v1/identity/Service/authorization/check` answers `404 API_ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`; after an Identity task is replaced, `/api/v1/auth/session` keeps answering without a gateway redeploy;
    - with the enrollment gates change, while NIN-only enrollment is off (production): `POST /identity/patient-enrollments` with a valid Turnstile token and a synthetic NIN answers `503 QOREID_DISABLED` or `503 QOREID_NIN_ONLY_DISABLED`, the same for a second synthetic NIN, and no `identity.public-patient-enrollment.nin-lookup-quota` audit row is written. With the Identity of the NIN holder proof change (0093) both answers are `503 PATIENT_REGISTRATION_NOT_OPEN`, on staging and in production, whatever the QoreID flags, because no holder route is open. Before 0093, in staging with NIN-only enrollment on, one sandbox enrollment start writes one `identity.public-patient-enrollment.nin-lookup-quota` success, and PostgreSQL logs no `permission denied for function consume_public_patient_nin_quota`. After 0093 that staging half runs only once a staging facility is designated (S4v); the runtime verifier covers the quota (§8);
    - with FE-4 (same branch), in production: `GET /identity/patient-enrollments/availability` answers `200 {"open":false}` (with 0093's Identity `{"open":false,"online":false,"facility":false}`), and the start's `503` above carries "New Health ID registration is not open yet. If you already have a Health ID, sign in." (the gateway passes it through, ADR-055); before 0093, in staging with NIN-only enrollment on it answers `{"open":true}`, and `{"open":false}` while QoreID is paused in Admin Settings → Integrations. After 0093 staging answers `open:false` too until a staging facility is designated (S4v);
    - with the recovery and patient search hardening: a clinician's `POST /identity/patient-lookup` for a synthetic unknown HID answers `404 PATIENT_NOT_AVAILABLE`, and for a patient they hold an active grant for `200`; a `POST /auth/otp/start` with `turnstileAction` `staff-reset` for a synthetic patient's email answers `202` and sends no code; PostgreSQL logs no `function auth.recovery_account_audience` or `identity.registration_nin_holder` error and no `permission denied for function`;
    - with governed workforce provisioning: a staff sign-in of an existing clinician answers `200` and PostgreSQL logs no `function auth.facility_role_is_effective(uuid) does not exist` and no `permission denied for function facility_role_is_effective`; `GET /admin/workforce/accounts/:accountId` for a synthetic staff account answers `200` to a Super Admin and `403 PERMISSION_DENIED` to a Support Admin;
    - with staff MFA: a synthetic staff account's `POST /auth/login` answers `mfa_required` or `mfa_enrollment_required` with only the `hid_access_mfa` cookie; its first sign-in enrols with the emailed code and a TOTP code and then reads `GET /auth/session` `200`; a staff session cookie from before the deploy answers `403 STAFF_MFA_REQUIRED`; CloudWatch shows no `MFA_UNAVAILABLE` and PostgreSQL logs no `MFA_ENROLLMENT_PROOF_REQUIRED` from the trigger (the service refuses first);
    - with ADR-055: as a clinician with access, save a draft note of 20,000 characters through the Worker (`POST /api/v1/ehr/patients/:id/encounters/:encounterId/clinical-notes`): `201`, not the WAF's `403`; `GET /` on each launch host carries `Strict-Transport-Security` and the portal `Content-Security-Policy`; the gateway's CloudWatch log lines are JSON with `path` and no query string (search the console's principals once and check the line);
    - with the facility selection port: a synthetic staff account with two facilities signs in (verify or enrolment activation answers `facilitySelection: 'default'`), `POST /auth/facility` for its second facility answers `selected`, and `GET /auth/session` without `X-Facility-ID`, then `POST /auth/refresh`, report that facility with `selected`; PostgreSQL logs no `relation "auth.session_facility_selection" does not exist`, no `permission denied for table session_facility_selection` and no `SESSION_FACILITY_SELECTION_INVALID`;
    - with the launch module permissions (0091): a doctor's `GET /auth/session` lists no `lab.*`, `pharmacy.*`, `ocr.*` or `outreach.*` permission, `identity.break-glass.write` or `ehr.document.write`; a synthetic patient's `GET /identity/me/access-requests` answers `200` (the bootstrap restored its grant) and `GET /identity/me/access-history` returns `truncated`; P11 lists the three launch controls false;
    - PostgreSQL logs no `permission denied for table` from a runtime role and no `deadlock detected` during the smoke checks and S4b, S4c, S4g and S4h.

## 7. Staging acceptance

These must all pass before production; record evidence for each. None of them
has been run, because no staging environment exists yet (Health-id
`docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`). With the edge and gateway change, rows that call
Lab, Pharmacy, OCR or Outreach routes through the edge (S4b, S4c, S4d, S4g,
and the Outreach registration cases S4h reviews) need a staging synth whose
`HID_ENABLED_API_ROUTES` names those modules (§6 step 11); with the default
`identity,ehr` the gateway answers `404 API_ROUTE_NOT_FOUND` for them.

| # | Test | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| S1 | Migration rehearsal on a restored staging snapshot. Restore it into an isolated database, then, as the migration administrator, run `db:plan`, `db:dry-run`, `db:migrate`, `db:bootstrap` (twice) and `db:verify-roles`. On RDS, record the I3 probe first (`select rolname, rolsuper, rolbypassrls, rolcreaterole from pg_roles where rolname in (current_user, '<migration administrator>')`, as the master) and, if the chain starts below 0015, the `SET ON PARAMETER` grant before the run and its revoke after 0083 (`MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6.1). Run the SQL suites and the restore check of `docs/TUF-STAGING-MIGRATION.md`, timing 0067, 0069, 0072, 0074 and 0075, and the scans of 0086 (the `otp_challenges` index build, step 19), 0088 and 0090 (the checks validated under `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE`, steps 21 and 23). `scripts/tuf-staging-migration-rehearsal.mjs` is local and synthetic only: it builds its own cluster and cannot use a snapshot. | Command output, timings and restore result. |
| S2 | `db:verify-roles` on staging, as the migration administrator that ran the bootstrap, and `db:verify-logins` with each of the ten staging runtime credentials' own URL (`MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6.3); repeat `db:verify-logins` after every credential rotation. | Command output. |
| S3 | The Identity runtime verifiers against staging Identity, especially `verify-platform-security-runtime.mjs`, `verify-platform-admin-runtime.mjs`, `verify-patient-safety-runtime.mjs` and, with staff MFA, `verify-staff-mfa-runtime.mjs`. | Verifier JSON. |
| S4 | The browser checks of §8, by hand with real authenticators, on the staging console (the Health-id build staged for `admin.staging` and served by `hid-admin-staging`, P14) and API. Check 3 needs a confirmation older than five minutes: wait it out, or use a second session. | Screenshots and notes for each check. |
| S4a | The governed-access loop in the staging provider and patient portals (the Health-id build staged for `staging`, P14; the provider workspace is `/provider/*` on that host): a clinician requests access, the patient approves, the clinician sees **Access active** with its expiry, writes a clinical record, closes access (**Closed by you**, writes disabled), then requests again and the patient revokes (**Access revoked**). No browser run of these pages has been done; only component tests and the HTTP verifier (§8). Insert no purpose-of-use code by hand for it: after 0082 the loop needs only the migrated `direct-care` (P10). | Screenshots and notes. |
| S4b | Since 0091 no role holds an OCR permission: this row runs only after OCR's own re-enabling migration (ADR-058), which grants the OCR permissions again and changes the bootstrap's role assertions with it; that migration applies to every environment, so it is a production decision too (production stays closed by the gateway route switch and an OCR desired count of 0). A permission granted on staging by hand stops every later bootstrap (`a role holds a permission withdrawn by the launch module set`). Until then, check that the OCR routes answer `403 PERMISSION_DENIED`; the OCR runtime verifier exercises the pipeline by granting the permissions in its disposable copy. Needs `ocr` (and `lab` and `pharmacy` for the publication's imports to be checked through their APIs) in the staging `HID_ENABLED_API_ROUTES`. The OCR pipeline on staging: create a job, let the worker extract it, validate, confirm the patient and publish (Stage 9 fixed the §4.2 failures of validation, confirmation and publication). With a provider slower than a third of the lease, the worker must log no `ocr.job.lease_renewal_failed` (0077). A confirmation of the correct patient that fails with `500`, while PostgreSQL logs `Patient confirmation does not match the canonical OCR source patient`, means the schema owner cannot read the source document (P8, 0076). Confirm the outbox events, the patient-linked audit rows for `ocr.job.find`, `ocr.validation.list` and `ocr.publication.list`, the worker's `queueDepth` and `oldestQueueAgeSeconds`, and `OcrQueueAgeAlarm` datapoints.
