If (6) fails, first clear the email of every subscriber the flag synced (§9, "Notification worker after the Novu verified email change"), then set the flag false again and record it in ADR-064. | Novu activity and subscriber screenshots, the test inbox, worker log lines, the P35 output. |
| S4x | The patient record release (ADR-065, 0096) on staging after 0096, its bootstrap and that EHR build, with synthetic data only: a synthetic patient with a completed and an open encounter at a synthetic facility, each holding a final vital, a corrected vital, a confirmed and a provisional diagnosis, an active and a cancelled prescription, and (where uploads are on) a clean PHI document. (1) With every flag off, `GET /api/v1/ehr/me/records` answers `released: ["encounters","notes"]` and the five categories withheld, and the download answers `404 DOCUMENT_NOT_AVAILABLE`; (2) with `HID_EHR_PATIENT_RELEASE_VITALS`, `_DIAGNOSES` and `_PRESCRIPTIONS` on, the patient sees only the completed encounter's final uncorrected vital, confirmed diagnosis (no `notes`) and active prescription, and the audit row `ehr.patient.self.records.read` holds only counts; (3) a staff session on either route answers `403 PATIENT_SESSION_REQUIRED`; (4) only once Lab is re-enabled on staging (its reviewed migration and `HID_ENABLED_API_ROUTES`, as for S4b and S4c): with `HID_EHR_PATIENT_RELEASE_LAB_RESULTS` on (staging only) and a released result of a completed encounter's order, the patient sees it, and after an invalidation of that release it is gone; (5) only once uploads are on: with `HID_EHR_PATIENT_RELEASE_DOCUMENTS` on the patient downloads the clean document (the URL answers it as an attachment with `Cache-Control: private, no-store` and expires after 60 s; the EHR task role's `HeadObject` with checksum mode on the SSE-KMS object succeeds), another patient's or a rejected document answers `404`; (6) the P36 check lists exactly the expected policies, each 0096 policy and the view following `app.patient_release`, and the view's exact ACL. P36 ties parts (1) to (3) and (6) to the vitals, diagnoses and prescriptions flags, part (4) to the Lab results flag and part (5) to the documents flag. | Responses, audit rows and the P36 output, with synthetic identifiers only. |
| S5 | Cross-origin preflights, only if staging serves the console from another origin. | `OPTIONS` responses. |
| S6 | Rollback drill: redeploy the previous Identity build against the migrated staging database and sign in. | Notes. |

## 8. Evidence gathered locally (Stage 5)

All of this was run on 2026-10-09 against local synthetic data. It was run as
the non-root `postgres` user, on a new local cluster
(`/tmp/hid-tuf-migration.stage5`, migrated 0001 → 0072 and role-bootstrapped).
No other local cluster was touched. Later stages say which cluster they used.

- **Unit tests:**
  - Identity 702/702. The new and updated tests (`refresh-revocation-reason.spec.ts`, `platform-session-end.spec.ts`, `config/cors.spec.ts`, `admin-contracts.spec.ts`) fail 36 of 103 against the `deb6fba` token service and CORS options. The 8 tests of a session ending during a refresh also fail on the first Stage 5 commit (`04b4903`), whose conflict path still recorded reuse.
  - Pharmacy 22/22. The new `config/cors.spec.ts` fails against the old header list.
- **Runtime verifier:** `verify-platform-security-runtime.mjs` passes. Its new checks fail on `deb6fba`:
  - "a signed-out refresh token is not reuse": 2 `reuse_detected` events instead of 0;
  - "CORS does not allow DELETE".
- **Browser:** `services/identity-api/scripts/admin-console-e2e` serves this Identity API on a disposable database copy. Chromium (Playwright 1.56.1, headless) drives the built Health-id console through its `vite preview` same-origin proxy. Twelve checks:
  1. platform sign-in, with first-time authenticator enrolment, sign-out, then password and TOTP;
  2. `STEP_UP_REQUIRED` on facility suspension, then the change listed under the facility's platform changes;
  3. `STEP_UP_EXPIRED`, with the "more than 5 minutes" note, on facility restoration; both changes stay listed after a reload;
  4. approvals cursor paging: 55 rows over 2 pages, newest first, no duplicates;
  5. demo-request cursor paging for one filter, restarting when the filter changes;
  6. an MFA reset is offered only where `mfaEnrolled` is true;
  7. revoked-session notice in session, after another Super Admin revokes all sessions;
  8. revoked-session notice after a reload;
  9. expired-session notice in session;
  10. expired-session notice after a reload;
  11. no `reuse_detected` recorded for those ended sessions;
  12. no browser errors.

  Results:
  - **Stage 5 backend and console:** 12/12, on four separate fresh-database runs. The last run used the final code with the stricter check 12, which fails on any response other than the expected `401` and `403`.
  - **Health-id `main` (`a180cea`) console:** check 7 fails, because the revoke-all confirmation disappears (§2).
  - **Refresh logic of `deb6fba`:** check 11 fails with 2 `reuse_detected` events.
- **Stage 5B (0073), same cluster, migrated 0001 → 0073:**
  - `verify-platform-security-runtime.mjs` races each revocation against a second connection. That connection holds a refresh rotation open, as `TokenService.refresh` makes it: the new session inserted and the old one marked rotated, not yet committed. The connection commits only once the revocation is waiting on it. The four revocations are a compromised-family revoke, revoke-all, an own-session revoke and a refresh-token reuse.
  - The verifier also holds an administrator's revocation open while a sign-out, and then an expired refresh, run against the same account. It also holds a refresh open while the same sign-in signs out.
  - Results by build (each case recorded separately):

    | Schema | Identity | Session from the rotation in flight | Sign-out / expiry during a revocation | Sign-out during a refresh of the same sign-in |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | 0072 | Stage 5 (`6ec5037`) | live after all four revocations | not applicable | not run |
    | 0073 | Stage 5 | revoked by the two admin commands; live after the own-session and reuse revocations | deadlock; the request fails (`503` and `500`) | new session live |
    | 0073 | Stage 5B | revoked by all four | `204` and `401`; the revocation completes | new session revoked (`logout`) |

  - `session-revocation-lock.spec.ts`: 9/9 pass. Each fails against the Stage 5 token and platform-security services.
  - `session-revocation-serialization.integration.sql` checks the catalog contract: the helper is security definer, `PUBLIC` cannot execute it, and both admin commands lock the target and actor rows, in account order, before their `UPDATE`. `runtime-roles.integration.sql` checks the helper's grant.
- **Governed access (D-Eminence/hid-system#26 and D-Eminence/hid-system#28):**
  - Rehearsal: `scripts/tuf-staging-migration-rehearsal.mjs` was run as `postgres` on `4a1ec18`, which has the same tree as `main` after D-Eminence/hid-system#28. It covered:
    - 0028 → 0074, with the dry run unchanged;
    - 438 foreign keys and 0 orphans;
    - 24 SQL suites, also under the non-superuser definer owner;
    - every runtime HTTP verifier;
    - backup and restore integrity.
  - `verify-patient-safety-runtime.mjs` runs over HTTP with the exact runtime role. The clinician's list:
    - reports the active grant and its expiry after the patient approves, and `?status=active` finds it;
    - reports `revoked` after the patient revokes, `?status=revoked` finds it, and `?status=active` no longer does.

    With the earlier Identity mapping it fails at the first of these checks. Without the D-Eminence/hid-system#26 grant, the list answers `403`.
  - `staff-access-request-outcome.integration.sql` covers:
    - every outcome, including grants that the patient or the clinician revoked only after they lapsed;
    - each filter value;
    - isolation from a colleague's requests;
    - a member without `identity.consent.write`.

    It fails against the first version of 0074.
  - `runtime-function-grants.spec.ts` names the ungranted function on the code before D-Eminence/hid-system#26.
  - Workspace gates passed (Identity 713/713).
  - Health-id: 399/399 tests and both builds passed. `GovernedAccess.test.tsx` has 12 tests, 4 of which fail on the first version of D-Eminence/Health-id#19.
  - **No browser run** of the provider or patient portal pages (S4a).
- **Stage 6 (0075):** on the local cluster `/tmp/hid-tuf-migration.stage6`, migrated 0001 → 0075 and role-bootstrapped (twice, to check the bootstrap is repeatable), and in the synthetic rehearsal of the branch. The rehearsal ran 0028 → 0075 with 25 additional suites, also under the non-superuser definer owner, plus every runtime verifier and the restore check.
  - `ocr-read-audit.spec.ts` has 21 tests:
    - for each of the three reads: the event, its patient (the source document's, while the job's own `patient_id` is null), its details, one transaction shared with the read, ordering after authorization, failing closed when the audit write fails, and no event when unauthorized or when no job exists;
    - every other OCR API job event names the canonical patient: read, extractions, retry, create, reuse, validation, confirmation, publication request, success and failure;
    - a second confirmation of the same job version gets `409`.

    16 of the 21 fail against the `main` OCR service; the other 5 check refusals that `main` already gets right. A mutant that writes the read event in a second transaction fails the transaction check.
  - `ocr-queue-metrics-and-outbox.integration.sql` checks:
    - the worker's aggregate equals a superuser count across two facilities, from a session naming a different facility;
    - the worker cannot read `ocr.jobs`;
    - six other roles cannot execute the command, and no other non-superuser role that does not inherit the worker holds `EXECUTE`;
    - the owner can read no column but `status` and `queued_at`;
    - visibility comes only from the exact policy;
    - `hid_ocr_api_runtime` appends outbox events for its facility, and another facility is refused.

    It passes on the branch and fails on `main` (`function ocr.worker_queue_metrics() does not exist`). Ten mutants each fail `runtime-roles.integration.sql`, and eight of them also fail the suite:
    - no outbox insert policy;
    - no metrics policy;
    - a superuser function owner;
    - worker `SELECT` on `ocr.jobs`;
    - OCR runtime `EXECUTE`;
    - full-table `SELECT` for the owner;
    - owner `SELECT` on `document_id`;
    - migration administrator `EXECUTE`;
    - owner `UPDATE (status)`, caught by `runtime-roles` only;
    - the owner made a member of `hid_ocr_runtime`, caught by `runtime-roles` only.
  - Worker: `repository.spec.ts` and `database-privileges.spec.ts` check that the worker reads only through commands that `hid_ocr_worker` can execute. 3 of 4 fail on `main`.
  - The compiled worker repository was run as a login that inherits `hid_ocr_worker`. It returned `{"queueDepth":2,"oldestQueueAgeSeconds":7211}` for two facilities. `main`'s query as the same login failed with `42501 permission denied for table jobs`.
  - On a copy whose functions and tables are all owned by a `NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS` role (P8), the new suite passes. `schema.integration.sql` fails at each function listed in P8, one by one, and passes once those functions are returned to a superuser owner.
- **Stage 7 (0076):** on the new local cluster `/tmp/hid-tuf-migration.stage7`, run as `postgres`. One database was migrated 0001 → 0076 from the branch, and one 0001 → 0075 from `main` (`dafe147`). Each had a copy with every schema, table and function owned by a `NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS` role, as in the rehearsal's owner check.
  - **Rehearsal and acceptance:** `scripts/tuf-staging-migration-rehearsal.mjs` ran as `postgres` on `91f3798`, the last code change of the branch (later commits change docs only), with a clean worktree. It covered:
    - 0028 → 0076, with the dry run unchanged;
    - 438 foreign keys and 0 orphans;
    - 27 additional suites, also under the non-superuser definer owner;
    - every runtime HTTP verifier, including the MFA races below;
    - backup and restore integrity.

    `scripts/run-container-database-acceptance.sh` passed for 0001–0076, including both new suites.
  - **Workspace gates passed:** build, tests, verify, release tests, release contracts, the EHR app tests and `git diff --check`. Identity has 724 tests, the OCR API 51 and the OCR worker 17.
  - **MFA lock order:**
    - `mfa-lock-order.spec.ts` has 11 tests: one per MFA transaction (the account lock comes first, in the same transaction, and is never the `FOR UPDATE` revocation lock), the challenge locked only after the account and for that account, a challenge refused by its re-read after the account lock, and platform session issue without a second pool connection. All 11 fail against the `main` services (`dafe147`) and against the first version (`f3300e0`), which used `auth.lock_account_sessions`; 10 fail against `399d4c5`, which also used it but already resolved the administrator on the transaction. With the account lock removed from the challenge path only, 7 fail, including the refusal test. The lock mode itself is a database property, checked by the suite and the races below.
    - `verify-platform-security-runtime.mjs` races an approved MFA reset against six MFA requests of its target: TOTP and recovery-code sign-in, step-up, recovery-code regeneration, enrolment activation, and enrolment started again over a pending factor. A second connection runs the real `auth.admin_decide_approval`, holding the target's account row as 0070 does until the request waits. While the request waits, it checks with `NOWAIT` which authenticator rows the request holds.
    - On `main`, every request held its factor while it waited. Sign-in and enrolment requests also held their challenge, step-up held its session assurance, and recovery-code sign-in and regeneration held their recovery codes. PostgreSQL detected six deadlocks (`pg_stat_database.deadlocks` +6, six `deadlock detected` reports in the server log), cancelled each MFA request (`500`) and committed the approval.
    - On the branch, each request waits holding no authenticator row, and the approval commits. The request is then refused (`401 MFA_CHALLENGE_INVALID` for sign-in and enrolment, `403 PLATFORM_SESSION_REQUIRED` for step-up and regeneration). It records no deadlock, leaves no live session, and never spends the recovery code.
    - The verifier also runs a step-up and a regeneration while a refresh of the same sign-in is in flight, and the platform password step while a staff sign-in of the same administrator holds the principal's login-attempt row.
      - On the branch, neither MFA request waits for the refresh and both answer `200`. The password step waits for the staff sign-in, which commits, and then answers `200`.
      - Against the first version (`FOR UPDATE`): both MFA requests waited, then answered `403 PLATFORM_SESSION_REQUIRED`. The password step deadlocked with the staff sign-in and answered `500`, while the staff sign-in committed. The server log shows the `deadlock detected` report between `delete from auth.login_attempts` and the staff `insert into auth.session_events`.
    - The verifier then checks that MFA transactions of one account run one at a time. A second connection holds the account lock until every request waits on it.
      - Six parallel step-ups with a wrong code: on the branch all six wait, then five answer `401 MFA_INVALID_CODE` and the sixth `429 MFA_RATE_LIMITED` (five `step_up_failed` events, one `mfa_rate_limited`). Against the second version (`FOR KEY SHARE`), all six codes were tested and refused as invalid: six failures, no rate limit.
      - Two parallel password steps, while the second connection also holds the principal's login-attempt row as a staff sign-in in flight does: on the branch both wait, the second supersedes the first's challenge, and one challenge stays open. Against `FOR KEY SHARE`, both waited only on the login-attempt row, ran together once it was released, and left two challenges open.
      - No deadlock is recorded.
    - `mfa-account-lock.integration.sql` checks the exact function body. It fails on the `FOR KEY SHARE` version and on bodies taking `FOR KEY SHARE`, `FOR UPDATE`, `FOR NO KEY UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, `FOR NO KEY UPDATE NOWAIT`, the lock inside `IF false` only, or no lock clause. `runtime-roles.integration.sql` fails when `EXECUTE` is also granted to `hid_api_runtime`, `hid_notification_worker`, `hid_ocr_queue_metrics` or `PUBLIC`; the earlier check, over a fixed list of roles, missed the first three.
  - **OCR guards:** `ocr-validation-guards.integration.sql` runs 23 cases.
    - On 0075 it fails with 10 wrong outcomes:
      - two confirmations whose source document is missing;
      - confirmations with no session account or membership, or with another member's account or membership;
      - validations whose extraction is missing, with no session account (empty or unknown subject) or no membership.
    - Under the non-superuser owner, 0075 also accepts the wrong patient and the right patient (12), because the guard cannot read the source document.
    - On 0076 it passes in both. `schema.integration.sql`, the 27 additional suites (including `mfa-account-lock.integration.sql`) and the role assertions pass on 0076; the additional suites also pass under the non-superuser owner.
    - Mutation check: each of the 12 comparison predicates of the two guards, removed in turn, makes the suite fail at the case named for it. That covers facility, source patient, job patient, version, account, membership, status and extraction. Removing an explicit `IS NULL` refusal alone changes no outcome: the `IS DISTINCT FROM` comparison that follows refuses a NULL session value against the supplied one, and a NULL supplied value is refused by the column's `NOT NULL` constraint.
    - The functions keep their owner, ACL, `SECURITY DEFINER` and `search_path`.
  - **P8 proposal probes**, on throwaway databases and roles that were dropped afterwards (PostgreSQL 16.15), for §4.1 and ADR-041. They confirmed:
    - the `plpgsql.variable_conflict` behaviour under a non-superuser owner;
    - the in-body option;
    - the non-superuser bootstrap limits on `ALTER ROLE`, ownership transfer and later `CREATE OR REPLACE`.

    A rolled-back probe confirmed that `ocr.validate_job_write` (on 0076) accepts a job whose document has no scan event (§9).
- **Stage 8 (0077):** on the local cluster `/tmp/hid-tuf-migration.stage7b`, run as `postgres`, with throwaway copies of one database migrated 0001 → 0076 from `main` (`158f68c`) and one 0001 → 0077 from the branch, and owner-check copies whose schemas, tables and functions belong to a `NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS` role, as in the rehearsal.
  - **Rehearsal and acceptance:** `scripts/tuf-staging-migration-rehearsal.mjs` ran as `postgres` on `f8bfbd7` with a clean worktree: 0028 → 0077 with the dry run unchanged, 438 foreign keys and 0 orphans, 32 additional suites (including the five new ones) under the migration owner and again under the non-superuser owner, every runtime HTTP verifier, and backup and restore integrity. `/etc/gitconfig` was restored byte for byte afterwards. `scripts/run-container-database-acceptance.sh` passed for 0001–0077 as `postgres`, including the five new suites.
  - **Workspace gates passed** on `84520c0` (later commits change docs only): build, tests (Identity 724, Outreach API 23), verify, release tests, release contracts, the EHR app tests and `git diff --check`. Every SQL suite (33) and the role assertions pass on 0077.
  - **Failing first:** on 0076 the five new suites fail with 17 (OCR worker lease, 41 cases), 12 (OCR job source and publication, 30), 60 (Lab, 86), 58 (Pharmacy, 84) and 67 (Outreach, 121) wrong outcomes; on 0077 they pass, twice on one copy, and under the non-superuser owner. Restoring any replaced Lab, Pharmacy or Outreach function to its earlier body, or dropping a suite's runtime role switch, makes its suite fail; 11 of 12 single-point mutants of the OCR functions are caught (the survivor is equivalent). `runtime-roles.integration.sql` and the Outreach API's `runtime-function-grants.spec.ts` fail on `main`'s grants.
  - **Reproduced on 0076:** a NULL claim token completed and failed another replica's job; every lease renewal was refused; a superuser session with no context wrote Lab, Pharmacy and Outreach rows attributed to another member; under the non-superuser owner a Lab import bound another facility's OCR validation and publication; a document marked entered in error during OCR made `ocr.claim_worker_job` raise for its provider; every Outreach write failed with `42501` as the Outreach runtime. The §4.2 locking statements failed as each service's runtime role, and the three §9 lock-order hazards (and the facility-suspension one) deadlocked with two connections.
  - **Read-only data check:** no local database holds an OCR job without an exact clean scan of its source; the P9 query lists such jobs on synthetic data.
  - **Adversarial review:** five independent reviewers (semantics, security, tests, docs, registration) and one verifier per finding. Every confirmed finding is fixed here or recorded above: the unbounded lease renewal, the out-of-play exemption, the same-status job event, the untested renewal and metadata rules, P9's cancel instructions and the counts.
- **Stage 9 (0078):** run as `postgres` on synthetic data only. The final evidence comes from databases migrated with the final 0078 (sha256 `00b869c0…`): the rehearsal's own fresh cluster, and `hid_rehearsal` on the local cluster `/tmp/hid-tuf-migration.stage9`, rebuilt from the final tree (0001 → 0078, role-bootstrapped) before the last verifier runs. Earlier local runs used `hid_rehearsal` migrated with the first version of 0078 (`ff7958ac…`, the definer predicate); the failing-first comparisons used scratch copies on `/tmp/hid-tuf-migration.stage7b` of a database migrated 0001 → 0077 from `main` (`54f79d2`). Every service verifier runs on its own disposable copy, which it drops.
  - **Rehearsal and acceptance:** `scripts/tuf-staging-migration-rehearsal.mjs` ran as `postgres` on `38912cd`, the last code change before the OCR publication retry fix below (the commits between them change docs only), with a clean worktree (`/etc/gitconfig` restored byte for byte afterwards): 0028 → 0078 with the dry run unchanged, 438 foreign keys and 0 orphans before and after, 33 additional suites (including `runtime-command-privileges.integration.sql`) under the migration owner and again under the non-superuser owner, the static check with `--catalog` (31 clauses, no violation, 146 tables, no difference), every runtime verifier (Identity registration review 9 checks, Pharmacy 10, Lab 16, OCR 14, Outreach 6) and the exact restore. It also passed on `ca0c41c`, `faf9a1e` and `111e54b`. `scripts/run-container-database-acceptance.sh` passed for 0001–0078 on `ca0c41c`, the last change to services or SQL (TLS `verify-full`, 8 non-owner logins, 8 cross-domain denials, six API health checks and SIGTERM exits). The CI workspace job failed on `82f062d`, `f98258d` and `7fc5a8f`; its artifact could not be downloaded here (the network policy blocks the artifact host), and the failure is consistent with Case 5 under the non-superuser owner, which a local rehearsal reproduced and `a8420e9` fixed. CI passed on every later push it completed.
  - **Workspace gates passed** on `38912cd`: build, tests (Identity API 726, Lab API 53, Pharmacy API 31, OCR API 64 (75 with the retry fix below), Outreach API 34, the script tests 35 with the 19 of the lock check), verify (including `verify:runtime-locks`: 31 lock clauses, no violation), release tests, release contracts, the EHR app tests and `git diff --check`.
  - **Runtime verifiers on the rebuilt `hid_rehearsal`** (final 0078): Identity registration review 9 checks, Pharmacy 10, Lab 16, OCR 14 (17 with the retry fix below), Outreach 6, each passed as its runtime role through the API, each ending with no deadlock in `pg_stat_database`.
  - **OCR publication retry fix** (`3a0448d` and the commits after it; they change `ocr.service.ts`, its unit spec, `verify-ocr-runtime.mjs` and this checklist, and the two commits after `8a5814a` also change the teardown of `verify-registration-review-runtime.mjs` and then of `verify-provider-enrollment-runtime.mjs`). A failure that is not a `4xx` refusal is now stored as retryable (§9). Local, synthetic evidence only; nothing here ran in GitHub Actions, on staging or in production:
    - The OCR API's 75 unit tests, `typecheck`, `lint`, `verify-runtime-locking-privileges.mjs` (31 lock clauses, no violation) with its 19 tests, and `git diff --check` pass.
    - On `c786f92`, `verify-ocr-runtime.mjs` (then 16 checks) passed four times in a row as `hid_ocr_api_runtime`. It ran on disposable copies of `hid_rehearsal` in a stopped file copy of `/tmp/hid-tuf-migration.stage9` (final 0078; its migration ledger matches the tree), served on its own socket directory and port with no TCP listener, and each run ended with no deadlock. `/tmp/hid-tuf-migration.stage9` itself was not started, and its files are unchanged.
    - The verifier now resends a failed publication before its retry time and requires `409 OCR_PUBLICATION_IN_PROGRESS`, about 15 s of backoff, no Lab call and an unchanged publication and job. It also requires that every Lab call of the retried publication, the refused ones included, sends `ocr-publication:<id>` and the same body, and that a replay asks the Lab API nothing. Its three waits for a held upstream call fail with a label after 30 s, or as soon as the request answers first. A stale attempt is checked too: the first request's Lab call is held while its claim is moved into the past, a resend claims the publication again with a new token and its Lab call is held, and the stale attempt, once answered, must get `409 OCR_PUBLICATION_CLAIM_LOST`, leave the resend's claim unchanged and record no `OcrPublicationFailed` or `ocr.publication.fail`; the resend then publishes once.
    - Failing first, each in a disposable source export: with `f0284b7`'s `ocr.service.ts`, the verifier fails because the Lab outage is stored as `LAB_IMPORT_UNAVAILABLE` (the fake Lab's code). With that check skipped, it fails at the early resend, which answers `409 OCR_PUBLICATION_TERMINAL`. Without `next_attempt_at <= clock_timestamp()` in the claim, the early resend is claimed and published (`201`). The unit suite catches 11 of 13 single-point mutants of the change and its guards; the two survivors are the failure update's processing-token guard and the claim's retry time, SQL the unit harness does not execute. The verifier catches the retry time at the early resend, and the processing-token guards of the failure and the publish update at the stale attempt (see the commit after `c786f92`). The unit harness no longer records a failure event after a lost claim, which PostgreSQL cannot produce because both updates require the same token.
    - On `c786f92`, locally, in a fresh clone installed with `npm ci` from every tracked lockfile and run as a non-root user: the steps of the CI job "Complete workspace verification" (`release/scripts/run-workspace-gates.mjs`: build, test, verify, release tests, release contracts, EHR app tests, the synthetic migration rehearsal 0028 → 0078 with every runtime verifier, `git diff --check`) passed, and `scripts/run-container-database-acceptance.sh` passed for 0001–0078. This is a local run of those commands, not a GitHub Actions run.
    - On `83f9844`, the commit that adds the stale-attempt check, the same local run passed again: the steps of "Complete workspace verification" (the rehearsal's OCR verifier now has 17 checks) and container acceptance for 0001–0078. In a disposable clone, without `processing_token=$2` in the failure update the stale attempt turns the resend's claim into `failed` / `OCR_PUBLICATION_CLAIM_LOST` and the verifier fails there; without it in the publish update the stale attempt publishes (`201`) and the verifier fails at its expected `409`. The commit after `83f9844` changes only this checklist.
    - Observed once, in a focused rehearsal run outside the runs above: `verify-registration-review-runtime.mjs` passed all its checks, then the forced `drop database ... with (force)` of its teardown ended a connection whose error nothing handles (`57P01`), so the process exited non-zero and the rehearsal failed. Every other run of it passed. It is a teardown race in that Stage 9 verifier, not in the OCR change, and could fail a CI run the same way. The connection was one of the verifier's own owner pool, idle and already closing: `pool.end()` resolves before its connections have closed, and that pool had no error handler (the platform verifiers have ignored every error on theirs since `448808a`). The commit after `8a5814a` handles it more narrowly: from its teardown on, the verifier accepts `57P01` from that pool and nothing else, so any other pool error and any failed check still fail the run, and the forced drop is unchanged. `verify-provider-enrollment-runtime.mjs`, which the rehearsal also runs, tore down the same way with no handler on its owner pool; with the race forced locally (that pool's closing connections held until the drop had signalled them) it passed all its checks and then exited non-zero on the same `57P01`. The commit after `d515b85` gives its teardown the same handler. This checklist records no rehearsal or gate run of the two commits after `8a5814a`.
    - Not run on these commits: GitHub Actions (none of them has been pushed), staging and production.
  - **Static check:** `verify-runtime-locking-privileges.mjs` on `main` reports 14 of 43 lock clauses, exactly the §4.2 statements; on the branch 0 of 31; with `--catalog` against the rebuilt `hid_rehearsal` it compares 146 tables and finds no difference. `runtime-locking-privileges.test.mjs` (19 tests) pins the 14 `main` statements as refused, and the shapes the two reviews used to slip a lock past it (see the review below). Seven of those tests fail on the first version of the check, which also passed the first review's three mutations of real service files (an OCR lock-clause parameter, a concatenated Lab accession lock, a typed strength constant on the Pharmacy reversal); five fail on the version the second review examined (`ca0c41c`).
  - **Failing first, as each runtime role:**
    - `verify-registration-review-runtime.mjs` on `main`'s Identity: `500` (`0A000`) where the stale-version `412` / denial `404` was expected; with only the lock fixed, every review failed with `42501 permission denied for table verification_evidence` until 0078.
    - `verify-pharmacy-runtime.mjs`, `verify-lab-runtime.mjs`, `verify-ocr-runtime.mjs` and `verify-outreach-runtime.mjs` on `main`'s services: the first write command fails with `500` and `42501` (`work_items`, `work_items`, `validations`, `campaign_members`).
    - `runtime-command-privileges.integration.sql` on 0077: the Identity identifier insert fails (`42501 permission denied for table verification_evidence`); with only the Identity part of 0078, Case 5 fails with `42703 record "new" has no field "ordinal"`. On the first version of 0078, which repaired the self-NIN policy through a definer predicate instead of dropping it, Case 6 fails: the Identity runtime inserted a self-NIN identifier directly.
    - Without the retry, four concurrent identical Pharmacy accepts lose one to `40001` (`500`); with three attempts 5–15 ms apart, eight concurrent dispenses of different work items fail with `40001` in each of three runs.
    - OCR lock order: with the old publication replay lock, the replay waits for the publication row while holding the job (the check fails); without the job key share in the publish or the failure transaction, the probe of the publication row times out (`55P03`) while that transaction waits for the job. Before the fix, the concurrent publication requests of the verifier deadlocked: the cluster log records three-way cycles between a publication insert waiting on the publication unique index, an outbox insert's key share on the job, and a request queued for the job (`deadlock detected`, 00:17 and 00:31; excerpt kept with the local evidence), hidden from the API by the retry. Each verifier now fails on any deadlock.
    - OCR terminal failure: a resent publication request that waits for the job (behind a key share, as behind the failure transaction) while the Lab API rejects the first request was, on the previous service, claimed again and sent to the Lab API a second time (`422`); now it answers `409 OCR_PUBLICATION_TERMINAL` and the Lab API is asked once.
    - Outreach: with the conflict mapped inside the transaction (`7fc5a8f`), the loser of identical concurrent registrations could get `409 OUTREACH_REGISTRATION_CONFLICT` instead of the replay (once in a rehearsal; 12 of 20 rounds in the review's probe). The verifier's concurrent check now runs ten rounds and fails in round 2 on that service; two unit tests also fail on it.
    - Unit specs: `nin-registration-review-lock.spec.ts` (1 of 2), the OCR lock-order and claim specs (4), the Lab accession spec (1 of 4) and the two Outreach conflict tests fail on the services before their fix; each `database.service.spec.ts` (Pharmacy, Lab, OCR, Outreach) covers the retry, its pause window and the once-only unique-violation retry.
  - **Retry sizing:** `services/pharmacy-api/scripts/measure-serializable-retry.mjs` runs rounds of concurrent dispenses and reversals of different work items as `hid_pharmacy_api_runtime`, with 20–40 ms Identity latency inside each transaction (`POLICY` = attempts, base and cap of the pause window; unset, the shipped policy). 20 rounds each:

    | Policy | Concurrent | Writes | Failed | Most attempts |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | 8 attempts, 20 ms, cap 500 ms | 8 | 320 | 0 | 7 |
    | 8 attempts, 20 ms, cap 500 ms | 16 | 638 | 2 (`40001`) | 8 |
    | 10 attempts, 20 ms, cap 1 s | 8 / 16 / 16 | 320 / 640 / 640 | 0 | 7 / 8 / 9 |
    | 12 attempts, 25 ms, cap 1 s | 8 / 16 | 320 / 640 | 0 | 7 / 9 |
    | 12 attempts, 20 ms, cap 1 s (shipped; the committed script, `POLICY` unset) | 16 / 16 | 640 / 640 | 0 | 9 / 9 |

    No run recorded a deadlock. 20 bursts of 16 took about 50 s.
  - **Lab order reconciliation query (§10):** on synthetic rows in a rolled-back transaction it lists an active, an on-hold and a completed request without a work item, and not an active one with a work item or a draft or cancelled one.
  - **Mapping:** one workflow per service reproduced each §4.2 statement as its runtime role (cheap statement and full transaction), measured races with two connections, and proposed the fixes, independently of the branch. Its Identity mapper also found the resolve-path constraint-name defect (§9).
  - **Adversarial review:** two independent reviews, each with a verifier per finding.
    - First review (`7fc5a8f`; concurrency, security, static check, evidence, docs). Fixed: the Identity runtime's direct self-NIN insert through the first 0078 (medium; the policy is dropped and Case 6 added), the Outreach conflict mapped inside the transaction (medium), the OCR terminal publication re-claimed by a resent request, the ways the static check passed a reintroduced lock or skipped a refused form (fragments placed by text, `for ${strength}`, shadowed names, variant truncation, role inheritance, `0A000` forms), the OCR verifier's vacuous probe, the Identity verifier's database leak, the Lab coverage gap (the verifier now runs the result path), the single-round Outreach check, and the doc inaccuracies (§2, §4.2, §8, §9, §10, ADR-042, `DATABASE.md`). Not a defect: the claim that the check is not in CI (the workspace gates job runs `npm run verify`, `npm test` and the rehearsal).
    - Second review (`ca0c41c`; concurrency, security, static check, docs). Security found nothing: dropping the policy opens no insert path, `bind_my_verified_nin` still works, no removed lock was an authorization filter, the kept locks keep their policy filters, the retry bypasses no guard and isolation is unchanged. Concurrency confirmed the lock order, the terminal claim, the Outreach and Identity paths and the retry's re-execution safety. Every confirmed finding was low after verification. Fixed: more shapes that slip past the static check (escape sequences, comments between `FOR` and the strength, `--` inside a SQL string, exported lock constants, destructured and loop names, an interpolation after `OF`, a lock in a ternary condition, `0A000` gaps, role-attribute order); stale local evidence (the rebuilt `hid_rehearsal`); and the docs (the two deliberate in-transaction `409` mappings, the per-attempt EHR source-read audit, Case 2's coverage, the bootstrap after 0078, S4h for the NIN review, completed requests in §10, these counts). Left to the runtime checks, and documented: SQL assembled by string operations outside literals. Refuted: a claimed mismatch in the shipped retry measurement (the shipped policy was then re-measured with the committed script).
    - Recorded in §9: Lab's in-transaction authorization audit on a second pool connection, OCR's terminal treatment of an owning domain's own `5xx` codes, the Lab refusals that answer `500` (self-verification, a second verification, release or entry), and the repeated audited calls of a retried attempt.

The rehearsal used its own temporary Unix-socket cluster, which it creates and removes on each run (`docs/TUF-STAGING-MIGRATION.md`):
  - **HTTP verifier:** `services/outreach-api/scripts/verify-outreach-cookie-read-runtime.mjs`, run by `scripts/tuf-staging-migration-rehearsal.mjs` right after `delegated_cookie_read_runtime` and recorded as `checks.outreach_cookie_read_runtime`. Like the #38 verifier, it runs only inside the rehearsal and works on a disposable copy of its database, which it drops. It runs Identity in a child process as `hid_identity_api_runtime` and Outreach as `hid_outreach_api_runtime`, and generates the local workload secrets per run. The version on `b9587cb` had five checks and created a campaign over HTTP. With the narrowing it no longer can, and it has six checks:
    1. a doctor's same-origin cookie `GET`s of registration cases and campaigns, with no Origin and no CSRF, are authorized through Identity;
    2. a cookie write with Origin and the session's CSRF token creates a registration case, which the cookie alone then lists and reads;
    3. one campaign is seeded with SQL in the doctor's own database context, as `createCampaign` writes it (the campaign, and the creator as its `admin` member). The cookie alone lists it and its single `admin` member, and a bearer read of campaigns is authorized. A campaign create, a status change and a member add, each with Origin and the session's CSRF token, are refused `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`, and the campaign is unchanged (status `planned`, `rowVersion` 1);
    4. a cookie read naming a disallowed Origin is `403 ORIGIN_DENIED`, and a cookie write without CSRF, without Origin, or with a wrong CSRF token (refused by Identity) is `403 CSRF_VALIDATION_FAILED`;
    5. a read with no session is `401`, with a forged cookie `401`, and with a patient session `403 PATIENT_SCOPE_DENIED`;
    6. called directly, Identity's `POST /identity/outreach/authorize` answers `201` for the authenticated Outreach workload reporting `GET` for a read permission; `403 ORIGIN_DENIED` for a forged workload token and for no reported method; `403 CSRF_VALIDATION_FAILED` for a reported `GET` with a write permission; and `401` for another workload (`ehr-api`).

- **EHR and OCR guard refusals before a facility is bound** (D-Eminence/hid-system#46; first on `main` `05df685`, the review follow-up on the branch merged with `main` at `5a2b885`), local unit tests only, run on 2026-10-12:
  - **Unit:** `services/ehr-api/src/auth/remote-security.guard.spec.ts` (17 tests) and `services/ocr-api/src/auth/remote-security.guard.spec.ts` (8 tests) serve a probe route over HTTP. They use the real `RemoteSecurityGuard`, `AuditInterceptor`, `AuditService` and `ProblemDetailsFilter`, registered as `AppModule` and `main.ts` register them. Identity's service session is stubbed to return a staff actor with a default facility. The stub database refuses what 0067's two staff CHECKs refuse, and can fail the control read or the audit insert on demand. Each row is checked for provenance, `details` (method and route), source IP and user agent as well as actor and scope.
  - **Failing first:** with only the first specs (9 and 7 tests), EHR failed 6 of 9 and OCR 4 of 7. In EHR, `FACILITY_REQUIRED` (missing and malformed header), `FACILITY_ACCESS_DENIED`, `PLATFORM_PORTAL_DISABLED` and `PLATFORM_MAINTENANCE` all answered `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` with no row, and the patient refusal was recorded as `security.authorization.denied`. In OCR, all four answered `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`, the patient's included. The own-facility pass, the bound-facility `PERMISSION_DENIED` row and the unauthenticated `system` row passed before and after the fix.
  - **Review follow-up:** against `5a2b885`'s guards, the new specs fail EHR 4 of 17: a statement timeout (`57014`), a lost connection and a missing grant (`42501`) in the control check answer a staff request `401 AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED`, and a timeout answers a patient the same. OCR passes 8 of 8; its follow-up change does not alter behaviour. Four mutants of the new audit branch each pass the previous specs (EHR 9/9, OCR 7/7) and fail the new ones: dropping `details`, `sourceIp` or `userAgent` (EHR 13 of 17, OCR 4 of 8 each), and `void` in place of `await` on the audit write (1 each: the fail-closed case gets `400` instead of `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`).
  - **With the fix:** EHR 98/98 (20 suites) and OCR 108/108 (12 suites), up from 81 and 100 on `05df685` and 90 and 107 on `5a2b885`. `tsc --noEmit` and `git diff --check` are clean.
  - **Merged with `main` after #44 and #45** (#45 changes SQL and docs only): EHR 102/102 (21 suites, #44's four tests included) and OCR 108/108; `tsc --noEmit` is clean for both.
  - Not run for this change: PostgreSQL (the spec reproduces the CHECKs, it does not run them), an HTTP verifier, the rehearsal, the workspace gates and a browser.

- **Non-superuser database chain for RDS (D-Eminence/hid-system#63, migration 0083)**, local and synthetic only, on a throwaway PostgreSQL 16.15 cluster and the disposable clusters `scripts/verify-non-superuser-database.mjs` creates:
  - **Failing first:** with the tests-only commit `593d1b7` against `main`'s code: on a superuser database migrated to 0082 and bootstrapped by `main`, `function-parameter-settings.integration.sql` fails ("application functions need a parameter privilege on every call: ocr.claim_worker_job(text,integer) sets plpgsql.variable_conflict"), `platform-control-owner.integration.sql` fails ("platform.control_enabled is owned by postgres instead of hid_platform_control_reader") and `runtime-roles.integration.sql` fails ("required HID role is missing: hid_platform_control_reader"). On a copy re-owned to a `NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS` role, as the rehearsal's owner check does, the Identity runtime's call of `platform.control_enabled` raises `PLATFORM_CONTROL_UNAVAILABLE` and `ocr-worker-lease.integration.sql` fails ("claim that recovers a job whose document was withdrawn: expected ok, got 42501"). `verify-non-superuser-database.mjs` sees 0015 refused without the grant and 0015-0082 applied with it, then fails at "role bootstrap (first run) failed: Database role bootstrap failed: permission denied to alter role". The nine start-up check specs fail to compile ("Cannot find module './runtime-role-guard'"), and the two new `scripts/tests` files fail to load their modules.
  - **On the head:** `verify-non-superuser-database.mjs` passes in about 5 s. As a `NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS CREATEROLE NOCREATEDB` administrator: 0015 refused without the grant (14 migrations committed), 0015 to 0083 applied with it and the grant revoked, the bootstrap twice and `db:verify-roles`, `db:verify-logins` for ten logins the administrator created, and refusals of the administrator's URL, of a login also granted `hid_schema_test_runtime` and of another service's login. `platform.control_enabled` answers `true,true,false` (patient portal, provider portal, maintenance) to the Identity, EHR and Outreach logins, the OCR worker login reaches the claim's own authorization check without the parameter privilege, and the two new suites pass. A superuser database migrated to 0082 and bootstrapped by `main`, then upgraded to 0083 and bootstrapped twice by this change, passes all 40 rollback-only suites; its copy re-owned to a `NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS` role passes the 38 suites the rehearsal's owner check runs. A fresh superuser chain to 0083, bootstrapped twice, passes `schema`, `runtime-roles` and the new suites. A catalogue diff between `main`'s bootstrap and this change's, on two databases migrated to 0082, differs only in the new role's column grants, schema usage, policy and function ownership; memberships and every other ACL are equal. `verify-runtime-locking-privileges.mjs` passes, also against that catalogue. The nine services' unit suites (1,625 tests) and `tsc --noEmit`, `scripts/tests`, `verify-migration-ledger.mjs` (83 migrations, 0083 last), `npm --prefix release test` and `npm --prefix release run verify` pass.
  - **Review fixes (bootstrap lock order; `ADMIN OPTION` rows on technical owners):** on the tests-only commit `bcc6f5c`, `verify-non-superuser-database.mjs` fails with "the first bootstrap held application table locks while it waited for platform.control_settings" (`ocr.jobs` and the five outbox tables; the bootstrap was the deadlock victim, and the second run waited for the table too), and the container acceptance's new step, run on its own against a local superuser database, fails with "db:verify-roles accepted an ADMIN OPTION membership of hid_ocr_worker in hid_ocr_queue_metrics". On the tests-only commit `0b2bf29`, the static privilege replay's new test fails: the guarded policy replace is reported as "policy statement in a dollar-quoted body". On the head all three pass. The first run waits for `platform.control_settings` holding no application table, and the other transaction then gets its `identity.outbox_events` lock; the second run takes no lock on `platform.control_settings` (five consecutive passes). `db:verify-roles` refuses an `ADMIN OPTION`-only grant of `hid_ocr_queue_metrics` to `hid_ocr_worker` on both clusters, and of `hid_platform_control_reader` to another `CREATEROLE` login, and passes after the revoke. The reviewer's two-session reproduction (a control read held for 3 s, then an `identity.outbox_events` lock, with the bootstrap started 0.7 s in) records no deadlock, both on a rerun and on a run that recreates the policy. Two superuser databases, each bootstrapped twice, one by `bcc6f5c` and one by the head, have identical owners, ACLs, policies and memberships, and the `schema`, `platform-control-owner`, `platform-controls`, `function-parameter-settings`, `ocr-queue-metrics-and-outbox` and `runtime-command-privileges` suites pass on the head's. `verify-runtime-locking-privileges.mjs` passes, also with `--catalog` against that database (147 tables, no difference); `scripts/tests` (42 tests), the EHR unit suite and `tsc --noEmit`, `verify-migration-ledger.mjs`, `npm --prefix release test` and `npm --prefix release run verify` pass.
  - Not run: the full rehearsal (CI runs it), the container acceptance (`npm run acceptance:container:database`), anything on RDS (whether the master may grant `SET ON PARAMETER`, and whether `rds_superuser` keeps PostgreSQL 16's `createrole_self_grant` and `SET ROLE` rules), and anything on staging (§7).
- **EHR clinical safety and launch module set (D-Eminence/hid-system#64, 0084; EHR and Identity APIs)**, local and synthetic only, on a temporary PostgreSQL 16 cluster migrated 0001 → 0082 (the base) and a copy migrated on to 0084 and role-bootstrapped; 0083 was not present:
  - **Failing first:** on the tests-only commit `0380964`, against the base and the earlier services: `encounter-lifecycle.integration.sql` fails listing every record write it expects refused ("clinical records do not follow their encounter's status: ..."); `patient-self.integration.sql` fails at "self note RLS released signed notes of encounters that are not completed: cancelled, completed, then entered in error, entered in error, in progress"; the role assertions fail at "a role holds a write permission withdrawn by the launch module set: clinician holds ehr.lab-request.write, ..."; 81 EHR unit tests fail (the encounter status guard, the encounter cascade, the diagnosis refusal and the note release cases) and 1 Identity unit test (break-glass `423`); `verify-encounter-lifecycle-runtime.mjs` fails at its first note create (`500`, `42P08`) and, with only the parameter cast applied, at "expected [409, 'ENCOUNTER_NOT_OPEN'] got [201]" for a prescription on a completed encounter; `verify-delegated-cookie-read-runtime.mjs` fails at "GET /ehr/me/records released a signed note whose encounter is not completed".
  - **On the head:** EHR 27 suites and 278 tests, Identity 68 suites and 867 tests, and `tsc --noEmit` for both pass. On the 0084 copy every EHR SQL suite of the rehearsal passes, `encounter-lifecycle.integration.sql` with all 83 cases (12 trigger property checks and 71 writes as `hid_ehr_api_runtime`), and they pass again with every function and table owned by a `NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS` role (P8). `verify-runtime-locking-privileges.mjs` passes (32 locking clauses, no violation). The runtime verifiers `encounter_lifecycle_runtime` (new: refusals on completed, cancelled and entered-in-error encounters, the cascade with versions and audit ids, a retraction waiting for an in-flight note insert, and eight activations racing a retraction with no `5xx` and no deadlock), `delegated_cookie_read_runtime` (note release; `403` for the withdrawn permissions with no row written), `ehr_lab_handoff_runtime`, `pharmacy_runtime`, `lab_runtime` and `ocr_runtime` pass; the module verifiers grant their permissions through their fake Identity, so 0084 does not change them.
  - **Mutant (not committed):** an order activation that locks the order row before its encounter (with a 30 ms pause) made the lifecycle verifier's race fail with `500` (`40P01`, deadlock); the service was restored. The committed verifier runs the services' order only and checks that it produces no deadlock.
  - **Checks:** P11 on the 0084 copy lists the three controls `true` (no operator change was made locally), no purpose or permission row and no control event; P12 on synthetic rows lists exactly the open notes, diagnoses and orders of the entered-in-error and cancelled encounters and none of the others.

Only the release contract needs 0083: on this branch alone `npm --prefix release run verify` fails with "components config.migration.count: expected 83, received 84", and `npm --prefix release test` passes 79 of 81 (the deployment-plan and pinned-admission cases fail on the same check), because the contract requires the migrations numbered without a gap. With `0083_ocr_claim_in_body_variable_conflict.sql` and its ledger and components entries inserted before 0084, as the merge after 0083 will have them, `verify` passes and `test` passes 81/81 (the temporary files were removed). The migration runner, the ledger check, the SQL suites and the runtime verifiers do not need 0083. Merge this change only after 0083 is on `main`.

Raw requests over a socket: before the fix (`b19b2b0`), `POST /api/v1/identity/service\authorization/check#x`, `/api/v1/auth/service-session\#`, `/api/v1/ehr/internal\ocr/documents/abc/source#` and the Lab, Pharmacy and Outreach equivalents reached the internal handlers (`200`), and of 480 requests (every spelling the gateway tests route, for each internal route, once with `X-Real-IP` and once with `X-HID-Client-IP`) 18 reached an internal handler. On the head none of the 480 does: each answers `400 INVALID_REQUEST_TARGET`, `404 API_ROUTE_NOT_FOUND` or `403 CLIENT_ADDRESS_REQUIRED`, with all modules enabled and with `identity,ehr`, and every answer, before and after the fix, equals the nginx model's. A request carrying only `X-HID-Client-IP`, or no header, answers `403 CLIENT_ADDRESS_REQUIRED`; `X-Real-IP` from the trusted range (IPv4 and IPv6) reaches the backend as `X-Forwarded-For` and is not passed on; the `400` carries `application/problem+json` and `Cache-Control: no-store`; a backslash or `%5C%23` in the query is proxied unchanged. The `origin/main` Worker, run with a stubbed `fetch`, deletes a caller's `X-Real-IP` and forwards its `X-HID-Client-IP`; this branch's Worker replaces `X-Real-IP` with the `CF-Connecting-IP` address.

(1) The staging step replaced and then deleted whatever sat at the destination, so a mistyped `--staged-directory` was destroyed after a successful stage; it now refuses, before writing anything, unless the destination is missing, empty, or exactly `site/` and a `provenance.json` of this schema staged for the same Worker and environment, and checks again just before the swap. (2) The `index.html` check matched only the first `src=`/`href=` of `<script>` and `<link>` and accepted `<base href>`, a `data-src` beside a foreign `src`, `<style>@import`, an `onerror` handler, `meta http-equiv=refresh` and `<iframe>`; it is now an allow-list of the shell Vite writes (see §2). (3) `VITE_HID_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` is not part of Health-id's public environment record, so the record check could never fire and provenance always recorded it as `null`; the Google check now also looks for `google.accounts.id` and `/gsi/client`, and provenance records only the record's five keys. (4) `verify:launch:<environment>` stopped with `ENOENT` on `apps/ehr/dist/index.html` without the legacy builds; it now skips the builds (not the configurations) of the Workers not deployed there.

Deployed, `main.ts`'s CORS middleware answers an `Origin` outside `CORS_ORIGINS` before any route, as on every Identity route; `src/config/cors.spec.ts` now pins that for the availability path with the real `corsOptions`: no `Origin` and the allowed one reach the route (`200`, the allowed one with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` and `Vary: Origin`), a foreign one gets `500` with no CORS permission and the route is not reached (these two cases pass on the unchanged code; they document behaviour, they do not change it). Step 19 now also reads availability from the allowed `Origin`, and its check text and the evidence above say what it shows. The decision is ADR-057 (it was an unnumbered ADR-048 addendum). No lower migration number is on another branch, so the release contract has no numbering gap.

It passed in five reruns and under the non-superuser owner. Identity's own insert takes one timestamp (`mfa.service.ts`). The fixture belongs to D-Eminence/hid-system#71.

(1) As `hid_ehr_api_runtime` a correction row could be inserted for a vital entered in error (only the vital's update was guarded): `ehr.require_final_vital()` now refuses it (`55000 VITAL_ENTERED_IN_ERROR`), reading the vital `FOR SHARE`. (2) A document entered in error could be set back to `uploaded` and be served as available again: `ehr.keep_retracted_document()` now refuses any update of one (`55000 DOCUMENT_ENTERED_IN_ERROR`). (3) The contract's and `DATABASE.md`'s summary of who may close an encounter was wrong for cancellation (which changes only open orders) and for records already closed; both now state it per action. (4) The `403 ENCOUNTER_RETRACTION_NOT_PERMITTED` detail named the missing permissions, telling a receptionist which kinds of record the encounter holds; it is now a fixed sentence and the permissions stay in the audit row.

Merge after 0089 and 0090.

- **Identity and service error and authentication hardening (D-Eminence/hid-system#76, no migration)**, local and synthetic only, on one private PostgreSQL 16 cluster (port 55471, built from empty to 0088 and bootstrapped, removed afterwards); the full TUF rehearsal was not run:
  - **Failing first:** on the tests-only commit `bc55102` (no production code changed), `cd services/identity-api && npx jest src/auth/security.guard.spec.ts src/auth/workload-refusal.spec.ts src/auth/refresh-failure.spec.ts src/auth/sign-in-refusal.spec.ts src/audit/patient-only-refusal-audit.spec.ts src/admin/principal-search-body.spec.ts src/config/cors.spec.ts` fails all seven suites, 46 of 74 tests, for example "answers a runtime-control fault from a statement timeout (57014) with 503 PLATFORM_CONTROL_UNAVAILABLE" (received `[401, "AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED"]`), "keeps the session cookies after a statement timeout (500)" (received `hid_access`, `hid_access_refresh`, `hid_access_csrf`, `hid_enrollment` cleared) and "MfaService issues no challenge and records the refusal for an active staff account without a membership" (received `[401, undefined]`); `workload-refusal.spec.ts` alone fails 15 of 18. In EHR, `cors.spec.ts`, `identity-api.service.spec.ts`, `service-workload-identity.service.spec.ts` and `platform-grant-runtime-controls.spec.ts` fail 11 of 21 tests, and `cors.spec.ts` does not compile (`TS2307: Cannot find module './cors'`), as in Lab, OCR and Outreach; `outreach-security.guard.spec.ts` fails 4 of 15 tests and Pharmacy's `cors.spec.ts` 2 of 7. As `hid_identity_api_runtime` on that cluster: `verify-patient-runtime.mjs` fails "a control fault must not sign the patient out" (`401 !== 503`), `verify-session-sign-out-runtime.mjs` on the cookies cleared by a refresh whose audit write failed, `verify-staff-mfa-runtime.mjs` at the no-membership sign-in (`401 !== 403`), `verify-platform-admin-runtime.mjs` and `verify-platform-security-runtime.mjs` at the `POST` search and export (`404 !== 200`), and `verify-outreach-cookie-read-runtime.mjs` at the other workload's refusal (`AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` instead of `WORKLOAD_AUTHENTICATION_FAILED`).
  - **Fault injection without a schema change:** the verifiers' database stand-ins take a `lock_timeout` seam while a side connection holds `access exclusive` on `platform.control_settings` (patient) or `audit.events` (refresh), in a transaction that is rolled back: `GET /auth/session` answers `503 PLATFORM_CONTROL_UNAVAILABLE` with no `Set-Cookie` and `200` once the lock is gone; a refresh answers `503` with no `Set-Cookie`, the session stays live, the retry rotates (`200`) without reuse, and exactly one `auth.refresh` row exists.
  - **On the head of this change:** Jest passes in Identity (85 suites, 1209 tests), EHR (33, 358), Lab (21, 171), OCR (16, 199), Outreach (11, 65) and Pharmacy (13, 72), and `tsc --noEmit` in all six and in `packages/api-client`. As their runtime roles on the cluster, the runtime verifiers `patient`, `session-sign-out` (new sections: the refresh fault and the patient-only routes, three staff `403 PATIENT_SESSION_REQUIRED` answers with one denied row each), `staff-mfa` (new: six no-membership sign-ins answer `403`, no challenge, no `auth.login_attempts` row, then the activated membership gets `mfa_enrollment_required`), `platform-admin` (an ended membership now answers `403 NO_ACTIVE_FACILITY_MEMBERSHIP` at sign-in), `platform-security`, `otp-recovery`, `patient-enrollment`, `patient-safety`, `patient-search`, `registration-review`, `provider-enrollment`, `membership-duplicates`, `delegated-cookie-read` (EHR, with a real Identity child process), `encounter-lifecycle`, `ehr-lab-handoff`, `lab`, `pharmacy`, `ocr`, `outreach` and `outreach-cookie-read` pass. `verify-migration-ledger.mjs`, `verify-runtime-locking-privileges.mjs` (no violations), `verify-platform-graph.mjs` (after `39ceea8` reworded a comment the graph read as a SQL reference to a schema `main`), `verify-production-convergence.mjs`, the web identity and Outreach contract checks, `npm --prefix release test` (81 of 81), `npm --prefix release run verify` and `git diff --check` pass. No SQL suite is affected (no database change).
  - **Review fixes, after merging 0089, 0090 and 0091:** tests-only commit `11966d4`, then the fix `774f7fa`. On `11966d4` two specs fail: EHR's guard answered a fault with no status of its own `401` (now `503 AUTHENTICATION_UNAVAILABLE`, `remote-security.guard.spec.ts`), and a Google sign-in refused while its session was issued was recorded as `outcome: failure` (now `denied`, `auth.controller.spec.ts`).
