To reproduce, run as the owner of a local cluster migrated to 0073 and role-bootstrapped (Stage 5B Identity calls `auth.lock_account_sessions` on sign-out and expiry; a cluster left at 0072 needs `db:migrate` and `db:bootstrap` again) (`services/ehr-api` `db:migrate` and `db:bootstrap`). The harness defaults to the database `hid_rehearsal`, the superuser `hid_rehearsal_admin` and ports 4010 and 4011. Change them with `HID_E2E_TEMPLATE_DB`, `HID_E2E_DB_SUPERUSER`, `HID_E2E_API_PORT` and `HID_E2E_CONTROL_PORT`; see the script headers. Stop `server.mjs` with Ctrl-C or SIGTERM, which drops its database copy.

1. Build the console from Health-id:

   ```bash
   VITE_HID_ENVIRONMENT=local VITE_HID_API_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:3200 VITE_HID_APP_ORIGIN=http://127.0.0.1:3200 \
     npx vite build --outDir "$OUT"
   ```

2. Serve it, also from Health-id:

   ```bash
   HID_LOCAL_GATEWAY_ORIGIN=http://127.0.0.1:4010 npx vite preview --configLoader runner --outDir "$OUT" --host 127.0.0.1
   ```

3. Start the API from this repository:

   ```bash
   HID_E2E_SOCKET=<socket dir> HID_E2E_WORKDIR=<dir> node services/identity-api/scripts/admin-console-e2e/server.mjs
   ```

4. Run the checks, with the same variables plus `HID_E2E_PLAYWRIGHT=<path to playwright/index.mjs>`:

   ```bash
   node services/identity-api/scripts/admin-console-e2e/flows.mjs
   ```

   The evidence is written to `<dir>/evidence.json`.

## 9. Rollback and recovery

- **Migrations are forward-only.** There are no down migrations. Recovery from a failed migration needs no action, because the transaction rolls back. Recovery from a wrong but successful migration is a restore from P2. Practise it in staging (S6) before relying on it.
- **Identity after 0072:**
  - The previous Stage 4A build runs unchanged, because Stage 5 has no schema change.
  - A pre-4A build also runs. Its nine-argument audit call resolves to the new function through the defaulted arguments; `platform-admin-contracts.integration.sql` checks this.
  - **Do not roll the console back below Stage 4B while Identity is at Stage 4A or later (§3).**
- **Identity after 0073:** Stage 5 and earlier builds run unchanged. The two admin commands keep their contracts, and only Stage 5B calls the new helper. Rolling Identity back to Stage 5 removes the lock from the reuse, own-session, sign-out and expiry paths. The two admin revocations keep it, because it is in the database. With that combination, any of those four paths at the same moment as an administrator's revocation of the same account can deadlock. PostgreSQL then cancels one of the two. Either the sign-out or refresh fails with a `5xx` (reproduced locally, §8), or the administrator's revocation fails and must be retried. Prefer rolling forward.
- **Identity after 0074:** once the bootstrap has run, earlier builds keep working (§3); roll Identity back freely. The new index and the new result columns stay. Rolling back the Health-id portal only hides the outcomes.
- **Identity after Stage 7:** roll back freely; 0076's helper stays and earlier builds do not call it. A Stage 6 or earlier Identity brings back the deadlock between an approved MFA reset and an MFA transaction of the same account (§2): PostgreSQL then cancels one, and either the MFA request fails with a `5xx` or the approval fails and must be retried. It also lets parallel second-factor attempts of one account exceed the failure limit again.
- **0076:** forward-only like every migration. To restore the earlier guard bodies, a new migration would have to recreate them; that would accept a confirmation for any patient again under a non-bypass owner, and validations from sessions without an account or membership. No OCR build depends on the earlier behaviour. The Stage 7 Identity needs `auth.lock_account_for_mfa`.
- **0077:** forward-only. Restoring the earlier bodies would need a new migration and would bring back the NULL claim token, the refused lease renewals, jobs for unscanned documents, publications attributed to any member and the NULL-session gaps of the Lab, Pharmacy and Outreach guards. No service build depends on the earlier behaviour. Revoking the Outreach grant would stop every Outreach write again.
- **0078:** forward-only. Restoring the earlier policy or trigger would need a new migration and would stop every Identity runtime insert of a patient identifier, including the NIN registration reviews (`42501`), and every Lab acceptance of an EHR order (`42703`) again. No build depends on the earlier definitions.
- **Pharmacy, Lab, OCR, Outreach and Identity after Stage 9:** rolling any of them back to an earlier build restores its §4.2 failure (`500` on every affected command) and nothing else; 0078 stays and harms no earlier build. Rows written by the Stage 9 builds stay valid.
- **0079:** forward-only. Restoring the 0033 body would need a new migration and would bring back the bypass: a correct PIN for one patient would again clear the actor's failures at others. No service build depends on either behaviour.
- **0080:** forward-only. Restoring the 0077 bodies would need a new migration and would let Pharmacy accept cancelled or nonexistent prescriptions and dispense work whose prescription was cancelled again. Rolling the Pharmacy API back keeps the refusals and answers them with `500` instead of `409`. Rolling the EHR API back is safe: 0080 does not compare the prescription time or dates, so the earlier EHR's millisecond time is accepted (§3), and day-early dates from an EHR process east of UTC are stored as before 0080. Once a later release compares the time and dates exactly, an EHR build before this change can no longer hand off: that release must record the EHR rollback floor.
- **0081:** forward-only. The table stays after an Identity rollback and harms no earlier build, which ignores it. Dropping it needs a new migration and stops every recovery start of the build that uses it (`5xx`).
- **Identity after the recovery start fix:** roll back freely once 0081 is applied. An earlier build brings back the start's timing and cooldown differences between known and unknown identifiers and the absence of a code budget; blocked budget rows stay in `auth.otp_rate_limits` and lapse on their own.
- **0082:** forward-only. The `direct-care` row stays: grants and requests reference it (`ON DELETE RESTRICT`), and no service build depends on its absence, so rolling back any service changes nothing here. To withdraw the purpose, set `active = false` by a reviewed change; every direct-care grant, request, close and PIN is then refused at once.
- **0083:** forward-only. Restoring 0015's setting would need a new migration, and on RDS every OCR claim would again need `SET ON PARAMETER plpgsql.variable_conflict`. No service build depends on either form.
- **Role bootstrap and platform control owner (D-Eminence/hid-system#63):** rerunning an earlier bootstrap needs a superuser again. To return `platform.control_enabled(text)` to the schema owner, run `alter function platform.control_enabled(text) owner to <migration administrator>` (as a superuser, or as an administrator holding `SET` on `hid_platform_control_reader`) and drop the policy `platform_controls_definer_read`; under an owner without `BYPASSRLS` that brings back `503 PLATFORM_CONTROL_UNAVAILABLE` for every patient and provider request. The role stays harmless while it owns nothing.
- **Start-up check and `db:verify-logins` (D-Eminence/hid-system#63):** code only, no persisted state. Rolling a service back removes its start-up check; `db:verify-logins` changes nothing it checks.
- **0084:** forward-only. The withdrawn permissions, the triggers and the recreated policies stay after any service rollback. Granting a permission back re-enables a module and needs its own reviewed migration, decision and staging acceptance (§2); never restore it by hand. Dropping the triggers or restoring the 0030 policies also needs a reviewed migration, and would let records be added to cancelled and retracted encounters again and release notes of those encounters to the patient again.
- **EHR after 0084:** rolling the EHR API back keeps the database refusals, which an earlier build answers `500` instead of `409` (nothing written); a retraction no longer cascades, so records of an encounter retracted meanwhile need the P12 review; `completed` to `entered_in_error` is refused again; the patient summary releases notes of encounters that are not completed again (the policies still do not); and every clinical note create fails again with `500` (`42P08`) (§3). Versions and audit rows written by the cascade stay valid.
- **0090:** forward-only. The triggers, the replaced vital guard, the vital status and the runtime grant stay after any service rollback. Dropping a trigger, or the grant, needs a reviewed migration (or, for the grant, a reviewed change of `runtime-grants.sql`) and would let a member retract records they may not write, rewrite closed orders and encounters, correct a retracted vital, restore a retracted document so it is served again, or block retractions; never do it by hand. Vitals already entered in error stay so: nothing turns them back to `final`.
- **EHR after 0090:** rolling the EHR API back keeps the database refusals, which an earlier build answers `500` instead of `403` or `409` (nothing written); its retraction no longer covers vitals, completed orders or documents, and there is no vital retraction route; `GET /ehr/me/records` answers a staff session `503` again, and refusals lose their semantic audit row and the `status` and `code` details again. Audit rows already written stay valid.
- **Identity after D-Eminence/hid-system#64:** code only. Rolling back answers break-glass without an `emergency` purpose `400 INVALID_CONSENT_COMMAND` again; nothing else changes.
- **Launch controls:** an operator who sets `outreach_portal_enabled`, `uploads_enabled` or `break_glass_enabled` back on does so through `POST /admin/controls`, which records the change; for `break_glass_enabled` that is not enough while no `emergency` purpose exists (P10).
- **0085:** forward-only. The replaced activation command, the counters and the command stay after an Identity rollback; an earlier build never calls the command, and the activation command's two refusals reach it as `503` and `409` without writing anything, the `409` leaving the enrollment stranded until it expires (§3). Restoring the 0058 activation body needs a new migration and would let a replay show the HID for 24 hours and a stale contact proof activate again. Dropping the command needs a new migration and stops every enrollment start of this Identity build that reaches QoreID (`503`).
- **Identity after D-Eminence/hid-system#68:** can be rolled back once 0085 is applied, with one stranding effect. An earlier build brings back the `409 NIN_ALREADY_REGISTERED` answer while the gate is closed, the HID in `GET /current` until the enrollment expires, 24 hours after start, the cookie kept at activation and uncapped QoreID lookups, and accepts `QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED` in production again; leave that flag off. It does not bring back activation replays after 15 minutes or contact proofs of any age, because 0085 still refuses both: a replay after 15 minutes answers `503 ENROLLMENT_UNAVAILABLE`, and an activation whose contact proof is more than 30 minutes old answers `409 ENROLLMENT_STAGE_INVALID` with no way back to the contact step, so that patient cannot activate until the enrollment expires, 24 hours after its start, and then starts again (§3). No operator action is needed; the enrollment lapses at its expiry. Quota counters and audit rows written meanwhile stay and lapse with the prune.
- **Identity after FE-4 (same branch, no migration):** rolling Identity back to a build without it removes `GET /identity/patient-enrollments/availability` (`404`) and brings back the generic detail on the gate's `503`. Health-id 82b474e does not call the route; a Health-id that does treats any answer other than `200 { "open": true }` as not open, so a rollback keeps registration shown as closed. Nothing is stored.
- **0086:** forward-only. The external sign-in identities it revoked stay revoked, and a revoked Google subject cannot be linked again (0059 refuses any subject ever linked), so the owner can link only a different Google account until a later change allows re-linking; the narrowed policy, functions, staff list rule, index and scopes stay. Restoring the 0011 policy would let every registration member read every patient again; restoring the 0010 `create_access_request` would bring back the `P0002` answer that tells an unknown HID. Both need a new migration.
- **Identity after the recovery and patient search hardening:** roll back only together with a decision, because an earlier build brings back every closed path: recovery codes to any account kind and to unverified emails, two sign-in budgets per account, the distinct `403`/`404` lookup answers, the `patientId` in access-request answers and in the clinician's list before a grant is active, approvals without recent sign-in and no recovery hold. An earlier build also reads registration duplicates directly from `identity.patients`, which 0086 now limits (§3), so a rollback can miss a duplicate at registration. Prefer rolling forward.
- **0087:** forward-only. Do not drop the one-active-membership index or restore the wider `staff_memberships_scoped_uq`: re-attached clinicians would collide with their ended memberships, and duplicates would make facility binding ambiguous again. Rolling Identity back to a build without this change is safe after 0087: earlier builds do not call the workforce commands or `auth.facility_role_is_effective`, and keep resolving staff as before; only an attested role whose attestation has lapsed then gives its permissions again through the older staff context, and staff see their memberships' stored labels as roles again, until the new build returns. To stop provisioning without a deploy, revoke `platform.workforce.manage` from `platform_super_admin` by a reviewed change; existing grants are unaffected. Never reactivate an ended membership: the trigger refuses it; attach the person again instead. Attestations, role-grant records and the lifecycle of ended memberships are evidence and stay.
- **0088:** forward-only, and harmless to an earlier Identity build, which writes platform challenges only (the defaults fit) and never reads the proof columns; its platform enrolment creates factors without a proof, which verify platform sessions as before. Removing the columns or restoring the 0069 guards needs a new migration; the 0069 assurance guard would refuse every staff sign-in of the new build. Factors enrolled at staff sign-in keep their proof whatever build runs.
- **Identity after staff MFA:** roll back only together with a decision, because an earlier build issues staff sessions on the password alone again (by `/auth/login`, provider activation and a staff Google exchange) and accepts sessions without assurance. Staff sessions issued by the new build stay valid under the earlier build; the earlier build's staff sessions are refused again (`403 STAFF_MFA_REQUIRED`) once the new build is redeployed. Factors and recovery codes enrolled by staff stay and are used again on roll-forward. Health-id builds with the staff MFA sign-in do not work with an earlier Identity, whose `/auth/login` answers a session: roll Health-id back with it. Prefer rolling forward.
- **0089:** forward-only, and harmless to an earlier Identity build, which never reads or writes `auth.session_facility_selection`. The table, its guard and the bootstrap's grants stay after an Identity rollback; dropping them needs a new migration, and the Identity build of D-Eminence/hid-system#73 would then answer every staff `GET /auth/session` `503`, every staff refresh `unavailable` and `POST /auth/facility` a `5xx`. Stored choices are only the default a session reports and authorize nothing, so they need no clean-up; a choice outlives its sign-in only as a row no live session reads.
- **Identity after the facility selection port:** code only; roll back freely once 0089 is applied. An earlier build stores no choice and reports no `facilitySelection`, so after a reload Health-id `82b474e` silently reports the primary facility again (the C1/FE-20 defect); `X-Facility-ID` still authorizes every facility-scoped request, so nothing is read or written at a facility the clinician does not hold. Rows written by this build stay and are reported again on roll-forward while their sign-in is live.
- **Identity after the Turnstile contract change:** code only; roll back freely. An earlier build answers Siteverify's server-side errors `403 TURNSTILE_REJECTED`, does not retry Siteverify, accepts an `admin-login` token on `/auth/login`, answers a missing token on five routes `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`, writes no Turnstile log line, and starts with a `CORS_ORIGINS` host that has no Turnstile actions (where every gated route then refuses with `403 TURNSTILE_CONTEXT_MISMATCH`). No state is stored.
- **0091:** forward-only. Re-granting a withdrawn permission, turning a launch control on, or restoring an earlier `ehr.patient_self_context` or request list is a new reviewed migration (a module's re-grant with its decision; the patient-self binding should never be undone). An emergency re-enable of a control goes through the audited `POST /admin/controls`, which records its actor. Earlier EHR and Identity builds keep working on 0091 (§3): an earlier EHR completes uploads without the control check, which no role can reach, and an earlier Identity returns the request list and history without the new fields. The reverse does not hold for Identity: restoring a pre-0091 backup (P2) also takes Identity back to the build before this change, because the new Identity selects the request list's 0091 columns and answers a patient's `GET /identity/me/access-requests` with `500` without them (§3); the new EHR build works on the earlier schema. Re-granting a withdrawn permission is a migration for every environment, never a hand grant on one database: the bootstrap's role assertions refuse a role holding a withdrawn permission (ADR-058). With 0090 applied, an encounter holding an initiated or uploaded document cannot be marked entered in error until uploads are re-enabled (§2); cancelling it is not affected. The `PATIENT_ACCESS_PIN_USED` inbox items stay, as every inbox item does.
- **0092:** forward-only. The 9-argument activation command stays after an Identity rollback, and an earlier Identity build works on it unchanged: its 7-argument call resolves to it (the new arguments default to null) and it never starts a record without a date of birth or gender. Restoring the 7-argument function alone needs a new migration, which would strand a pending enrollment whose record lacks those fields (it could no longer activate). Patients activated with self-reported values keep them; the authoritative profile and `selfReportedFields` keep showing which values were entered.
- **Identity after D-Eminence/hid-system#82:** code only; roll back freely once 0092 and the bootstrap are applied. An earlier build answers a start of a record without a date of birth or gender `502 QOREID_PROVIDER_RESPONSE_INVALID` again, and drops `requiredProfileFields`. A pending enrollment already started with such a record by this build cannot activate under the earlier build (its activation passes no date of birth or gender, and the command refuses it: `409 ENROLLMENT_STAGE_INVALID`) until Identity is rolled forward or the enrollment expires 24 hours after its start. Production is not affected while NIN-only enrollment is off there.
- **0093:** forward-only. Its schema stays after an Identity rollback. An earlier Identity build on it cannot register anyone (it fails closed, §3); sign-in and recovery are unchanged. Restoring the unique NIN constraint or removing the holder check needs a new migration and would let a NIN alone create a Health ID again: do not. Enrollments the backfill sent back to `verify_holder` expire 24 hours after their start. Holder events are pruned after 31 days (send, failure) and 366 days (proof).
- **Identity after D-Eminence/hid-system#84:** roll back only to a build that keeps registration closed. The earlier build answers `409 NIN_ALREADY_REGISTERED` at start again (the existence answer this change removed) and cannot finish any enrollment on 0093. Turn `QOREID_NIN_ONLY_ENROLLMENT_ENABLED` off first, so that every start answers `503` before it reads a NIN. A pending enrollment proven by this build waits at the contact step until Identity is rolled forward or it expires.
- **notification-api after D-Eminence/hid-system#84:** roll back only with the online route off (`NIN_HOLDER_SMS_ENABLED=false`): an earlier build refuses the holder purpose and Identity sends no code (`503 OTP_DELIVERY_UNAVAILABLE`).
- **0094:** forward-only. Its tables, commands and grants stay after an Identity rollback. An earlier Identity (0093's) works on it: it calls the replaced proof and activation commands with the same signatures and never the new ones; its availability answers `facility: false`. To stop the facility route without a rollback, suspend the designations (P30) or turn `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED` off. In production, suspending the last active designation also closes the online holder route, because Identity opens it only while a facility takes part: every new registration stops. Keep one designation active to keep the online route open. Production refuses `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED=false` while `NIN_HOLDER_SMS_ENABLED` is on (Identity does not start and the synth is refused), so turn the online route off first. Removing the in-person checks or the designation table needs a new migration and loses the audit trail behind each in-person proof: do not.
- **0096:** forward-only, and safe to keep under any earlier EHR build: the 0096 policies and the view admit a category only to a patient-self transaction that names it in `app.patient_release`, which only the EHR build of this change sets. An earlier build, including one from before the record summaries change (D-Eminence/hid-system#52) that still reads all seven categories in the patient context, therefore reads nothing new on a database with 0096. To stop a release without a rollback, turn its `EHR_PATIENT_RELEASE_*` flag off (the summary drops the category and the download answers `404`); a signed download URL already issued stays valid for its 60 s. Dropping the policies or the view needs a new migration (and the bootstrap's assertions changed with it).
- **Identity after D-Eminence/hid-system#85:** roll back to 0093's build only with `NIN_HOLDER_SMS_ENABLED` off in production (that build refuses it there). An enrollment proven in person waits at the contact step and activates under either build, because the activation command decides. A desk code shown by this build is not shown by the earlier one.
- **0095:** forward-only. Its two functions and grants stay after a worker rollback, and an earlier worker ignores them. Dropping them needs a new migration; a flag-on worker would then refuse to start.
- **Notification worker after the Novu verified email change (ADR-064):** with `NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC` never set to `email`, roll back freely. Once it has been `email` anywhere, the emails it synced stay on the Novu subscribers, and main's trigger (sent by a flag-off or earlier worker) can email them, opted-out and deleted patients included. Before setting the flag off or rolling the worker back: (1) stop the worker (desired count 0), so nothing is synced while the emails are cleared (events wait in the queue, within its retention); (2) list the patients as the migration administrator: `select distinct envelope.patient_id from notification.delivery_attempts attempt join integration.outbox_envelopes envelope on envelope.event_id = attempt.event_id where attempt.workflow_id = 'novu-subscriber-email-v1';`; (3) for each, `PATCH /v2/subscribers/{patient id}` with `{"email": null}` (or `DELETE /v2/subscribers/{patient id}`) on that environment's Novu, and confirm in the Novu dashboard that no subscriber keeps an email; (4) then set `HID_NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC_ENABLED=false` (or deploy the earlier worker) and start it. Record the run in ADR-064. Emails mapped by hand (`STAGING_PROVIDER_ACCOUNTS.md`) are not in the list and are removed the same way.
- **OCR after 0075:** roll the OCR API and worker back freely. Earlier workers lose the metrics again; earlier APIs write the earlier, possibly unlinked, events. The function, the role and both policies stay. Removing the outbox insert policy would stop patient confirmation and publication again.
- **Delegated cookie reads (#38):** code only, no persisted state. Rolling back Identity brings back the `403 ORIGIN_DENIED` refusal for the same-origin cookie reads of all four services (EHR, Lab, OCR and Pharmacy). Rolling back EHR brings it back for EHR's and OCR's cookie reads (OCR reads each job's source document through EHR); rolling back Lab, OCR or Pharmacy brings it back for that service only (§3). Nothing else of #38 changes; an EHR API rolled back past the paging fix also loses that fix (next entry).
- **Outreach cookie reads (D-Eminence/hid-system#43):** code only, no persisted state. Rolling back either Outreach or Identity brings back the refusal of Outreach's same-origin cookie reads: `403 CSRF_VALIDATION_FAILED` from an earlier Outreach, `403 ORIGIN_DENIED` from an earlier Identity (§3). Rolling back Identity also brings back `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` on the two campaign reads for bearer requests (a same-origin cookie read is already refused `403`, as above). The three campaign writes answer `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` with either Identity, so a rollback changes nothing for them. Registration-case writes are unaffected.
- **EHR to Lab order time (#44):** code only; no schema or grant change. Rolling the EHR API back brings back Lab's refusal (SQLSTATE `23514`; `500` from a Lab build before D-Eminence/hid-system#56, `409 LAB_ORDER_NOT_CURRENT` from a later one) of almost every new EHR order acceptance. Work items Lab accepted from the new build keep a digest over the microsecond text, so a same-key replay of one of those orders through the earlier build gets `409 IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`; Lab keeps the work item (§3).
- **EHR to Lab handoff (D-Eminence/hid-system#56):** code only; no schema or grant change. Rolling the EHR API back removes `POST .../lab-work-item` and `labHandoff`, makes Lab's status and code the create's failure again, and stops updates from reaching Lab; requests saved meanwhile stay valid, and Lab keeps their work items. Rolling the Lab API back brings back `500` for the acceptance refusals of §2, the narrower body limits and version 4 ids only, which this EHR then reports as `502 LAB_HANDOFF_UNCONFIRMED` or `409 LAB_HANDOFF_REFUSED` (create) or `failed` (update) (§3). Lab's other commands answer a database refusal `500` in both builds, so rolling either service back changes nothing for OCR's Lab import.
- **EHR clinical list paging:** code only, no persisted state. Rolling the EHR API back brings back the rows skipped within a millisecond. Cursors issued by either build are refused by the other with `400 INVALID_CURSOR`, after which Health-id reloads the first page (§3).
- **Record summaries:** code only, no persisted state. Rolling the EHR API back brings back the seven-category patient read with five always-empty arrays and no `withheld`, an emergency summary that returns every category whatever the reader's category read permissions, unscanned and rejected documents in the emergency summary, `limit` 100 without truncation, and an emergency audit without counts. Audit events written with the new details stay valid rows.
- **Refresh-event change:** this is code only. Rolling back Identity brings back the old misclassification and nothing else. Existing `refresh`/`denied` events remain valid rows.
- **Enrollment contact-code budget:** code only. Rolling Identity back brings back the per-challenge limit alone (about 2,400 guesses a day per contact) and stops checking the blocks. Its `recipient` rows in `auth.otp_rate_limits` stay valid and are ignored by earlier builds; nothing prunes them, as for every other bucket of that table, though enrollment's own rate rows are pruned after 48 hours (§2, retention follow-up).
- **Session-route refusal audits:** code only. Rolling Identity back brings back the `503` for a staff refusal on a session route, and returns patient and anonymous sign-out refusals there to `api.*` rows. The `auth.*` failure rows it wrote remain valid rows.
- **Sign-out and client kind (#39):** code only. Rolling Identity back brings back the idle sign-out that answers `401` and leaves the sign-in live, and removes the client-kind check and the `x-hid-client` CORS entry. `logout` events marked `presented: refresh_token` remain valid rows.
- **Purpose of use and OCR workload refusals (D-Eminence/hid-system#60):** code only, no schema change. Rolling Identity, EHR, OCR, Lab or Pharmacy back brings back the `500 INTERNAL_ERROR` for a missing or unknown `X-Purpose-Of-Use` on that service's routes listed in §2. Rolling OCR back also brings back the pass-through of an EHR, Lab or Pharmacy workload refusal as `401` or `403`, which a publication then stores as terminal. Publications the new build stored as `OWNING_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` stay retryable under an earlier build, which already treats that code as retryable.
- **EHR and OCR guard refusals before a facility is bound (#46):** code only, no persisted state. Rolling the EHR or OCR API back brings back the `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` answer, with no audit row, for those refusals, and in EHR the `401 AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` answer to a patient whose control check hits a database fault. `auth.authorization.denied` rows already written stay valid.
- **Released Lab results:** code only. Rolling the Lab API back returns unreleased correction values to released-only history readers again, removes the order route (`404`) and stops the read audit events. Audit rows already written remain valid.
- **Enrollment and session hardening:** code only. Rolling Identity back brings back the argon2id run on every activation replay and unlimited activation, the enrollment cookie that outlives sign-in and sign-out, and `SESSION_CLIENT_MISMATCH` for a sign-in whose actor refresh cannot resolve. Enrollment-scope rate rows it wrote stay valid and lapse with their window.
- **Document upload completion (D-Eminence/hid-system#59):** code only, no persisted state. Rolling the EHR API back lets any facility member with `ehr.document.write` and a write grant complete a colleague's upload again, and answers a colleague's call after the uploader's completion with `200` until the scan is clean or rejected. Documents completed by either build stay valid rows.
- **Production workload identity (ADR-047, D-Eminence/hid-system#54):** no persisted application state. Before any production task runs, leave the issuer in place; from then on, scale the token-bearing services to zero and never switch a service to `local-secret`. Minting stops at once with the issuer's reserved concurrency at `0`; issued tokens expire within 300 seconds. On suspected key misuse, reserved concurrency `0` also stops the JWKS; disable the key as well (a warm issuer would stop publishing it within five minutes anyway). Receivers accept a token signed with the key only while it is at most 300 seconds old and only until their cached JWKS expires, at most 10 minutes later; a token pre-signed with a future `iat` is ended only by a new key. The signing key and issuer log group are retained. Rolling a receiver back brings back RS256 and tokens without `exp`; rolling Identity back also sends an expired Notification token again; rolling the agent back brings back a JWKS fetch for every renewal; rolling the regional stack back removes the key-policy Deny statements, the web ACL and the restart policy and makes the agent essential again. `AWS_DEPLOYMENT_RUNBOOK.md` §12 has the steps.
- **Edge and gateway change (D-Eminence/hid-system#65):** code and configuration only, no persisted state. Roll back in the reverse of §6 step 11: Identity first (an earlier build charges the shared buckets again, keyed per address), then the gateway image with its regional stack (back to a resolve-once gateway that routes every module and the internal routes, and forwards the load balancer's address), then, if wanted, the Workers (the header is harmless to an earlier gateway). Never roll a Worker back while a gateway requiring the header runs: every API request through that Worker would answer `403 CLIENT_ADDRESS_REQUIRED` (it fails closed; an earlier Worker deletes a caller's `X-Real-IP`, so no caller-chosen address is trusted). Rate rows keyed by an IPv6 /64 simply expire.
- **Launch frontend hosting (ADR-052):** configuration and staged artefacts only, no persisted state. To roll a host's frontend back, restage the previous accepted Health-id commit's build for that host, run `verify:launch:<environment>`, and redeploy that Worker only; no API image or schema changes. Do not point `hid-web` or `hid-admin` back at the legacy `apps/web` or `apps/admin` builds: their retired routes answer `404` and their staff sign-in is refused. A host that is not deployed stays undeployed; its API stays closed at the gateway.
- **Edge and gateway hardening (ADR-055):** code and configuration only, no persisted state. Each part rolls back alone. Rolling the regional stack back restores the WAF's 8 KB body block on clinical notes (long notes refused again) and the rate limit per Cloudflare egress address. Rolling the gateway image back restores the interception of backend 5xx (with the `Set-Cookie` drop on a refresh `503`), nginx's 1 MiB body default, the `main` access log with query strings, the forwarded origin secret and a public `scan-events`. Rolling the Workers back removes HSTS (browsers that saw it keep it for up to a year, which is harmless while every host is HTTPS-only) and the policies. To relax the portal policy for a Health-id build that needs another origin, change `HEALTH_ID_CONTENT_SECURITY_POLICY` in a reviewed Worker release; never by removing the policy.
- **MFA key:** if `MFA_SECRET_KEY_B64` is lost, every enrolled authenticator must be re-enrolled. Store it with the same care as the field-encryption keys.
- **Identity MFA key in the task definition (D-Eminence/hid-system#49):** infrastructure only. Rolling the regional stack back to a task definition without the reference brings back `503 MFA_UNAVAILABLE` for every platform sign-in and changes nothing else; enrolled authenticators work again once the same key and `aws-v1` return. Keep the `mfaSecretKeyB64` field for as long as any deployed task definition references it: ECS resolves it whenever it starts an Identity task (deployment, scaling, replacement). Never change the key or the version to roll back.
- **Lockout:** if every Super Admin is locked out (lost authenticators and recovery codes), recovery needs the documented break-glass procedure. That procedure is not built yet; see the product decisions in `PHASE_4_STAGE_4A_ADMIN_CONTRACTS.md` §9. Keep two independent Super Admins with stored recovery codes.

**Known limits of Stage 6:**
- Queue metrics are emitted with each claim. A worker that claims nothing (stopped, stalled, wrong provider, ineligible documents) emits none, and `treatMissingData` is `NOT_BREACHING`. So `OcrQueueAgeAlarm` cannot detect a stopped worker, and `OcrDrainRateAlarm` cannot breach. Fixing this needs two changes, both out of scope here:
  - an OCR worker change to log a separate, periodic queue-metrics event from `ocr.worker_queue_metrics()` (it cannot reuse `ocr.job.claimed`, whose `claimedJobs` feeds the throughput metric);
  - matching metric filters in `infra/aws`.
- The OCR worker's own database audit events (`ocr.worker.claim`, `ocr.worker.complete`, `ocr.worker.fail`, `ocr.worker.lease_recovered`) still carry `ocr.jobs.patient_id`, so for a job created without a patient they remain unlinked.
- The age is time since the earliest `queued_at`, as before. A job retried by the worker keeps its first `queued_at`, so the age includes earlier attempts and back-off.
- Replays of idempotent OCR writes return the stored result without a new read event; the original write event remains.

**Lock-order hazards:** the MFA reset hazard recorded here until Stage 6 is fixed by Stage 7A (§2). These pre-existing hazards remain; none involves an MFA authenticator. In each, PostgreSQL cancels one command, which fails and can be retried; nothing is half applied:
- two administrators acting on each other's accounts at the same moment. A status change, a platform role change, an approval request or an approval decision locks its target's account row and then references its actor's (the idempotency row, `requested_by`, `decided_by`, `revoked_by`, the audit event). A session revocation joins the cycle when the target's account id sorts before the actor's (§5, step 6);
- a new approval request and a decision on an earlier request for the same target and action, exactly as that earlier request expires;
- an OTP password-recovery resend and the completion of the same recovery: the resend locks the open challenge and then inserts a new one (a `FOR KEY SHARE` on the account), while `auth.complete_recovery_otp` locks the account and then that challenge. It needs the resend cooldown to have passed while the challenge is still open.

Each would be fixed the same way: lock every account involved, in account order, before anything else. Stage 8 reproduced all three locally as real deadlocks (`40P01`, `pg_stat_database.deadlocks` +1 each), running the Identity API's own transactions as a login of the Identity runtime on a 0076 copy:
- two Super Admins disabling, or granting roles to, each other; an approval of an MFA reset of B while B requests one for A; and a revocation of A's sessions by B while A disables B. Sent in the same event-loop tick, two role changes deadlocked in 78 of 200 runs; 0.5–1 ms apart, in 1–2 of 100; 2 ms apart, never. The cancelled side gets `503 ADMIN_COMMAND_UNAVAILABLE`, wrote nothing (no idempotency or audit row) and can be retried with the same key. Which side is cancelled depends on timing: in two runs it was a Super Admin's revocation of a compromised administrator's sessions, which then stayed live until retried;
- a decision landing within milliseconds of its request's expiry while a new request for the same target and action arrives: either side may be cancelled; an expired request never executes;
- an OTP recovery resend between the completion's account lock and its challenge lock: the cancelled side answers `500` instead of `403` or `202`;
- a fourth hazard, not listed before: a Super Admin suspending or rejecting a verified facility (`identity.admin_transition_facility`, 0072, which takes the last-Super-Admin advisory lock and then references its actor's account) while another disables that Super Admin or revokes their Super Admin role (which lock the target account and then take the same advisory lock). Reproduced: the disable was cancelled and left nothing.

A prototype on a copy (the 0073 ordered account lock in `auth.admin_transition_account`, `auth.admin_change_platform_role`, `auth.admin_request_approval` and `auth.admin_decide_approval`, the latter reading the request's target unlocked first, and `auth.lock_account_for_mfa` in `OtpService.start`) removed those deadlocks, 0 of 200 in the same-tick test, and the existing suites passed. An independent check then found that it does not fix the facility hazard and opens a new cycle: two concurrent requests of one administrator, one suspending a facility and one disabling another Super Admin, deadlock with each other (reproduced; they do not on the current code). It is not in Stage 8: each hazard is fail-safe and needs two administrators or a recovering user acting within about a millisecond, while the fix rewrites the two-person approval and last-Super-Admin commands and needs two-connection race tests. A command that waited for its actor to be disabled or signed out also proceeds or fails on the audit trigger (`503`) instead of `403`; re-checking the actor's permission after the lock belongs with that change.

**Known limits of Stage 7B**, all closed by 0077 (§2): the OCR job trigger's scan comparison for inserts, source changes and starts of processing, the NULL claim token of the OCR worker commands, the Lab OCR-import guard's unchecked lookups, the thirteen Lab, Pharmacy and Outreach guards that compared the session with `<>`, and the unbound OCR publication requester. For the twelve guards whose coverage had not been checked, row-level security refuses a NULL session for the runtime roles (reproduced locally); the guards themselves now refuse it too.

**Known limits of Stage 9.** None is introduced by Stage 9; each was hidden behind the §4.2 failures:
- A unique conflict that a retry cannot resolve still answers `500`, after one retry: two members verifying or releasing one Lab result version, at once or one after the other (`result_verifications` / `result_releases` unique keys), a second result entry for one Lab execution under a new key (`results_execution_id_key`), a prescription or OCR publication already accepted or imported in another facility (hidden from the replay read by row-level security), and keys reused across targets in some commands. Mapping these constraints to `409` is a follow-up; for the cross-facility case it must decide what a `409` may disclose, and for the OCR import it must keep OCR's retry (§3). Since D-Eminence/hid-system#56 Lab's EHR-order acceptance alone maps its own: a `lab.work_items` key left after the retry (a work item row-level security hides from the actor) is `409 LAB_ORDER_ALREADY_ACCEPTED`, naming no version, and an order Lab holds at another version is answered before the insert (`409 LAB_ORDER_ALREADY_ACCEPTED` for a later version, `409 LAB_ORDER_NOT_CURRENT` for an earlier one).
- Contention costs latency. Concurrent writes on small tables cancel each other under `SERIALIZABLE` even when they touch different rows; the retry lets them through one by one, so under a burst a request can take several attempts (9 at most in the local measurements, the shipped policy included). Pharmacy dispense and reverse, the Lab accession, specimen, execution and result commands and the OCR validation, confirmation and publication requests call Identity (and OCR also the EHR) inside their transaction, which lengthens every attempt, and each attempt repeats that call, which Identity audits (`identity.authorization.check`); each OCR attempt also reads the source document from the EHR, which records a patient-linked `ehr.document.ocr-source.read` event and EHR's own Identity check, so an access report built from the audit trail can count one OCR command several times. Lab writes that audit on a second connection of its pool while the transaction holds the first, so a burst of as many Lab commands as the pool has connections (`DATABASE_POOL_MAX`) can stall until the 5 s connection timeout and answer `503`; accession creation reaches this since Stage 9, the specimen, execution and result commands did before. Authorizing before the transaction, as Pharmacy acceptance and import, Lab import and Outreach already do, is the recommended follow-up. As the tables grow, their index pages split and fewer unrelated requests collide.
- Pharmacy, Lab and Outreach replays match the stored row by its target (prescription, work item, dispensing, publication), not by actor and key, so a second member who sends an identical body with another key receives the first member's row as a replay, after passing the same authorization. The Lab accession replay read can match two rows (one by work item, one by key) and then answers a replay or `409` depending on plan order.
- OCR publication: only `OWNING_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` and `REQUEST_IN_PROGRESS` count as retryable failures, for a later request and for the claim alike. A failure that is not a `4xx` refusal (an owning domain's `5xx`, a timeout, a lost connection, or an error inside the attempt) is stored as `OWNING_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE`, and the code it arrived with stays in `failure_summary` and in the audit event's `ownerFailureCode`: either the owning domain's own code (the Lab API's default code for a `503` is `DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`), or the code OCR's integration gives a call that got no answer (`LAB_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE`, `EHR_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE`, `PHARMACY_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE`, `IDENTITY_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE`). A retryable failure can be claimed again 15 s later, up to `max_attempts` (3) attempts, so an owning domain's `5xx` that is not transient also uses every attempt; this trade-off comes with the retry fix (§8), where such a failure was terminal at once. After the last one a publication answers `409 OCR_PUBLICATION_IN_PROGRESS`, not `409 OCR_PUBLICATION_TERMINAL`, and no operator path retries it. A retry repeats the original request, from the same account in the same facility, with its `Idempotency-Key` and an identical body; the EHR review workspace keeps that key only while it stays open, so after a reload its Retry answers `409 OCR_ALREADY_PUBLISHED` (a frontend follow-up). A `4xx` refusal other than `REQUEST_IN_PROGRESS` stays terminal under the owning domain's code. In JWT workload-identity mode, the Identity, EHR, Lab and Pharmacy APIs each answer `401` when they cannot verify the caller's token, and a failure to fetch their JWKS is such a failure (`workload-auth.service.ts` in Identity, `ocr-internal-caller.guard.ts` in EHR, `lab-workload-identity.service.ts`, `pharmacy-workload-identity.service.ts`). Every attempt asks Identity and reads the source document from EHR. Since D-Eminence/hid-system#60, OCR answers a workload refusal by EHR, Lab or Pharmacy as `503 <SERVICE>_SERVICE_AUTH_UNAVAILABLE` and the publication stores it as retryable (§2); a JWKS outage at Identity still ends a publication as terminal, because Identity's workload refusals cannot be told apart from a user refusal by code (a follow-up in Identity).
- Some refusals by the database still answer `500` where the service has no mapping, for example a Lab member verifying a result they entered (`23514 Result verifier must be independent`; nothing is written, and Stage 9 makes the Lab result path reachable for the first time), a row-level security refusal when Identity allowed a request the database does not (a Lab import, for one), or an Outreach trigger refusal reached in a race (`42501`, `23514`). Since D-Eminence/hid-system#56 Lab's EHR-order acceptance alone maps its own: the guard's refusal of the work-item insert (`23514`) is `409 LAB_ORDER_NOT_CURRENT` and row-level security's (`42501`) `403 LAB_ACCESS_DENIED`. A Lab runtime missing the `INSERT` grant on `lab.work_items` (`42501`, a deployment fault) is also answered `403` there; the log line names the SQLSTATE and route. Every other Lab command keeps answering `500`; for the OCR import, OCR stores it as the retryable `OWNING_SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` (§3).
- Outreach campaign registration requires `outreach.campaign.write` (§3). Deactivating that permission also stops campaign-linked registration.
- Lab orders created on the EHR while Lab acceptance failed have no work item (§10). Since D-Eminence/hid-system#56 each can be sent with `POST .../lab-work-item`.
- Lock-order hazards: Stage 9 changed one (the OCR publication cycle, §2, fixed and race-tested). The administrator lock-order hazards below are unchanged. Every service write takes `FOR KEY SHARE` on its actor's account, membership and facility rows through foreign keys, which an administrator command locking those rows `FOR UPDATE` waits for or makes wait; the Stage 9 mapping found no cycle with them, because no service write locks an identity row first.
- P8: OCR patient confirmation and campaign-linked Outreach registration still depend on a schema owner that can read row-level-secured rows (§4).
- Identity NIN resolve (`POST /identity/nin/resolve`, not a locking path): the service recognises a duplicate request by the constraint name `registration_cases_facility_id_created_by_account_id_idempotency_key_key`, but PostgreSQL truncated that name to 63 bytes (`..._idempo_key`, 0011). The loser of two concurrent identical resolve requests therefore gets `500` instead of the replay. Found by the Stage 9 mapping; a follow-up.

**Known limits of Stage 8:**
- Twelve Identity definer functions gate on `platform.current_actor_subject() <> 'system:auth'`, which passes when the session has no subject, and two compare a stored enrollment token HMAC with `<>` against the caller's, which passes for a NULL argument (`identity.activate_public_patient_enrollment`, `auth.bind_google_onboarding_capability`). Only the Identity runtime can execute them, it may set `system:auth` itself, and the Identity API always passes a computed HMAC; defence in depth for a later stage.
- 0077 makes the guards refuse a missing value only where they already compared it. So, under a session that bypasses row-level security: the Pharmacy work-item event guard still does not compare the facility; the Outreach event guard compares neither the facility nor the purpose; the Outreach campaign guard never compares the purpose, and its update branch compares only the facility, so a campaign's status can change with no account in the session. The event's actor must still be its work item's accepter (Pharmacy) or the session's account (Outreach). For the runtime roles, row-level security requires all of them.
- Seven Lab triggers compare only the facility with `platform.current_facility_id()` using `<>` (`validate_specimen_parent`, `validate_specimen_transition`, `validate_accession_child`, `validate_execution_transition`, `validate_result_insert`, `validate_result_head_update`, `validate_execution_child`). Row-level security requires the session facility for the Lab runtime.
- The Lab, Pharmacy, OCR, notification and event runtimes cannot call `platform.current_account_id()` directly (no `USAGE` on schema `auth`). No policy, invoker trigger or service of theirs does; a future one would need the grant and the schema usage.

Each is a follow-up.

**Known limits of the delegated cookie-read fix (#38):**
- Identity cannot check the reported method. Any approved workload caller with valid credentials can have the cookie Origin and CSRF check skipped on the patient authorization check by reporting `GET`, `HEAD` or `OPTIONS`. A real browser write is still checked first: EHR, Lab, OCR and Pharmacy revalidate the session through `POST /auth/service-session` (Outreach checks writes in `outreach-security.guard.ts` and through `POST /identity/outreach/authorize`, which #38 does not mark; the Outreach cookie-read branch marks it, and after a reported read it then authorizes only a read permission). The reported method is the method of the request the service is serving: for a service hop that forwards the user's cookie, such as OCR's `GET /api/v1/ehr/internal/ocr/documents/:documentId/source` during job creation, EHR reports that hop's `GET`, after OCR has already revalidated the originating browser write.
- Before D-Eminence/hid-system#43, Outreach's own guard (`outreach-security.guard.ts`) required an allowed Origin and an `X-CSRF-Token` on every cookie request, `GET` included, and refused it otherwise with `403 CSRF_VALIDATION_FAILED`. A same-origin browser `GET` carries no Origin, and neither the Cloudflare frontend worker (`infra/cloudflare/src/frontend-worker.mjs`) nor the gateway (`gateway/proxy-headers.conf`) adds one. Health-id also sends `X-CSRF-Token` only on writes (Health-id `packages/http/src/client.ts`). Same-origin cookie reads from Outreach were therefore refused. This was first recorded from reading the code; it has since been reproduced locally over HTTP, together with the two Identity refusals behind it, and all three are fixed by the Outreach cookie-read change (D-Eminence/hid-system#43; §2, ADR-045).
- The HTTP verifier covers EHR only (§8).

**Known limits of the Outreach cookie-read fix (D-Eminence/hid-system#43):**
- As with #38, Identity cannot check the reported method and relies on the authenticated workload. After a reported read, `POST /identity/outreach/authorize` authorizes only a read permission, so a misreported method can authorize an Outreach read but not a write.
- Campaign writes stay closed: Identity refuses `outreach.campaign.write` with `400 VALIDATION_FAILED`, as on `main`, so no campaign can be created through the API and S4c's campaign create cannot pass (§7). Who may create campaigns, change their status and add members is an open product decision (§2, ADR-045). Health-id's Outreach page can list campaigns and members, but its campaign create, status and member actions get `400`; Health-id should hide or handle them.
- Campaign-linked registration works as the runtime role since D-Eminence/hid-system#34, but only for a campaign that already exists, and none can be created through the API while campaign writes stay refused.
- The evidence is local and synthetic: the HTTP verifier and the unit specs (§8). No browser run covers Outreach cookie reads.

**Known limits of the released Lab results change:**
- Any member holding `lab.result.released.read` with a `read_records` consent grant for the patient at the facility reads an order's released results, not only the clinician who placed the order; this is the same patient-level authorization as every other clinical read.
- A clinician is not told that a correction is pending release, and is not notified of a release: nothing in the EHR consumes `LabResultReleased`, so the result appears only when the order route is read.
- Released revisions keep their stored version numbers, so a gap (versions 1 and 3) shows that an intermediate revision was never released; its value and reason are not returned.
- Break-glass reads are allowed, as for every Lab read, until the break-glass scope of Lab and Pharmacy reads is decided.

**Known limits of the record summaries fix (D-Eminence/hid-system#52):**
- A vital's recorded measurements, which the emergency summary returns, are not immutable in the database. No EHR API route updates them (a correction moves only `current_correction_no`), but `hid_ehr_runtime` holds `UPDATE` on `ehr.vitals`, `vitals_update` (0005) allows it under `write_records`, and `ehr.capture_record_version` (0004) guards only ownership and attribution and requires a change reason. An update by another code path, a script or an operator is versioned in `ehr.record_versions`, and the emergency summary and the vitals routes then return the updated values. A guard refusing changes to the eight measurement columns is a follow-up migration (`DATABASE.md`, "Record summary reads"). Closed by 0084 (D-Eminence/hid-system#64): `ehr.keep_recorded_vital()` refuses any update of a vital other than of its current correction number (§2).
- Documents stay readable under break-glass through `GET /ehr/documents` and its download for a reader holding `ehr.document.read` (§2); that list shows documents of every status, including those not yet scanned and those the scanner rejected, each labelled with it. This change does not alter those routes.
- `scripts/run-container-database-acceptance.sh` now runs `emergency.integration.sql`. Five other suites in `services/ehr-api/database/tests` still run only in the rehearsal: `cutover-staging-integrity`, `demo-requests`, `otp-recovery`, `patient-enrollment` and `pricing` (`runtime-roles.integration.sql` runs in both, through the role bootstrap).

**Known limits of the EHR to Lab handoff (D-Eminence/hid-system#56):**
- Lab holds one work item per EHR request, at the version it accepted. Cancelling, holding, completing or marking entered in error a request that Lab accepted, or changing its priority or clinical information, is not sent to Lab: Lab can still accession, execute and release it, and its work list does not show the request's status. The EHR update reports `already_accepted` for an edit of an active request and nothing for the other changes. What Lab must do at each stage (not accessioned, specimen collected or received, execution started, result entered, verified or released) is an open clinical workflow decision; until it is made, this is the behaviour.
- Health-id has no control for `POST .../lab-work-item`, offers no activation or resume of a lab request (only cancelling a draft or marking it entered in error) and does not read `labHandoff`, so today the route and the update handoff are reachable only through the API.
- The route requires an `Idempotency-Key`, as clinical commands do, but Lab keys the acceptance by request and version whatever the key, so a resend under a new key returns the same work item.
- The EHR's `pharmacy-work-item` route still returns Pharmacy's answer as it is, including Pharmacy's `401` when it cannot verify the EHR's workload identity. Health-id's "Send to Pharmacy" action takes that `401` for an expired session: it refreshes, repeats the request and, on the second `401`, signs the clinician out. Answering it as the Lab route does (`502`) is a follow-up.

**Known limits of the purpose-of-use and OCR workload-refusal fix (D-Eminence/hid-system#60):**
- Identity's workload refusals are still `401 AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` and `403 ACCESS_DENIED`: OCR passes them through, a publication stores them as terminal, and the browser signs in again for them. A follow-up needs distinct Identity workload codes first (§2).
- Health-id cannot retry any publication: it sends a new `Idempotency-Key` with each publish and hides Publish once a publication exists (§2). A retryable failure is reachable only by the original requester resending the original key and body in the same facility, today through the in-repo EHR review workspace while it stays open; another member of the facility gets `409 OCR_ALREADY_PUBLISHED`, and no one else can resume it. Until Health-id keeps the key, a failed publication in the provider portal has no retry, whatever its code.
- A lapsed grant or a refused permission during the attempt stays terminal (open decision D16, §2).
- Publications that failed terminally under a workload code before the upgrade stay terminal, with no operator path to reopen them.
- The request audit row of a refused read still records the `X-Purpose-Of-Use` value as sent (each service's `audit.interceptor.ts` reads the header, or a `purpose` query parameter, rather than the validated purpose); a follow-up.
- The OCR verifier covers a Lab workload refusal over HTTP; EHR and Pharmacy are covered by the unit specs. The `400` in Identity, EHR, Lab and Pharmacy is covered by unit specs only.

**Known limits of the EHR clinical safety change (D-Eminence/hid-system#64):**
- Existing records are not changed by 0084: notes, diagnoses and open orders of encounters cancelled or retracted before it keep their status until reviewed (P12).
- (Since 0090 the cascade itself needs `ehr.lab-request.write`, so no one can cancel or retract an encounter that holds a lab request; see the 0090 limits.) Under the launch module set no role holds `ehr.lab-request.write`, so an open lab request is closed only by its encounter's cancellation or retraction cascade. One on a completed encounter stays open unless the whole encounter is retracted, and one of an encounter that is already cancelled or entered in error needs a reviewed data change (P12). Lab is off, so nothing acts on it.
- (Superseded by 0090: the cascade now covers final vitals, completed orders and linked documents.) The cascade does not change vitals or documents of a retracted encounter. The emergency summary still returns its vitals, as first recorded, and documents linked to it stay listed; the patient summary does not release them, because it releases only items of completed encounters, and the emergency summary keeps notes of cancelled encounters (D9).
- Pharmacy and Lab are not told of a cascaded change, as they are not told of any change after acceptance (§9, D-Eminence/hid-system#56 and #48). Under the launch module set no shipped role can hand an EHR order to either, so no work item is created for an order after 0084.
- The cascade semantics (which statuses change, that cancelling an encounter cancels its open orders, that late notes, vitals, diagnoses and documents on a completed encounter and resuming a held order stay allowed) are the owner defaults; the clinical owner should confirm them.
- The encounter audit event lists every changed id in `details.cascade`; an encounter with many records gives a large row. Nothing bounds it.
- Changes to existing records are not checked against the encounter's status: on a cancelled encounter a note can still be amended, a diagnosis updated and a vital corrected, and on an entered-in-error one a vital can still be corrected (its notes and diagnoses are entered in error and refuse changes). The register item named creates and activations only.

**Known limits of the EHR retraction integrity change (D-Eminence/hid-system#74, 0090):**
- No role holds `ehr.lab-request.write` (0084), so an encounter that still holds an open or completed lab request cannot be cancelled or retracted by anyone until Lab ordering is enabled again by its own migration. No lab request can be created at launch, and the release checklist retracts open ones before 0084 (P12); one left on a staging or migrated database needs a reviewed data change.
- The permission rule follows the record kinds, not authorship: a nurse holding `ehr.note.write` and `ehr.vital.write` may retract a doctor's signed note and vitals through the encounter, as she may on the note and vital routes. Who may retract whose documentation is the clinical owner's decision (ADR-056).
- Notes, diagnoses and vitals of a cancelled encounter can still be amended, updated or corrected (0084 limit above); only the encounter's own header and its closed orders are final.
- A retracted vital keeps its values and its corrections made before the retraction; staff lists show it with `status`, and Health-id `82b474e` shows no marker yet (§3).
- A document entered in error takes no change at all, so a legal-hold flag or any other later column change on one needs its own reviewed change; holds are recorded in `platform.legal_holds` (0064), not on the document row. The emergency summary leaves it out; diagnoses and orders entered in error stay listed there with their status, which Health-id labels as recorded in error.
- The refusal audit row is written outside the refused transaction, after the rollback. A refusal whose audit write fails answers `503` instead of its own status, as every audited refusal does.
- Allergies are not recorded (ALLERGY-1): no structured allergy, no allergy check at prescribing.
- Health-id `main` does not offer `entered_in_error` for a completed encounter, still says that an encounter change does not change its orders, and shows `423 BREAK_GLASS_DISABLED` as a generic error with its detail (§3; Health-id follow-ups).
- The disabled modules' ECS services and edge routes stay as deployed until the infrastructure owner changes them; their permissions are gone, so no write reaches them through the shipped roles.

## 10. Operator actions, not performed here

These are documented for the operators and were not done in this stage:
- deploying anything;
- running migrations or the role bootstrap against staging or production;
- adding `mfaSecretKeyB64` to each environment's Identity sensitive secret (P3), and deploying the regional stack that references it;
- changing AWS, DNS, Cloudflare, Supabase, IAM or Softbridge;
- creating the staging environment needed for §7;
- the read-only OCR scan evidence check P9 on staging and production;
- the read-only purpose-of-use check P10 on staging and production, before and after 0082, and the decisions on `emergency` and `healthcare-operations` (§2);
- on staging and then production RDS (D-Eminence/hid-system#63): the I3 probe as the master, the `SET ON PARAMETER plpgsql.variable_conflict` grant to the migration administrator for the migration run and its revoke after 0083, or, if RDS refuses the grant, a reviewed ledger exception for 0015 (`MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6.1); the role bootstrap and `db:verify-roles` as that administrator; and the ten runtime LOGINs and their secrets, created and granted their aggregate roles by that administrator (the master may not grant them) and verified with `db:verify-logins` (§5 step 31, S2);
- the read-only check P11 after 0091 and its bootstrap (and, only for a launch control an administrator had changed and left on, setting it false through `POST /admin/controls` before ingress), the read-only check P12 after 0084 (and before it), the clinical owner's review of what P12 lists, and the clinical owner's confirmation of the encounter cascade semantics (D-Eminence/hid-system#64);
- scaling the disabled Lab, Pharmacy, OCR and Outreach services to zero and removing their edge routes, if the infrastructure owner decides so (launch module set); since D-Eminence/hid-system#66 the regional stack pins their counts at 0 and the gateway refuses their routes, so only a deployment of that stack remains;
- for production launch (ADR-053, `AWS_DEPLOYMENT_RUNBOOK.md` §13): the operations owner naming the on-call recipients; creating the customer-managed key, the topic `hid-production-operational-alerts` and its confirmed subscriptions (P16); SES production access and DKIM (P17); the Termii account and approved sender ID (P18); writing the production Identity sensitive secret with five independent keys and an empty `googleOidcClientIds` and the notification secret with exactly the four `otp-email-sms` fields; running the production preflight (P15, P19); the reviewed I6 parameter file (P20), with the on-call recipients told that the running-task alarms notify until each service is scaled; pausing the Brevo fallback (P21); and, after deployment, synthetic signup OTPs over email and SMS and a recovery OTP whose `notification.otp.outcome` lines show `accepted`;
- a decision on the Novu account and region before `HID_NOTIFICATION_WORKER_ENABLED` may be enabled in production, and on Brevo as a production fallback processor; and, before `HID_NOVU_SUBSCRIBER_SYNC_ENABLED` is set in staging, P35 and then S4w (ADR-064), with the Novu subscriber deletion follow-up and its owner;
- the staging workload issuer proof (I5, `AWS_DEPLOYMENT_RUNBOOK.md` §7) and the production workload issuer plan of §12 (I6, P13), each with its own deployment authorization; ADR-047 is accepted, the plan is not approved;
- setting `PATIENT_ENROLLMENT_QOREID_DAILY_CAP` (and, if carrier-grade NAT needs it, the per-network limits) in the Identity task from the QoreID contract; the defaults are 200 a day in all, 5 an hour and 15 a day per network (§2). The cap bounds cost, not availability: at the defaults 14 networks can spend it and close enrollment starts that need a lookup until the next day bucket, so size it with that in mind;
- an alert on audit events `identity.public-patient-enrollment.nin-lookup-quota` with outcome `denied` and `details.exhaustedLimit` `platform_day` (the platform cap is spent), and a watch on `network_hour` and `network_day` denials; no such alert exists in the repository (§2);
- before `ASSISTED_REGISTRATION_ENABLED` is set anywhere (ADR-063): P29 to P33, the designation of each participating facility (P30), and a weekly review of in-person checks per staff member and per facility with the read-only query below, with a follow-up for any staff member near the daily budget, many `not_matched` or `code_not_found` checks, or checks outside the facility's hours; no such alert exists in the repository;
- before `NIN_HOLDER_SMS_ENABLED` is set anywhere (ADR-062): alerts on the holder SMS volume and the holder failure ratio, read from `identity.public_patient_holder_events` with the read-only query below, and on every `identity.public-patient-enrollment.holder-route-closed` audit event; no such alert exists in the repository. Suggested thresholds, to be set by the operations owner: sends in an hour above twice the busiest hour of the previous week, failures above half of sends in an hour with at least 20 sends, and any route-closed event with `window` `month`;
- the read-only count of patient accounts without a verified contact below, before 0086 and after the legacy import;
- before the staff MFA Identity deploy: telling staff that the deploy signs them out and that they will set up an authenticator app at their next sign-in, and counting (read-only, counts only) the staff accounts with an active membership and no email address, which cannot enrol until given one (`MIGRATION_RUNBOOK.md` §6);
- the Lab order reconciliation below;
- the Pharmacy work review below;
- building and staging the Health-id frontend for each launch host and deploying only the launch Workers, `hid-web` and `hid-admin` (and the production apex redirect), with their `ORIGIN_AUTH_TOKEN` and the Turnstile widget's site key (P14, S4l; ADR-052), checking each deployed host as in §6 step 11; the gateway image with the regional stack and Identity in the order of §6 step 11, choosing the staging modules for S4b, S4c, S4d, S4g and S4k with the synth input `HID_ENABLED_API_ROUTES`, and the owner's decision on `PATIENT_ENROLLMENT_START_RATE_MAX_REQUESTS` (default 5 per 15 minutes per client network).

**NIN holder SMS volume and failure ratio (0093).** Run as the migration administrator, every hour. No runtime or monitoring role can read the holder events; a monitoring role would need its own reviewed grant and policy first. The table forces row-level security and admits only the `system:auth` actor, set in this read-only transaction only. It counts digests, never a phone or NIN:

```sql
begin transaction isolation level repeatable read read only;
set local statement_timeout = '120s';
set local lock_timeout = '5s';
select set_config('app.actor_subject', 'system:auth', true);
select date_trunc('hour', occurred_at) as hour,
       count(*) filter (where kind = 'send') as sends,
       count(*) filter (where kind = 'failure') as failures,
       count(*) filter (where kind = 'proof') as proofs,
       count(distinct phone_digest) filter (where kind = 'send') as phones,
       round(count(*) filter (where kind = 'failure')::numeric
         / nullif(count(*) filter (where kind = 'send'), 0), 2) as failures_per_send
  from identity.public_patient_holder_events
 where occurred_at > clock_timestamp() - interval '48 hours'
 group by 1 order by 1 desc;
rollback;
```

**In-person checks per staff member and facility (0094).** Run as the migration administrator, every week and on any suspicion. No runtime or monitoring role can read the checks; a monitoring role would need its own reviewed grant and policy first. The table forces row-level security; the `system:auth` actor, set in this read-only transaction only, reads it. It returns ids and counts, never a name, document, NIN or contact. Resolve an account or facility id through the admin console:

```sql
begin transaction isolation level repeatable read read only;
set local statement_timeout = '120s';
set local lock_timeout = '5s';
select set_config('app.actor_subject', 'system:auth', true);
select verification.facility_id, verification.staff_account_id,
       count(*) as checks,
       count(*) filter (where verification.outcome = 'matched') as matched,
       count(*) filter (where verification.outcome = 'not_matched') as not_matched,
       count(*) filter (where verification.outcome = 'code_not_found') as code_not_found,
       count(*) filter (where verification.proof_outcome = 'verified') as proved,
       count(*) filter (where verification.proof_outcome = 'existing_identity') as existing_identity,
       min(verification.verified_at) as first_check,
       max(verification.verified_at) as last_check
  from identity.assisted_registration_verifications verification
 where verification.verified_at > clock_timestamp() - interval '7 days'
 group by verification.facility_id, verification.staff_account_id
 order by checks desc, verification.facility_id, verification.staff_account_id;
rollback;
```

**Patient accounts without a verified contact (0086).** After the Identity build of the recovery and patient search hardening, a patient whose account email is not verified (`email_verified_at` null) and who has no verified enrollment phone gets no recovery or activation code. Count them by source, as the migration administrator, with the read-only query below (it returns counts only, no identifier), and decide for each source how those patients are reached before launch; legacy accounts take `email_verified_at` from the source's confirmation time (`promote-legacy-identity.mjs`). It counts the phone as verified without checking that it decrypts with the current key, so the real number can be higher. Accounts made by facility-assisted enrollment (`source_system` `hid-governed-patient-enrollment`) are always in this count; that route is off at launch (D17). It was run only against an empty local database.

```sql
begin transaction isolation level repeatable read read only;
set local statement_timeout = '120s';
set local lock_timeout = '5s';
set local row_security = off;
select account.source_system, count(*) as patient_accounts_without_verified_contact
  from auth.accounts account
 where account.status in ('active', 'pending_reset')
   and account.email_verified_at is null
   and exists (select 1 from identity.patients patient
                where patient.account_id = account.id and patient.status = 'active')
   and not exists (select 1 from identity.public_patient_enrollments enrollment
                    where enrollment.account_id = account.id and enrollment.state = 'active'
                      and enrollment.contact_channel = 'phone'
                      and enrollment.contact_verified_at is not null)
 group by account.source_system
 order by account.source_system;
rollback;
```

**Pharmacy work no longer dispensable (0080).** After 0080, an accepted work item without a dispensing whose EHR prescription is missing, no longer `active`, at another version, or of another patient, facility or encounter answers `409 PHARMACY_PRESCRIPTION_NOT_DISPENSABLE`, and Health-id still offers its dispense form. List them with the read-only query below, as the migration administrator, and tell the pharmacies concerned; a changed prescription needs a new EHR prescription and handoff (§2). The query was written for this change and has not been run against a database.

```sql
begin transaction isolation level repeatable read read only;
set local statement_timeout = '120s';
set local lock_timeout = '5s';
set local row_security = off;
select item.id as work_item_id, item.facility_id, item.patient_id, item.source_ehr_prescription_id,
       item.source_ehr_prescription_version, prescription.status, prescription.row_version, item.accepted_at
  from pharmacy.work_items item
  left join ehr.prescriptions prescription on prescription.id = item.source_ehr_prescription_id
 where not exists (select 1 from pharmacy.dispensings dispensing where dispensing.work_item_id = item.id)
   and (prescription.id is null
        or prescription.status is distinct from 'active'
        or prescription.row_version is distinct from item.source_ehr_prescription_version
        or prescription.patient_id is distinct from item.patient_id
        or prescription.facility_id is distinct from item.facility_id
        or prescription.encounter_id is distinct from item.source_encounter_id)
 order by item.accepted_at, item.id
 limit 500;
rollback;
```

**Lab order reconciliation (Stage 9).** The EHR commits a lab request, then calls Lab acceptance. Until Stage 9, 0078 and the EHR to Lab order-time fix (§2), every acceptance (almost every, after Stage 9 alone) failed, so on any environment where the EHR API created active lab requests, those requests have no Lab work item. A retry of the same EHR request with the same Idempotency-Key re-sends the acceptance while the request is active, at any time (the replay does not check the key's `expires_at`). Before D-Eminence/hid-system#56 nothing else did; with it, `POST .../lab-requests/:requestId/lab-work-item` sends an active request at its current version, and an update that leaves a request active (activating a draft, resuming an on-hold request) sends it (§2). After the release, list them with the read-only check below, as the migration administrator, and decide with the clinical owners whether to send each one (that command, through the API until Health-id offers it, a new EHR order, or a reviewed backfill under change control) or record why not. The check also lists completed requests without a work item: the EHR completed them although Lab never received them, so review each clinically (where was the test done, and is its result recorded?). Verified locally on synthetic data: it lists an active, an on-hold and a completed request without a work item, and not an active one with a work item or a draft or cancelled one. Pharmacy acceptance is an explicit pharmacist command that can simply be repeated.

```sql
begin transaction isolation level repeatable read read only;
set local statement_timeout = '120s';
set local lock_timeout = '5s';
set local row_security = off;
select request.id as lab_request_id, request.status, request.facility_id, request.patient_id, request.row_version,
       request.created_at
  from ehr.lab_requests request
 where request.status in ('active', 'on_hold', 'completed')
   and not exists (select 1 from lab.work_items item where item.source_ehr_order_id = request.id)
 order by request.created_at, request.id
 limit 500;
rollback;
```
